The LF line endings (#147) were checked with an autocrlf clone on a Mac; the `exact-js` build script was not run against a CRLF tree. Android, on no device: the purchases listener's declaration order (#157; Kotlin read, not compiled), `flock` for a `secret.keep` lease (#158; compiled for `aarch64-linux-android`), the `default` store for a drive that names none (#153; Windows too).
- **Landed on 2026-11-10 without a device or the XCTests** (the Mac they were made on has no Metal toolchain, no Android device and an emulator that does not boot). Android: the Maps module without a key (#64), a placed canvas child's pixels (#50), the held-frame scratch texture (#47). iOS, seen on a simulator only: `system-bars-hidden` (#52; the home indicator's fade cannot be seen in a simulator capture), `font-feature-settings` (#41; Windows compiled only), the aria-hidden canvas child (#69, read through lldb because XCUITest would not start). XCTests written and never compiled: `StatusBarIOSTests` (two new tests), `FrameHealthMetricsTests` (on `wave4/driver`).
- **Android frame timing and canvas children, on a phone** (2026-11-12, #302, #53, #50). Built and run on the emulator, never on a phone: `perf frames` from Choreographer turns and the window's FrameMetrics (#302; the emulator's vsync is not a panel's), a GPU canvas's frame-rate cap asked of the platform and its `period_ms` (#53; whether a 60 fps cap on a 120 Hz panel presents evenly wants the reporter's Seeker and SurfaceFlinger's present times), `Each` children following a surface that moves them and `Composite` children uploaded as a texture (#50; the emulator's Vulkan driver crashes on GPU canvases, so unit tests only).
- **A placed canvas child, with VoiceOver on a phone** (2026-11-11, #69). On UIKit a child the surface places hides its own drawing behind an empty mask on its layer and keeps alpha 1 (`NodeLayer.drawnElsewhere`), so VoiceOver descends into it; the controls and fields Exact makes report their frames through the placement, a stepper's, segmented control's and compact date picker's parts by stand-ins (`PlacedAccessibilityIOS.swift`), and a context menu lifts from where the surface draws the box (2026-11-12). All of it was read on a simulator through UIKit's VoiceOver traversal, not heard: listen to a placed row's text and a placed switch with VoiceOver on a phone. Still open: focus does not reach into a placed child (no UIKit focus item; on tvOS its `focusItems(in:)` offers none) until UIKit's focus geometry is mapped through the placement (a focus item container whose `coordinateSpace` converts through it is the likely way). A filtered placed child's capture is its filter's picture: this simulator's filter chain draws no pixels in an XCTest, so that is read on a device or the Mac only. With the sky canvas on, VoiceOver's traversal of Caltrain's window returned no elements (seen once on 2026-11-10, not pursued): the sky's overlay is at alpha 0.
- **A glass joining or leaving a group on the Mac, and a named glass morph** (2026-11-12, #121). On iOS a glass a person's input adds to a `glassGroup` grows out of the nearest glass within the spacing and one it removes is drawn back into it (`GlassMorphIOS.swift`, LLP 1053.000.000.001 r2). The Mac appears and vanishes in place: AppKit jumps an animated glass frame, so its join and leave would step the frame on the view's display link. A native glass button's leave is a plain glass capsule without its title. A named `glassId` morph between two glass (r1) is not built; each waits for an app that asks.
- **Known edges of 2026-11-10's fixes.** #56: a surface name registered by both the `gpu` crate and a declared module builds and draws the crate's; a declared GPU module's surfaces do not run on the web's JS target (refused by name). #62: with two overlapping animations the suggested `clock +N` can be the shorter one's, and the wasm target always gives the scroll sentence. #41: a bound value with more than sixteen features draws on the web's JS target and is refused natively; `font-variation-settings`, `font-stretch` and `font-optical-sizing` are not in a component's `text` group. The web writes a keyframes rule's name unquoted. #63's review found the agent's `back` cannot press the Back that UIKit's More shows. #123: on iOS a rolling paragraph rolls digit by digit (SwiftUI's numeric text) only when a raster could hold it, it is one whole line of text with no image and no white space it keeps, and what it paints lies within its box, and it has no shadow, no stroke and nothing hidden; any other rolls as a whole picture where it rasters, and otherwise not at all. Unchecked on the rolling line: a CSS `filter` on the text (its picture is the subtree drawn once, so the roll's motion may not show through it).
- **The `tests` workflow is red on Linux for reasons older than it** (2026-11-10). Until that day the Linux job stopped at clippy and never ran a test. It now runs them: about twenty Cargo tests fail on Linux (`notify::`, `local_network::`, `contacts_calendar::`, `inherited::a_percentage_min_height_on_a_list_row_has_no_basis`, `incremental::every_app_plan_updates_incrementally_exactly_as_it_does_in_full`, the executor's async suite, `tamber_rows_check`'s budget), and 25 script tests, most because the runner has no `nightly-2026-08-21` with `rust-src` ("the web artifact builds with nightly-2026-08-21 and its std sources"). macOS passes in about 95 minutes. `four_times_the_uses_compile_in_about_four_times_as_long` measured 4.7 once on a macOS runner against its 4.6 limit and 4.1 to 4.3 on an 18-core Mac before and after the day's commits. Three `exact-web-js` colour tests (`a_bound_filter_goes_through_the_colour_map` and two beside it) fail now and then in a full run and pass alone. Neither workflow compiles the Swift host. `games/garden/Cargo.lock` is stale (`bun game/app/shells.mjs game/games/garden --lock`).
- **A signed build chooses an identity and profile that match** (2026-11-10, LLP 1156.004 D1, deferred). `identity()` takes a team's first Apple Development identity and `profile()` development profiles only, so a profile without that identity's certificate signs and is refused at install or upload. Choose a matching pair for every embedded target before compiling; it needs a Mac with several identities and profiles to verify.
- **`border-width` with no `border-style` draws nothing, silently** (2026-11-10, LLP 1156.002 D6, deferred). A warning judged from the node's attributes alone would miss classes, shorthands and state styles; judge it from the effective style.
- **Caltrain's macOS footprint grows with no input** (2026-11-11, LLP 1158.003 r2, open question 1). One production launch (`--release`, a 420×860 window, no `EXACT_SMOKE`, M5 MacBook Air) read 212 MiB at 2 s and 331 MiB at 60 s by `footprint`: graphics 134 → 199 MiB, everything else 78 → 132. Hello and the AppKit floor stay flat from 2 s. One launch only: repeat it, then find what grows (its canvases, image or glyph caches, the journal ring), and say whether it levels off.
- **Compile an app's module incrementally** (2026-11-11, LLP 1156.003 D1). `bench/apple-module-compile.md` measured `swiftc -incremental -enable-batch-mode -output-file-map` on forty files sharing a 2 MB binding: a one-line edit takes 0.26 s against 31 s for `moduleArgs`' whole-module compile. Two conditions come with it. `snapshotSources` (`host/apple/arms.mjs`) must leave unchanged files untouched, since `copyFileSync` refreshes every modification time and all forty files compile again (14 s). A removed source's object, `.swiftdeps` and `.d` must be deleted, since Swift leaves them and a link of the folder's objects still exports the removed code.

- **Android canvas touch and rest on a device** (2026-11-10, #12, #13). Multi-touch on a GPU canvas and the at-rest render skip compile, build Weatherlight's APK and pass presenter tests, but ran on no device or emulator. Two fingers on a canvas, a scroll takeover, a replaced surface, and presents at rest want a phone. Hiding a canvas with `display: none` mid-touch does not cancel its remaining fingers.
- **A 30-minute soak of an alternating GPU canvas on a Vulkan Android phone** (2026-11-10, #47). A canvas whose surface answers `frame_ready() == false` on every other render now reuses one scratch texture, copy pipeline and bind group (`gpu/src/frame.rs`, `Scratch`), which the gpu crate's tests check on Metal only. Run the issue's `held` surface (or any that holds every other frame) as a full-window canvas in a release build on a Mali phone at 120 Hz, animating, for 30 minutes in one process: unpatched it aborted in wgpu-hal's descriptor allocator within 3 to 4 minutes. `adb logcat` should show no abort and `dumpsys meminfo` a flat graphics line. wgpu-hal still answers `ERROR_OUT_OF_POOL_MEMORY` and `ERROR_FRAGMENTED_POOL` with `unreachable!()` (`vendor/wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/descriptor.rs`); returning an error there would be a fifteenth local patch, and wgpu's default error handler would panic on it all the same.
- **`Body.ccd` is encoded by hand to keep old hashes** (2026-11-10, #27). It is written only when true, so reading a serialized body without it into one with CCD leaves CCD on. The derive would fix that and changes `pile-600`, `minimal-120`, forest's tick-4500 and rivals' tick-1080 pins and their save digests; `bun game/prove.mjs <game> --repin` is the way, and it refuses today because forest's and rivals' captured `Cargo.lock` lag exact2.
- **iOS: `testAHeadingsAuthoredTypographyIsTheBarsTitleFont` fails** (2026-11-11, seen landing the header search's Enter test). On an iPhone 18 Pro simulator `NavigationBarIOSTests` passes 46 and fails this one: for native-fixture's `title-font` heading, which authors no typography, `drawnLarge()` (`NavigationTitleInkIOSTests.swift:45`) finds no large title drawn.
- **An arrangement's `.intrinsic` pane on iOS 27.1** (2026-11-12). A pane holder now measures its authored content and margins for UIKit's `.intrinsic` dimension (`ArrangementSizingIOSTests`), checked on iOS 27.0, which has no native arrangement. Run those tests on an iOS 27.1 simulator to see UIKit's arrangement allocate the measured size. On an open iPhone Duo (iOS 27.1) `testIntrinsicAllocationFollowsContentAndOtherDimensionsStillWork` fails at "the native dimension sets the allocation" (2026-11-12, b5ad88a1e3). `PaneView` answers UIKit two ways: `intrinsicContentSize` measures the pane in the arranged element's whole room, `sizeThatFits` and `systemLayoutSizeFitting` in UIKit's proposal. Find which one `.intrinsic` reads; a measure aligned with CSS gives a percentage size on that axis no basis (CSS Sizing 3 §5.2.1), so `height="100%"` would count as automatic there.
- **Keyboard Check route on a Duo panel switch** (2026-11-04): folding from 130° to closed while editing returned the pushed editor to the Keyboard root. Inspect native navigation events and the app recorder; gutter checks select the pose before opening the editor.
- **Native Mac element arrangements** (2026-11-04): the AppKit prototype reset a scroller from 120 points to zero during adoption. Keep the CSS layout until native containment retains authored scroll offsets.
- **Duo native panes under the bars** (2026-11-05): qualify visible bounds through bar minimization and every fold pose before moving the Split and Overlay description into Workspace.
- **Arranged panes get no side clearance** (2026-11-08). The open Duo's 84-point side strip is the secondary pane's own right safe area; a UIKit app with the same arrangement ends its rows at it, but Exact lays the pane's content across it (pane and content both 475 points wide). LLP 1123 D3's local clearance for pane owners, or D9's `var(--exact-safe-area-inset-*)`, would keep content clear of it.
- **An iOS bar item's share test fails on main** (2026-11-07). `NavigationBarIOSTests.testAShareFromABarItemPresentsFromTheItem` misses its presentation assertion on iOS 27.1 (24A94232). It fails with this branch's navigation code and after swapping `NavigationBarIOS.swift` and `HeaderShape` back to upstream main in the same checkout. Check the native share presentation and test isolation.

- **On the JS target, a page whose answers read the store waits for the reader on a device with its own store** (2026-11-09, found by reading; not yet reproduced). Both runtimes ask a page's answer that read the store (`readers`, LLP 1048.000 D6) again at boot when the device's store holds the app's own entries, which the render never had (`Runner::boot_checkpoint`; `mount` and `res` in `host/web-js/rt.js`). LLP 1048.007 D1 does not count such an answer, so a page whose render left the runtime nothing else to do waits for the reader. The wasm host's document glue starts the runtime at once when `localStorage` holds an `exact.secret.` key (`host/web/document-glue.js`); the JS target's capture script (`host/web-js/capture.js`) does not, so its page shows the render's answer until the reader acts. Wake the JS runtime as the wasm host does, or do not let a page whose checkpoint lists `readers` wait.
- **A Bun render lists every answered element of a list in `open` when one element's answer watches a document or is refused storage** (2026-11-11, found by reading; not yet reproduced). `answers` (`host/web-js/checkpoint.js`) lists an answer in `open` when its resource keeps a ticket, or when the render saw a watch or a refused storage call under the resource's name (`watchedInRender` and `refusedInRender` in `host/web-js/ts-data.js`, LLP 1048.000 D9). A list's elements share the list's name, so the reader's page asks every answered element of that list again, where a stream or a pending request asks only its own element. Keep a watch and a refusal on the call of the answer a resource holds, and list only that answer.
- **A runner element under a closed gate at boot shows its placeholder, not its page's answer** (2026-11-11, found by reading; not yet reproduced). A boot from a rendered page gives an element the page's answer at its arguments once the settlement has evaluated them (`seed_member` in `runner/src/runner/each.rs`). The settlement reads a gate (`when`, LLP 1161.002) before the arguments, and a closed gate puts a new element to sleep on its placeholder without evaluating them. A declared resource under a closed gate sleeps on its page's answer, and so does a JS element, whose answer `keptMember` (`host/web-js/each.js`) finds when the element is made, before its gate is read. They differ only when the gate was open at the render and is closed at boot, as when it reads the device's store. While a list's page answers are held, evaluate a new element's arguments under a closed gate, asking nothing, and let it sleep on its page's answer.
- **A declared resource is asked at boot at arguments a stand-in gave** (2026-11-12, found by reading; not yet reproduced). A declared source never waits for the data module (LLP 1162.001 D4), so the boot's settlement asks a declared resource with whatever arguments the first frame has (`runner/src/runner/settlement.rs`, by `source_ready` in `fetches.rs`). When those arguments read a module's store reader that shows a stand-in, its placeholder or, at a launch whose store holds the app's own entries, the bake's empty-store answer (`secrets_unseen` judges the same), the request is for arguments that are not this launch's: its reply is waste once `data_ready` gives the device's arguments, and if it lands first the screen shows an answer for the stand-in's arguments meanwhile. On a fresh install the bake's answer is the launch's own, and the ask is right. On the web JS target a TypeScript module loads with the page, so a fetch goes out with the device's arguments. Hold an ask whose arguments read a stand-in until they are the launch's own, and ask it then.
- **On Apple, a peer's store write before the data module loads reaches a declared store reader only at activation** (2026-11-13, found by reading; not yet reproduced). While the module is not activated, `refresh_store_after` (`host/apple/src/activation.rs`) defers a peer's store write, a widget's or an extension's, and applies it at `data_ready` with `adopt_store` and `data_ready_after_store`. A declared source never waits for the module (LLP 1162.001 D4), so a declared resource that reads a secret could be asked again as soon as the write lands; it keeps the launch's answer until activation instead. A browser's `storage` event reaches every reader at once. Apply the write before activation, asking the readers whose source can answer, and leave the module's readers for `data_ready`.
- **A kept module source's seed is checked and held as a not-ready source's only while the data module is not ready** (2026-11-13, found by reading; not yet reproduced). A kept module source (LLP 1133.015 D4) shows its kept answer and is asked at boot (`seed_kept_modules`, `ask_at_launch`). When its source is ready, the settlement consumes the seed's mark at once (`runner/src/runner/settlement.rs`, "Consume the mark on mismatch or activation"), so the seed is neither compared with the launch's arguments, where LLP 1133.015 D4 shows it only when they match, nor kept provisional: if the boot ask fails, the kept value stands as the failed resource's value and `carry()` (`carry.rs`) takes it without its failure, so a reload or an update applied at once shows the last launch's value as an answer, where LLP 1027 D4 says a seed is neither carried, checkpointed nor newly persisted. The kept sources today take no arguments (`packages/purchases/exact.json`), so the comparison waits for one that does; the carry is reachable now. Check a kept seed's arguments and identity whatever its source's readiness, and keep it marked, out of `carry()` and `document_checkpoint()`, until a fresh answer replaces it.
- **A closed gate drops a request an earlier pass of the same settlement built, without discarding it** (2026-11-13, found by reading; not yet reproduced). The settlement's closed-gate branch (`runner/src/runner/settlement.rs`, LLP 1161.002) sets the resource's effect to answered over a `Later` effect an earlier pass made, where the ask path discards such requests first (`discard_request`): a TypeScript continuation it parked is not discarded, and a plain request's source does not hear that it was forgotten. It needs a gate that closes between passes after the resource asked, as when a later resource's store write makes the settlement pass again and a reader the gate reads answers differently. Discard an earlier pass's requests in the closed-gate branch, as the ask path does.
- **The journal calls a page's answer asked again at `data_ready` the last session's value** (2026-11-11, found by reading). A boot marks stale a page's answer that it asks again before its source is ready: an open stream's, or a store reader's on a device with its own store (`runner/src/runner.rs`, `seed_member`). When the fresh answer differs, `revalidated` (`runner/src/runner/settlement.rs`) takes any stale value that is neither build-time nor a placeholder for a kept answer and writes "answered differently from its kept answer: the first frame showed the last session's value". The value was the page's, so the line misleads a reader of the journal, though nothing shown changes. Mark a seeded state with where it came from until its answer lands (`kept_seed` is consumed at activation), and write the page's own line, or none.
- **A page's surrogate keys grow with its answers** (2026-11-09). The render host names each answer's source and arguments in `Surrogate-Key` and `Cache-Tag` (`keys` in `host/render/src/serve.rs`, LLP 1048.000 D11), and a list's page has an answer for each element, so the headers grow with the list, and `keys` checks each new key against every one before it, so its time grows with the square of the keys. Nothing bounds them, and a key left out would keep a stale page (D11). Check the limits of the CDNs these headers are for before a page with a long list is cached, and decide what a page past them carries.
- **A `render=build` page keeps its render's draw of randomness** (2026-11-13, found in review). Both build writers write a page whatever its render drew: `render.mjs --build` ignores the page's `drew`, and the render host's `--build` lines (`host/render/src/lib.rs`) carry no `drew` for `host/web-js/build.mjs` to read. Every reader of the static file then starts from one draw, where LLP 1069.005 D2 has a bake compile no answer that drew and the device ask it after first pixel. Refuse the route at build (decided 2026-11-13): the build fails, names the route and tells the author to make it `render=request` or `render=client`. `Rendered::drew` and the Bun page's `drew` already say which renders drew.
- **A let-go answer's draws of randomness can go uncounted** (2026-11-13, found in review; not yet reproduced). Two ways. (1) `__exact_forget` (`js/src/prelude.js`) sets a call's `letGo`, which refuses its later fetches, yields and timers, but leaves its status `pending`, and `answerOnly` checks only the status, so a `finally` or a later `then` of a let-go answer may draw; the native host runs those continuations in `forget_calls` (`js/src/turns.rs`) with no store installed, and door operation 8 (`js/src/door.rs`) then drops the draw's mark. (2) A worker's turn that drew while its answer was live, and is let go before its reply lands, loses the envelope that carries the draw: `runner/src/runner/reply.rs` drops a reply whose ticket is no longer pending before reading it. Either way a module that keeps the value can give it to a later answer on a page whose render reports no draw, which a server then shares, and a warm realm keeps it. Refusing a draw in a let-go call on every host (`answerOnly` in `js/src/prelude.js` and `host/web-js/ts-fetch.js`, as LLP 1069.005 D3 gives entropy to a pending call) closes (1); (2) needs a dropped reply's draws counted, or the realm that drew not kept warm.
- **A lifted child state reads resources before the JS boot** (2026-11-10, found by reading; not yet reproduced). The initializer of a child's state used outside every region (`late`) runs in the app's body before `mount` (`host/web-js/src/emit.rs`), outside every commit, and it can read a parent's resource through a prop. An ask it causes while the source can answer never lands: `send` queues it on `Out`, which the boot commit replaces as it starts, and a resource whose ticket stands at its arguments asks nothing more, so it stays pending (`host/web-js/rt.js`). A list's elements that it makes also take no page answer, since `keptMember` (`host/web-js/each.js`) gives one only while the runtime boots. The runner starts such a slot after its boot settlement (`init_late_slots`). Start late slots in the boot commit, after its settlement, as the runner does.

- **Chrome conformance fails on seven apps on main** (2026-11-12, e494c5d2). `host/web-js/conform.mjs --build --strict` in Chrome differs on weatherlight (11 rows), fieldnotes (119), messages (40, the same rows at shifted indices), paging (56), pagetail (58), photo-editor (2) and markdown (1); bootpress differs intermittently. The Linux leg fails `slots.choice` and the radio's focus after `key radio-a ArrowRight`, and Firefox lays text out 1920 px tall against Chrome's 1536. Sort which are the JS target's defects and which the oracle's.

- **The Contract compiler's growth test crosses its CPU ratio bound on main** (2026-11-04). `contract/cli/tests/growth.rs`'s `four_times_the_uses_compile_in_about_four_times_as_long` measured 4.8× and 5.0× for 4× the input; a control with main's web files measured 4.64×, above its 4.6× bound. Other controls pass with the same compiled binary. Check the measurement and whether repeated work has returned before changing the bound. On 2026-11-12 it passed three runs in debug and failed every `--profile host-dev` run at 5.4–5.6×, while allocations and allocated bytes grew about 4× and clock inference took three rounds at both sizes. Neither a faster clock map, another sampling order nor allocator and cache controls brought it under 4.6, and profiling found no quadratic step; a control that scans every handler again measured 5.1× in debug and 6.6–6.9× in host-dev.
- **iOS: a prompt's hardware keys with a real keyboard** (2026-11-03, LLP 1115 D6's amendment of that date). The alert's field hears a hardware key through a gesture recognizer (`PromptKeys`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PromptFieldIOS.swift`); its tests hand it synthetic presses and the agent's keys go round it. Type into a prompt with a Mac keyboard in Simulator.app or an iPad's: a `key` handler should hear each key once and a prevented one type nothing. If UIKit gives keyboard presses to no recognizer, Return and Backspace are still the delegate's, and the LLP's known differences grow by the other keys.
- **`-exact-continuous` past `apple_radius_limit` is not UIKit's** (2026-11-02, found reviewing the frame-health card's corners). Where a box has no room for the curve's 1.53 radii, the kernel's outline (`kernel/src/corner.rs`) reduces the radius, while UIKit's `UIBezierPath(roundedRect:cornerRadius:)` bends the curve toward a circular arc and SwiftUI's continuous `RoundedRectangle` differs from both: a 200×60 box at a 30-point radius lies 3.9 points from UIKit's, a 60×32 box at 12 points 2.1, and a 100×100 box at 40 points 7.2 (UIKit through Mac Catalyst, macOS 27.2). A layer with one radius takes Core Animation's `.continuous` there; the kernel's outline is what Linux draws, and what Apple draws where a layer cannot say the shape.
- **`image-rendering` on Android, on a device** (2026-11-02, Pokédex benchmark round 17). Every host samples an image by its inherited `image-rendering`; Android's canvas compiles and its unit tests pass (`./gradlew :canvas:testDebugUnitTest`). Left: on a device, a `pixelated` sprite enlarged by a scale animation (its layer re-records at the animated scale) and one held as a hardware bitmap (drawn through a RenderNode, not copied). A pixelated image in a layer nested inside another scaling layer does not take the outer layer's scale.
- **`formatLocale` carries a forced hour cycle on Apple and Android only** (2026-11-02, LLP 1027.000.000; since 2026-11-12 the formatters of an app with format data read it too, LLP 1054.000.003 D6). Windows tells no place at all (the runner's `en-US` and `UTC`): `GetUserDefaultLocaleName` is the format locale, and `GetLocaleInfoEx(LOCALE_NAME_USER_DEFAULT, LOCALE_SSHORTTIME)` against the same with `LOCALE_NOUSEROVERRIDE` shows a time format the person changed (`H` for 24-hour, `h` for 12).
- **A restart keeps per-node state that a new node with the same id then picks up** (2026-10-31, found with #1228). Booting a booted session again (`ExactSession.boot` calls `resetHeld`: the dev menu's Reload, a development plan or connection reload, an update) keeps the `Presenter` and its hosts and clears them through `Presenter.reset` (`PresenterIOS.swift`, `PresenterMac.swift`), a list kept by hand that differs from what a node's destroy clears (`release(id)`, `stickies.forget`). The restarted runner numbers its nodes from 1 again, so a host's state keyed by node id outlives its node and is read for the new node with that id. #1228 fixed one case: `SegmentHost` remembered the adopted tablist by id, and the restarted tablist drew its tabs at the window's top left. Found by reading, not yet reproduced: (1) `StickyHost` (iOS, tvOS, macOS) forgets a record only on a destroy, so a header whose `position: sticky` a restart removed still sticks (`Sticky.swift`, `PixelGridIOS.swift`); (2) on macOS `SplitHost.reset` never releases `BlockHost.shown`, so text selection can meet an old and a new paragraph with one id and trap in `Dictionary(uniqueKeysWithValues:)` (`TextSelectionMac.swift`); (3) `SvgHost`'s maps survive, so a new svg can take an old scene's layer and a press can reach an old scene (`SvgScene.swift`, `PointerIOS.swift`); (4) smaller: `Canvas2DHost` keeps an old layer and `born`, the macOS `hoverMoves` keeps entries, and `ModalHost.refusedRoute` and the macOS `SegmentHost.decisions` can each drop a journal line. A browser's reload builds a new document in the same window, and nothing of the old document's state carries over unless the window holds it. The same for Exact would make a restart build a new `Presenter` and hosts, and carry over only what belongs to the session or its view, each named, so no per-node state can outlive its document and `reset` stops being a list to keep. Numbering nodes per session rather than per runner would also stop anything that does carry over from matching a new node.
- **No bulleted or numbered list** (2026-11-01, the Pokédex agent runs, rounds 5 to 8: three or four agents a round wrote `display="list-item"` with `list-style-type="disc"` or `"circle"`, were refused, and drew each bullet as a box). CSS Lists 3 §3 gives a `list-item` box a `::marker` (`disc`, `circle`, `square`, `decimal`) placed by `list-style-position`; the web would take it as written, and the kernel would need the marker box laid out beside the item's first line, with each host painting it.
- **A `table` takes no `border-collapse`** (2026-10-31, the Pokédex agent runs): `border-spacing` is a row since (one length; HTML's 2px unsaid; `border-spacing=0` with cells' `border-bottom` rules a table flush), but `border-collapse: collapse`, which merges neighbouring cells' borders by CSS 2.1 §17.6.2's conflict rules, is not: the web would take it as written, and the kernel's table grid would need the merge.
- **The fragment after LLP 1153 D5: five edges the last review left** (2026-10-30, from the delta review of ca3c18eecf). (1) The JS target's settlement (`host/web-js/router.js`) counts held.js's `Counting`, which keeps an ordinary asynchronous ticket after its resource replaced or forgot it (rt.js changes `r.ticket`; held.js drops the count only when the request finishes), so a navigation to settled content can wait on an obsolete request where the runner removes forgotten tickets from `pending`: count only current, owned tickets, or retire a count when the replacing commit lands. (2) A new navigation does not end a native hold: Linux (`host/painted/src/presenter.rs`) arms the new activation without clearing `fragment_anchor`, and `hold_fragment()` checks only the person-scroll count and the node; iOS (`Fragment.swift`) can add a second `ElementScrolls` request without dropping the first, and replacing `fragmentHeld` loses the first request's name: retire the previous hold when navigation supersedes it, a navigation that asks no fragment scroll included. (3) The deferred traversal restoration in `host/web/fragment.js` (two frames after Back, around Chrome's own scroll) checks only `personScrolls`: neither `cancel()` nor a later `arm()` invalidates it and it does not check the current history entry, so an app's `scrollIntoView` before it runs is overwritten; bind it to a cancellable activation and history entry. (4) Activation numbers restart with each `RouterContext`, but Linux's reload path and Apple's presenter resets keep `fragment_seen`/`fragmentSeen`, so after a runner replacement activations below the old watermark are not armed and run without the person-scroll check: reset the watermark with the runner, or put a runner generation in the activation's identity. (5) The JS target's `scrollBy` calls `cancel()` with its default `pending = true`, marking an armed fragment superseded, where Linux clears only the existing hold and the runner cancels a pending activation only for `scrollIntoView`: `cancel(false)` for the held-only meaning, with a pending-data test.
- **Native table accessibility stops at roles** (2026-10-30, LLP 1153 D7). The Apple, Linux and Windows hosts expose a table, its rows and its cells by role (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/TableAccessibility.swift`, `host/painted/src/presenter/access.rs` `structure_role`, `host/windows/src/accessibility.rs`), not where each cell stands: AppKit's `AXTable` answers its rows, columns and header elements, UIKit's `.dataTable` container `UIAccessibilityContainerDataTable` (each cell's row and column ranges), AccessKit a row's and a cell's indices and spans, and Android's reader `CollectionInfo` and `CollectionItemInfo`, which `Access.kt` does not set for the new roles (it draws them, and a description list's, as groups). The kernel's placement holds every cell's row, column and spans (`kernel/src/layout/table.rs`): expose it on `Kernel` and answer each platform's table protocol from it, before the Mac's table becomes an `NSTableView` (LLP 1115.002).
- **LLP 1154 steps 2 and 3: Snapback publishes `snapback4-exact`, then exact2 deletes `snapback4/`** (2026-10-31). Step 1 is on main: the native slot's admitted and reserved storage, a package's prebuilt library fetched by hash and embedded on Apple and Linux, its web assets, and `packages/fixtures/vault` proving M5 on the Linux host, the web, macOS and the iOS simulator. What remains is Snapback's (§4: publish the package with D2's `exact.json`, dynamic libraries for Harmony's five targets, the module's call over the C ABI, `init --exact`, the guide) and then exact2's step 3 in one change (§3 Deletes; Harmony depends on the package; M1 to M6). Nothing in exact2 changes until Snapback publishes.

- **Reduce Motion for web-host motion** (2026-10-31; LLP 1061 D5, LLP 1013 D8). Verify the motion the web host starts against platform behavior under Reduce Motion. Shared-element flights (`host/web-js/shared.js`), switches and modal presentation (`host/web/neutral.css`), and reorder groups (`host/web/group-glue.js`) already read the preference. Check navigation, sheet and popover presentation, reorder glides and scrolling. Author-controlled motion reads `exactViewport().prefersReducedMotion` (`docs/recipes/motion.md`). A lane that lands host motion when Reduce Motion turns on (2026-11-12, three review rounds, about 890 lines in 17 files; not landed) still missed: a virtualized list's smooth correction under Reduce Motion already set eases where iOS and the Mac land it on the commit (`host/web/collection-glue.js`); `cancelHostScrolls` (`host/web/navigation/scroll.js`) dropping an ancestor's tracker when an inner scroller's `scrollTop` or `scrollBy` is written, which only `scrollIntoView` should do; Space on a focused button counted as a person's scroll; and an aligned fragment in a scaled, bordered scroller (`host/web/fragment.js`) never released, mixing transformed rectangles with unscaled `clientTop`.
- **The runtime's calendar-difference test stubs its date parser** (2026-11-13, reproduced with main's test at 4dc704ebe). `host/web/tests/js-runtime.test.mjs` loads real `roster.js` with a stand-in for `format.js`, so `isoDate` returns nothing and `x_calendarDiff` returns `null` for valid dates. The suite passes 64 tests and fails this one. Load the real parser for these assertions.
- **Computed presence strings on the JS target** (2026-11-05). Bound exit and layout declarations accept complete string literals in their binding bytecode (`host/web-js/src/presence.rs`). A valid string assembled at runtime clears with a diagnostic, as `presence_tests::exit_values_write_native_css_and_clear_unlisted_values` checks with `leave  200ms both`. Support other computed values through the kernel's parser rather than a second CSS grammar; first decide how the page loads that parser.
- **The web's iOS idioms round 34 left** (2026-10-31, LLP 1115.000 D4.8, D4.14, D4.16). Graders still named: the
  segmented control's grey track and raised chip (four web pairs; a web button group or tabs may read better); the
  split's 320 px list column beside a mostly empty detail at 1440 px (shop-inventory, support-inbox); the phone's
  bottom tab bar; an unstyled bar button that opens a menu showing no affordance ("Ada"); and link rows that are
  `user-select: none`. A switch in a sheet looks like a checkbox but keeps `role="switch"`; Firefox and Safari reach
  a sheet's actions before its fields (no `reading-order`); the Linux painter's empty split still says "No
  Selection". `neutral-look.test.mjs`'s "route header geometry" reads a selector LLP 1153 D8 renamed and fails on
  origin/main.
- **A box over a navigation root sits under the iOS status bar** (2026-10-30, bake-off r34 gradebook-ios). With a native stack the session's viewport is the whole view and `env()` is 0 (`ExactViewIOS.swift` `fit`, `wantsWholeView`), so a child of the navigation root that is no route, an overlay at `position="absolute" inset=0`, is laid out from the screen's top: its first row is under the status bar, where iOS gives it no touches, and the author has no inset to pad. On the web, the layout oracle, the same box starts below the browser's chrome. The compiler now refuses the case the bake-off hit (`navigationPresentation` off a route); lay such a box out in the safe area, or give it `env()`'s insets, for the rest. A lane that subtracts the root overlay's clearance in Taffy's absolute placement (2026-11-12, `vendor/taffy/src/compute/common/absolute.rs`, three review rounds; not landed) moved boxes that were already placed: an auto-inset child whose static position is below the content (y=100 became 162), a flex-start child under a padded parent, and an `inset=0` child in a grid area already inside the safe area, which it shrank. Intersect the child's area with the safe rectangle instead of adding the clearance.
- **Two edges of `var(--name)` (LLP 1153 D6)** (2026-10-30). The loader writes a `var()` naming a token as the name before lowering (`contract/lower/src/var_tokens.rs`), so (1) a row it then refuses quotes the name, not what the author wrote: `color="var(--space.row)"` is refused as `` `color="space.row"`: `space.row` is a length token ``; and (2) a component argument is written only when it is one `var()` (it may be text), so `Card(edge="1px solid var(--line)")`, `line` declared, reaches its row unwritten and is refused there (`lower-css-shorthand`: `` `var(--line)` is not an admitted `border` width, style or color ``). Keep each literal's spelling beside the rewrite for the refusal's words, and write an argument where its prop reaches a style row (`params::reached` walks that path). A lane that resolves composite and forwarded props in style rows and keeps the authored spelling in refusals (2026-11-12, three review rounds, about 880 lines in 18 files; not landed) regressed two things the loader's rewrite did: a prop routed through state (`state selected = tint`, `color=selected`) stays `var(--ink)`, which the native parser refuses, and an enum-typed style prop given `var(--ink)` fails `type-prop` because the rewrite now follows type checking. Its authored-spelling fix covered only refusals worded `` `property="value"` ``, so `font-family="var(--space.row)"` still reports the token's name.
- **Glyph atlas upload nits** (2026-10-31, LLP 1149.001 M10, cut 7; Claude findings 3 and 7). Split a shelf-wrap dirty rectangle if measured worthwhile: the current union copies about 16.9 KiB per fling copy for a few KiB of masks. Packing dirty rows then copying them into staging costs two copies; Claude cites 29.8 μs for cold staging allocation/copy and a few μs per fling batch. A `(shadow, stride, rect)` device input could remove the intermediate copy. Neither optimization is needed for parity; measure before widening the device seam.
- **Complete M10's first-screen attribution and 100 ms budget** (2026-10-31, LLP 1149.001). Cut 7's atlas batch lowers main's carrier from 253.124 to 215.080 ms (qualified n=10/9; nine matched pairs save 32.862 ms median), without the other startup cuts. After the review fixes, five rest/bar pairs give −0.541%/+2.295% median CPU changes, with both ranges crossing zero: the earlier +14.084%/+6.143% singles are variance under the paired-range test. Bar still has intermittent extra carrier timer/dispatch work (40 → 2,460 timer passes in one ten-second pair); isolate its trigger before predicting a saving. Metal/wgpu and Vulkan parity pass; the Windows lane qualifies the original cut at `d008a448c7`, not the review follow-up. A7's raster workers are parked: upload waits dominated, and workers bought no saving. Chunk 2's excluded cohorts still owe observer controls and per-launch dyld attribution where available. See `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “M10, cut 7: the atlas uploaded once” and “M10, the first screen's phases”. The six-check gate remains assigned to the Mac mini. A later layer pass that discovers new glyphs can still flush another batch; batching across an entire such frame remains unimplemented.
- **M10: confirm the combined cold lead and remove the fling instruction increase** (2026-10-31, LLP 1149.001). The six cuts over cut 7 give Ink 187.214 → 159.679 ms on the silver, n=10 per carrier, against GPUI 197.134 and egui 203.295 ms after (n=10/9). Two egui timeouts remain among 80 originals. This combined revision is below both controls in 1 of 1 qualified sessions; six-cut carrier revisions are 3 of 4, with the contrary confirmation and traced cohorts retained. Repeat the combined session before claiming a stable lead. One qualified post-boot pair meets ±5% CPU/cycles in fling, rest, bar and caret, but fling instructions add 32.095 million/s, missing the ≤30 allowance. Cumulative instructions are 258.231 million lower before fling and 63.499 million higher through its end; this pair does not establish pure displacement. The earlier three source-fix rounds stop unrepaired. Remove the remaining fling work without requesting offscreen pictures before first paint. The 100 ms obligation remains; the mini owns the six-check gate. See RESULTS.md, “M10, landed: the six cuts over cut 7”, and retained originals in `target/m10-landed/`.
- **`check` on the Windows host** (2026-10-31, bake-off Windows lane). The painted presenter answers no `check` observation (`scripts/agent-check.mjs` takes web and iOS), so `verify windows` captures each screen unjudged: no contrast, target-size, overflow or largest-text findings. Answer it from the presenter as the Apple agent's `check` does, then let `verify windows` judge light and dark as iOS's does.
- **M2 rest trace on the caret workload** (LLP 1149.001 M2b). The G5m caret now presents twice per second but still uses 27.148 process / 24.661 main-thread ms/s. Its `every(250, tick)` and `every(1000, second)` fire five times per second, and the display link starts and stops twice. See `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G5m, after M2b”.

- **A Mac atom's tooltip does not follow a rewrap** (2026-11-12). `InlineAtomEditorMacTests.testTheTooltipIsAskedOfTheTextViewAndFollowsARewrap` fails at line 819 on main: after the text rewraps, the pill's tooltip rectangle does not move. Check that the same GPU’s `registryID` and archive path survive a reboot, then require `MTLPipelineOptionFailOnBinaryArchiveMiss` to load that retained archive on the same OS and device. If the ID changes, choose a stable device key before claiming retained-cache startup across reboots.

- **Windows device-loss recovery does not reliably restore Mica within three presents** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149.001 Stage 0). The native fixture passed an isolated retry and the next complete suite, then failed with desktop composition disabled through all three retries; an earlier failure restored Mica on retry two where the fixture requires retry one. Separate the host's recovery obligation from compositor availability before changing that assertion. The Stage 0 review-fix continuation passes forced device loss in the NucBox's interactive desktop: the recovered/fresh Mica band has mean 0 and no pixels over 32. That machine needs no GO file. G6d's retry code was already in the earlier failing tip; no recovery policy changed, and the earlier intermittency remains unexplained. Failures: `target/s0/windows/native-attempt/d3d11-device_loss.log` and `target/s0/windows/native-period/d3d11-device_loss.log`; passing recheck: `target/s0/review-fixes/windows/native/d3d11-device_loss.log`. See the Stage 0 section of `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`.
- **A focused Mac textarea's focus ring shows a 1 px line at its right and bottom edges** (2026-10-30, the textarea-resize lane; not caused by `resize`). Seen in a key window after a drag made the agent's window key: its pixels show the ring coming through the box's fractional last pixel. The ring's mask is `TextAreaScroll.drawFocusRingMask` (`Mac/NativeFieldsMac.swift`). A mask on the pixel grid was tried and not verified by running; look at it in a key window with a fractional-height textarea.
- **MIDIMacTests.testAFollowedClockIsBatchedAndStartGoesAtOnce failed once under load, not reproduced** (2026-10-30, iosfix2). One full parallel Mac run at load 49 (2026-10-29 19:28Z) failed `ticks alone no more often than 50 ms` (0.028 s, not above 0.04). Not reproduced since: 5 runs of the class alone beside a cargo build (load 14-18), 5 full parallel Mac suites (load 6-22), and 3 alone, all 0 failures. If it recurs, the interval it measures is wall time between delivered ticks, which a stalled process compresses: measure it on the deliveries' own timestamps.
- **IframeWindowsTests' download case, a red in rt1's 10-29 baseline, not reproduced** (2026-10-30, iosfix2). 3 runs of the class alone at load 12 and 8 full parallel iOS runs (2026-10-29/30, load 15-235) were all clean. It has flaked under load before; if it recurs, keep its log.
- **The native executor's `Proxy` wrapper reads an explicit new target's `prototype`** (2026-10-30, rt1's crossing skip; theme2's 06:26 ruling). js/src/binary.js puts the global `Proxy` and `Proxy.revocable` behind Proxies of the engine's own whose traps record every proxy made, so `keptReply` never keeps a value holding one. Hermes, constructing a callable proxy, runs `ordinaryCreateFromConstructor` on the new target before the `construct` trap, so `Reflect.construct(Proxy, args, C)` (or a bound `Proxy`) reads `C.prototype`, which the engine's `Proxy` doesn't (a getter there runs; js/tests/it/kept_json.rs pins today's one read). Fix in the engine: skip that creation when a `construct` trap is present (the spec's order), or give the executor a native Proxy predicate so the wrapper can go.
- **On the Mac a surface inside a card is darker than the card in dark mode** (2026-10-30, the Mac surface roles; theme2's 04:06 ruling). The Mac's card roles (secondary-background, secondary-grouped-background) are AppKit's underPageBackgroundColor (#f6f6f6 / #282828); tertiary-background and tertiary-grouped-background, a surface inside a card, stay controlBackgroundColor (#ffffff / #1e1e1e), white inside the grey card in light but #1e1e1e inside #282828 in dark, where iOS goes lighter (#2c2c2e inside #1c1c1e). AppKit has no solid between them (unemphasizedSelectedContentBackgroundColor #dcdcdc / #464646 is too strong in light). Revisit if AppKit gains one, or name a dark-only literal pair.
- **Windows: G6e restores cadence; its CPU gate remains open** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149 G6e). The G6d cadence regression is done: restoring the full-period wait and removing the clean-frame reservation skip gives five quiet flings with zero late intervals, p99 16.85 [16.77–16.93] ms and 58.15 [58.07–58.53] displays per gesture second. The full gate is not done: process CPU is 84.08 [74.70–93.39] ms/s against G6c’s 73.11–84.05 range. All 1,247 snapshots match successful frames. No third pacing policy was tried. The PresentMon trace qualifies cadence but has no CPU stacks to attribute the remaining range miss. Evidence: `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G6e”; `target/g6e/final/` and `skip-trial/`.
- **Windows: resolve G6c’s remaining startup shift** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149 G6c). The waitable reservation fixes the D3D11 fling, but startup neutrality is not established: ten quiet final launches give DWM 182.25 [171.22–211.84] ms against G6’s 175.98 [169.40–190.49]. A quiet alternating ten-pair control gives before/after medians 184.29/188.43 ms and paired increase +3.28 [−10.72–37.23] ms. Compare the startup phase timestamps and waitable admission before deciding whether this is launch variation or remaining scheduling cost. G6c stopped after three performance revisions. Evidence: `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G6c”; `target/g6c/final/warm-startup/` and `target/g6c/paired-waitable/`.
- **Egui heavy-list intermittently fails readiness** (2026-10-29, LLP 1149 §4.1 G4; owed to `bench/heavy-list/egui/`, not Exact). One of thirteen timed launches, `target/g4b/series/cold/egui-8.log`, timed out at 30 s with its window frontmost and key. G4a's first functional launch took 14.7 s; four G4b readiness probes did not reproduce the delay. The scoped system logs name no cause. Capture the frame, image-worker and drawable timeline on a failing launch before choosing a fix; the twelve successful launches do not close this. See `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G4”.
- **The s15b2 gate's web-wasm image-raster rule covers whatever is drawn over a full-bleed image** (2026-10-30, theme2's s15b2 sign-off, a follow-up). video-devi's Image box is the whole screen (a full-bleed poster), so the 4-level noise rule (scripts/stage12-named.mjs `WASM_IMAGE_NOISE`, inside Image boxes laid out alike on both sides) also covers the overlays drawn above it. Before the next gate leans on it, count a pixel as image noise only where the Image is the topmost node there (from the whole layout's order), so an overlay's change still differs. A lane that keeps overlay paint out of the rule's coverage (2026-11-12, `host/web/agent-input.js` and `scripts/stage12-named.test.mjs`; not landed) passed in the fleet's Chromium, but its own browser test failed in this Mac's Chrome: a one-level change under an 8×8 image counted as one differing pixel, not image noise. Its reviewers also found stroked overlay text still exempt, since `Range.getClientRects()` leaves out `-webkit-text-stroke`; give a nonzero stroke unknown coverage.
- **A module's availability source could answer from the app's grant** (2026-10-29, rt1; theme2's 21:47 ruling). Without `device.calls` the runner still substitutes `@exact/calls`' sends and sources: `startCall` lists an in-app call and `callsStatus()` answers `available: true` (probe beside `contract/cli/tests/it/voip_calls.rs`), so an app can't hide a feature from the module's own answer. Harmony doesn't need it: its test call's grants are a development build's only (LLP 1069.008, as built 2026-10-29) and the row reads Harmony's own `grants` (`testCallBuild`). For the next app that does: a module's availability source answers its unavailable form, and its grant-gated sends refuse, when the app lacks the module's grant (runner/src/runner/modules/admit.rs), calls as the first example.
- **ResetGate's known losses** (2026-10-29, rt1; triaged with theme2, by probe). `ResetGate.knownLoss` (host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/ResetGate.swift) lists 16 held Runtime calls whose content a restart's reset would lose: `storeChanged`, an incoming `share`, `picked`, an incoming `navigate`/`hostGo`, a document's paths through `change`, `controlTextChanged`, `deliverySync` (re-asked only after `applyTogether`), `canvasImage`, `canvasDisplay`, `textReady`, `midi`, `activities`, `preferExtension`, `designRows`, `setModes`. Most come from the main queue, which the hold never drains: ResetHoldTests (iOS and Mac) shows nothing queued before a restart (main queue, common-modes timer and perform) runs inside it, with a sheet open or two windows restarted together. So none is reachable today; they stay listed for the scan. Reopen if ResetHoldTests fails (the teardown turning the run loop in a common mode, or draining the main queue): then keep such an arrival at the session while held and deliver it after `replayHeld`.
- **PARKED: an import's accent offered as the packages' brand fills** (2026-10-29, dtool3; theme2's 07:21 stop rule, after six review rounds). Design: an import that carries an accent (shadcn's primary, Astryx's accent) offers it as `actions.follow-fill` and `cards.empty-button`, each with its ink token set per scheme to white or black, whichever reaches 4.5:1 (the two ratios multiply to 21 on an opaque fill, so one always does), in a `review.brands` list of its own (a tamber/1.18 line), off until ticked, left out of a bare accept below 4.5:1, on an import's first proposal only. Built and tested on branch `dt3/import-brand-parked` (8d20b67c53, not merged). The rounds found, in turn: inks gone stale through every re-hold (partial accept, edit-held, recompute: aliases, `when` lines, design-held values), designs that check only with their packages (a line-stripping measure hid accent branches), dial orphans across role and brand lines, older tools taking brand items on recomputed proposals, and, last, an offer that invalidates its own measurement (the accent's `when` branch reading the very ink token the offer rewrites). Revisit when the importer can fold the FINAL design (the import and its offers' own operations applied, with the app's packages joined) and measure every tuple there; until then an app sets the brand tokens by full name, which loses nothing but the convenience.
- **LLP 1149 S1, what G3c leaves open** (2026-10-29; `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, G3c). (1) The carrier's fling peak footprint, 592 MiB against GPUI's 409 and the native host's 229: about 340 MiB of owned-unmapped graphics memory during the fling that `vmmap` and `malloc_history` cannot attribute (S1.4). First drop decoded CPU pixels after their GPU upload and re-decode when the texture is evicted; then attribute what remains with Metal allocation profiling at launch. (2) Cold start to the first complete frame is 344 ms for ink, 312 for GPUI and 370 for the native host, all over D15's 100 ms budget; the 100 ms budget phase by phase, as S2 owes on Windows. (3) No content-cadence instrument exists for GPUI, which re-presents unchanged scenes for a second after input: the presented-drawable cadence row compares content cadence (ink) with tick cadence (GPUI) outside a gesture. (4) G3c does not rerun D15's vello-with-rows or wgpu control arms on the Mac. (5) The drawable ring's wait duplicates the per-present completion wait: use one mechanism or let the worker pipeline frames. (6) A per-frame staging arena would remove the two-slot serialization and the double copy.
- **Safari leaves its top-edge blur over the page after a resume, on the iPhone Duo's cover display** (2026-10-30, iOS 27.2, Safari, the Duo closed; seen on Unsaid's first screen). Brought back from the background, Safari blurs the band where a phone's status bar would be, over an unscrolled page's title, though on the cover display its status items sit in its side column; switching tabs clears it. Exact's page draws no blur there and changes nothing across the resume, and the iOS 27.1 Duo simulator does not show it, so it reads as Safari's: report it to Apple (Feedback Assistant) with a photo, and do not pad every page's top to dodge it.
- **The web's touch press under a person's scroll, not yet seen in Firefox for Android** (2026-10-29). `input-glue.js` counts a scroll as a person's when it follows a touch or pen `pointercancel` or a wheel, moves a press under any other scroll, and swallows the click of a touch lifted outside the target it began on. iOS Safari 27.2 on an iPhone passed a pan, a caught glide (Safari keeps that touch: no pointer or touch events reach the page) and a chat that keeps itself at the bottom, by a diagnostics page over the bundled glue. Firefox for Android has run it with synthetic events only.
- **LLP 1071.001 part B: the feed's rows built cheaper** (2026-10-30, re-measured by x2-perf4; the stage 1 line it replaces is done). Part A landed in 1c7b918556 (A1-A3, A5-A8). A4, snapshots compared by revision, is CLOSED: on main 1b2e291a69 (`bun bench/web-shootout/scrollprof.mjs bench/dioxus/web/dist/bounded <out> {6000,24000} 3`, load 16-19, two runs each) `JSON.stringify` has no sampled self time and `snapshot()` is 0.5-2.3 ms inclusive per 3 s, at most 1% of a 255-388 ms task. The scroll's time is now the browser's style, layout and paint of the rows just built, 60-75% of the task: collection-glue's clamped offset read carries 46-94 ms of the forced pass over the new rows. Only part B moves that: B1 (a row is one template clone) and B5 (enumerated style bindings as classes; the feed makes 5,629 `setProperty` calls per 3 s). It waits on Charlie's LLP 1071.001 §8 answers for part B (D5, its measure: a Krausest entry, which needs LLP 1152 D3, or Conduit's feed page), then a measured plan (~/x2theme/x2p4-webfeed-measure.md).
- **Speech, what is owed** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.012 §13's as-built notes). (1) On Android's reader an extension's `preferred` call lands on the main thread after the drive's next request has moved the clock, so an agent path that stamps what a drive prefers with `now()` sees it late: drive 6 of `packages/speech-recognition/test/drives.mjs android` reads 29 of 30 finals. The carrier needs a signal that the call has landed, as `polls` is for a jump's asks. (2) On the reader, `clock settle` cannot wait for an extension's answer, which only the main thread gives. (3) Synthesis has run on no Android app, and neither package's real Android engine has run; the Android 14 emulator's Google services would serve a fixture that binds them. (4) D12 item 6 and `microphone=true` on Apple wait on Charlie's decision (§13).
- **The web shootout, stage 0** (2026-10-28, LLP 1152 r2 §10). `bench/web-shootout/`: the streaming proxy that injects one probe into every arm and applies the leg's latency once, the local Conduit API server, the drives, `report.mjs`, the three baseline arms (static HTML, vanilla JS, Exact's render-served Conduit) on Mac Chrome, the pilot that fixes repetition counts and the tie rule, and the phone feasibility spike (a touch route that exists on a physical iPhone, the `PerformanceObserver` entry-type matrix per pinned engine). - **Windows clipboard colour conversion** (#196). Convert PNGs carrying gAMA, cHRM or iCCP to sRGB before offering a bitmap. PNG-only items preserve colour information, but Paint 11.2609.401.0 loses gamma interpretation and Edge 157.0.4341.46's paste editor receives no image (Windows 11 build 26200.9457).
- **Clipboard fixture large-file setup** (#196). The existing `Uint8Array.from(atob(b64), …)` path for a 3,503,857-byte PNG exceeds the native source turn’s 100 ms CPU budget on Windows 11: 421.9 ms, after `atob` and before the array conversion finishes, with no file write or clipboard call. The refusal is in agent logs while the fixture’s answer stays empty. Prepare the PNG in the app sandbox for the path test; make the fixture’s conversion yield between bounded chunks or use a file-producing source.
- **@exact/clipboard: what stage 1 still owes** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.006 §9 as built). (1) Item 5, `storage.fs.compressImage`'s amendments: `maxDimension` omitted with `type: "image/png"` keeps the size and `maxBytes` refuses (`unfit`) rather than shrinks; `maxPixels` refuses a source over it before decoding; SVG input, on Apple through the hosts' SVG rasterizer (`host/svg-file`) and on the web through an `Image` (which a worker realm has none of), sized as an `img` is; and frame 0 of an APNG, which ImageIO does not give (`packages/clipboard/apple/Clipboard.swift` `firstFrame` does it for the clipboard). Across `js/src/binary.js`, `data/host/src/native.rs`, `data/src/image.rs` and `image/apple.rs`, and `host/web/storage-image.js`. Then the fixture's JPEG, SVG with no size and 16,000 × 1,000 buttons. (2) `ClipboardItem.supports` answers from the declaration on every target, true where the module is absent (Android, Linux, Windows): the binding is one for all targets and data code has no synchronous way to ask whether a module is linked. (3) The Rust binding has no bytes form for a keyed shape's key. (4) A person's steps: `packages/clipboard/test/checklist.md` (Preview, Messages, Safari's and Firefox's activation, D3).
- **The local listener, what is owed** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.005 §10's as-built notes). The T3 Code clone's adoption drive is the module's acceptance (`desktop-activation.ts` on `exactListen`, its broker in TypeScript, `codex-setup-ops.ts` on `openAuthSession`, then `t3 app <dir>` from Terminal with D2's three routing differences and a real ChatGPT sign-in with a cancel and a port in use), on `daehyeon/t3-code-main`. `focus()` from Terminal against T3 Code's own activation (D10), and whether it brings the app in front of the browser after a loopback sign-in, need a person at a Mac.

- **LLP 1133.007, what is still open** (2026-10-30, §10, §14, §17). Not built: the secret field (stage 2) and Windows (stage 3); Linux's `fork` path has not run on Linux ("Desktop processes, after stage 1" below). - **A missing-oracle divergence can print `<end>` on both sides** (2026-10-28, LLP 1148 A2): Harmony's second default walk and Shelf's swipe-delete test diverge at boot, but `verify::locate` drops the reason comments that `compare` used. Show the missing oracle answer and the runner's refusal reason when the first difference has no configuration line; the raw observations already retain both. A lane that shows both (2026-11-12, `semantics/difftest/src/verify.rs`, three review rounds; not landed) took the aggregate `outcome` reason when a refused due task precedes a refused handler, so it reported the handler's `NonFiniteValue` against "no answer for load" and dropped the task's `UnwrapNone`: match each missing-oracle comment to the refusal it follows.

- **Default `contract verify` is slow on Design Tool** (2026-10-28, LLP 1148 A2): its runner half took 515 s for 142 search scripts, including 20 walks of 40 events and 71 stored replays. A sample shows timed-task commits rebuilding the document during clock advances. Measure the cost of the 60-second advances and the search policy before making this a routine author loop; retain the coverage and receipt denominators.

- **Shelf: module effects are outside the differential oracle** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G). `contract verify apps/shelf/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0xeed4df11b739e972 --confirm 2` still disagrees at `clock +1`. The runner consumes `setContentIndex` and `setAppShortcuts` as module effects (`runner/src/runner/modules/declared.rs:132`, `runner/src/runner/commit.rs:682,747`); Lean retains them as host commands (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:824,877`). This is not repeated-command deduplication. The one-event case is kept in `semantics/pending/shelf-module-effects.contract`; reproduce with `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/shelf-module-effects.contract`. Modelling these effects needs their module declarations and observation boundary; do not remove commands by name just to make comparison pass.

- **Excord: login-item sends bypass the differential oracle** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G; pre-existing host-operation boundary in §5). The login switch commits on the runner; Lean refuses because its transcript has no `registerLoginItem` answer. The runner routes module sends internally (`runner/src/runner/modules.rs:355,368`; substitute: `runner/src/runner/modules/declared/desktop.rs:105,116`); Lean asks the data oracle (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:851,206`). The isolated case is `semantics/pending/excord-login-item.contract`: `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/excord-login-item.contract`. App receipt: `contract verify apps/excord/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0x986bb432cbe497dd --confirm 2`. Keep module replies and their later resource changes in scope together when extending the oracle.

- **Photo Editor: camera and photo-library sends are outside the differential oracle** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G; pre-existing, §5). `getUserMedia` and `copyPhotoAsset` commit through the runner's module route (`runner/src/runner/modules.rs:355,368`; caller: `runner/src/runner/queue.rs:397`), while Lean asks the data transcript and refuses its missing answers (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:851,206`). `semantics/pending/photo-editor-host-operations.contract` keeps one input per operation; reproduce with `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/photo-editor-host-operations.contract`. App receipt: `contract verify apps/photo-editor/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0x98a7091cbe8d1f60 --confirm 2`.

- **Eight apps' explore runs diverge at their first host operation outside the oracle** (2026-10-28, pre-existing: on `8233879705` they diverged at boot; after LLP 1148 stage 1 captures the boot's host facts they diverge at `registerLoginItem`, `getUserMedia`, `getCurrentPosition`, `selectContacts`, `addGeofence`, `cancelSpeech`, `exactTime` and a live time). The harness has no answer for a host operation; LLP 1148 D12's inventory is where they get one, and until then `verify` on such an app says `OUTSIDE` for nothing and diverges instead.
- **What can still lose a request kept past its answer** (2026-10-28, LLP 1146 §8). LLP 1146 D2 keeps a let-go request with effects running until it lands: a macOS quit, the Apple driver's exit and the Linux host's orderly exit (an agent's quit, a headless run's end) wait for it within their bounds, and iOS holds a background task while one is in flight (`FinishHold`). A reload or an update ends the engine with the request in flight, and closing a window on Windows drains only the store (`host/windows/src/window.rs`): each loses its reply. Nothing protects the request on Android once the app is stopped, as the reader drains only the store at `onStop` (`host/android/canvas/src/main/java/dev/exact/canvas/ExactActivity.kt`), nor on iOS once the background time the system grants runs out: the app may then be suspended, and a process the system ends before the reply lands loses it. A rotating refresh token spent by a request whose reply is lost signs the person out at the next refresh; Lexy refreshes Lexus's rotating token about every half hour, and each refresh is exposed while it is in flight. The Windows close can wait as the Linux headless exit does (`exact_painted::teardown::finish`), driven on the NucBox; the Android reader can keep its process running while `must_finish` holds; a reload and an update need the request handed to the engine that follows.
- **A held-out OAuth case fails now and then on the Linux host under heavy load** (2026-10-27, LLP 1146 D7). Twice, a case that passes on the web failed once on Linux at a load average of 700 to 900 and passed on rerun; 64 runs of every profile's cases at 90 to 830 all passed, and no failing output survives. No known cause fits the two failures. Since 3b1a98297b, which landed after both, the Linux host writes the store on a thread drained at exit within a bound, and `reload` kills the app 2 s after closing its input (`scripts/agent-stdio.mjs` `close`), which under load could cut a write short and fail a case anew. Ruled out: `clock data`'s 20 s bound (the same on the web), and a kept connection the fixture closed, as it keeps idle connections open (`idleTimeout: 0`, `packages/oauth/test/fixture.ts`) and the Linux transport now sends a request again when a kept connection closes unanswered (expo/ibex#3, vendored). The held-out cases are withheld from the package's authors (`~/.cache/exact-oauth-heldout`): render them with `packages/oauth/test/cases.mjs` `casesFor`, never read them.
- **The local network's owed pieces** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.036 §14 as built). Apple's probe (D8) is not built, so a reach's allowance becomes evidence only through a Bonjour browse or resolve; the bake does not refuse a literal `browseServices(type=…)` no `net.dns-sd` line lists (admission refuses it, `SecurityError`); stage 3's conformance fixture in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, stage 4's seam tests, and the Avahi and windns browsers are not written. Every real-network drive needs a person: macOS's Local Network alert for the bundle, and drive 9 with a WLED light on an iPhone, an Android phone and Chrome (`packages/local-network/test/checklist.md`).
- **LLP 1133.021: Android's hosted views take no touches, and there is no headless session** (2026-10-27, §9 items 4 and 6). `exact-map` on Android does not pan or zoom by touch, and `MapView` hears only create, start, resume, pause and stop (not `onSaveInstanceState` or `onLowMemory`); a geofence crossing on Android waits for the next launch. map-demo has no Android crate. Each is owed by the RFC's stage 3.
- **LLP 1133.021: what is not yet built or measured** (2026-10-27). Stage 4 (each package packed into an `exact new` app, and a third party's region module reaching `context.regions`); the simulator's background relaunch for a region under `--bind geofencing`, with its boot time and `backgroundTimeRemaining`; D21's region-carrier launch cost and the time to the map on an iPhone; Caltrain's arrival notice, which waits for `@exact/notifications` (§15 question 5).
- **@exact/nfc's Apple and Android stages: injected tags, their tests and the drives** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.035 §12 stages 2 and 3). The registries (`packages/nfc/apple/NfcRegistry.swift`, `packages/nfc/android/Nfc.kt`) are written but take no injected tag source in a test build, so the XCTests and JVM tests over claims, `busy`, `once`, writes, commands with their leases and two sessions sharing the registry do not exist. Not run: Shelf's drives on the iOS simulator and the Android emulator, drive 0 on macOS (and that its bake links no Core NFC), `--tvos --no-install` with no NFC key, the fixture's Linux drive (`packages/nfc/test/linux.mjs`), and the bound fixture on the simulator and emulator reading `available` false. The runner's permission continuation (`permits.rs`) does not call `arrival_claimed` when a write held on its permission becomes a value ticket, so a write allowed after another is held replaces nothing; the web runtime does.
- **The share extension on a device** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.024 stage 2). A device build skips it and says so until the team registers the App Group `group.<app id>` and the App ID `<app id>.share`; `entryProfiles` (`host/apple/devices.mjs`) then has to take the share extension's id beside the widgets'. A Mac build under an Apple Development identity packages it with the team's group, which no drive has run: the drives use ad-hoc builds and a scratch inbox.
- **Android's share target, what is owed** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.024 stage 3). Sharing text and a photo to Fieldnotes from another app's share sheet on an Android phone, and the web member's `share_target` in an installed Chromium, both by hand. The copy runs on exact2's thread with no five-second bound (D3), so a large warm share holds frames while it copies: move it off that thread, setting the token from there. The `shareIntent` hatch (D6) waits for a consumer.
- **Compose, what is owed** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.024 stage 5). The Calendar example has no Android crate, so `@exact/compose`'s Android cell is compiled and not driven, and the iOS simulator drive was not run (CoreSimulator hung on `simctl list`); give it one (its contacts and calendar packages have Android cells) and run `examples/calendar/tests/compose.mjs android`. `share(files=)` on Android still carries text only: wire it through the reader's file provider (`dev.exact.canvas.Files`, `ExtensionContext.contentUri`) and drive a file share on the emulator. The hand steps of `packages/compose/test/checklist.md`.
- **LLP 1133.024 stages 6 to 8 are not built** (2026-10-28). Stage 6: the Rust implementation slot for the painted hosts (D22 item 4) and Windows's browser and compose cells, driven on the NucBox. Stage 7: print (the kernel's `print_layout`, the cut over pages, `@exact/print` and its cells, the Chrome oracle). Stage 8 waits on LLP 1115.004 S3.
- **The in-app browser, what is owed** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.024 stage 4). (1) The hand steps of `packages/in-app-browser/test/checklist.md`: Safari's view and Done on the iOS simulator and an iPhone, a Custom Tab and its close on an Android device with Chrome and on one without a Custom Tabs browser, the Mac's default browser, a new tab on the web. (2) What `androidx.browser` adds to Excord's release APK after R8, measured against a build without the module (its AAR is 229,576 bytes). (3) A module's `context.log` on Android goes to logcat, not the agent's journal, so `state`/`logs` under the agent never show it there; the drive reads logcat. (4) The Custom Tab stays unpinned (LLP 1133.024 D12, ruled 2026-11-11), so the Android hand steps include a link an installed app claims (checklist step 8); nobody has watched one.
- **Payments: what is owed before stages 1 to 3 are done** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.034 §12's as-built notes). (1) The Apple conformance suite with `--bind payments` on the simulator beyond the wallet's status (the request from the method data, the sheet over the session's window and refused over an inactive scene, a dismissal answered `AbortError`), on the model of `packages/camera/test/apple.mjs`; and the cost probe. (2) A call with no `onmerchantvalidation` and an Apple Pay method on the web is refused `payment-merchant-validation` when made; D10 has the bake refuse it. (3) The web send is `"gesture": true`, not D16 item 7's adoption by `exact-payment-button` that the declaration's `adopts` names; Safari's call-stack rule may refuse the gesture path, and no engine here can show which (Playwright's WebKit has no `PaymentRequest`). (4) Drive 11 on an emulator with a Google Play image (none on this Mac), and the async lane's second tier building the fixture's APK (`bun packages/payments/test/android.mjs`). (5) Drive 6's third part (leave during a retry, then a second Order a Print: `busy` on the web, queued natively) is held by the runner's tests, not driven.
- **A `scrollIntoView` that meets the reader's scroll: what LLP 1070.000 §2.6 leaves open** (2026-10-31). (1) iOS takes as the reader's move against a request only a finger's drag, the taps that scroll a list and the tvOS remote's step, so a request's corrections can move against an assistive technology's scroll, a hardware key's (on iPad, where `allowsKeyboardScrolling` is on) and the tvOS focus engine's. UIKit's own signals tell them: `accessibilityScroll(_:)`, `isScrollAnimating` (iOS 17.4, which a list's own requests never set, since Exact drives their animation) and `didUpdateFocus(in:with:)`; whether each comes for its path is unchecked on a simulator. On the web an assistive scroll cannot be told from momentum: a request made during it waits, then lands. macOS takes it as the reader's, as any move it did not make, and the presenter Android, Linux and Windows share does too, since it makes that scroll through the reader's own input path. (2) A nested request whose outer list waited begins its inner part in the report that admits the outer row, before the inner list's styles are computed, so an inner `scroll-padding` costs one more correction: the command's continuation phase (`runner/src/runner/into_view.rs`) has no counterpart in a report. (3) Windows: what a programmatic scroll does to the reader's momentum is unmeasured. Linux gains the rule with kinetic scrolling: GTK 4 drops a jump made during kinetic scrolling unless `gtk_scrolled_window_set_kinetic_scrolling` turns it off first. (4) No XCTest drives UIKit's moving paths through `CollectionIOS.correct`: a real deceleration (instant, and a smooth request's `stopScrollingAndZooming()` then `OffsetDriver`; Excord's "a send while the list is still moving" drive covers the instant path on a simulator), and the paths that need a finger: a smooth request's animation under a still finger, stopped where the port is when the finger drags (`scrollViewWillBeginDragging`) and then reported as a gesture, and a smooth request made while a finger drags landing at once. `isTracking` and `isDragging` cannot be set from a test, so these need a touch-driven harness (the in-process touch synthesis of the UIKit fling probe). (5) The presenter Android, Linux and Windows share reports no finger on the port and no animation, and a gesture only as the reader's move of the port: so a request made under a finger is admitted there and taken over by the drag, and a press that moves nothing is no gesture there. (6) LLP 1070 H4's motion guard is owed one rule for every host: the web makes no anchor's shift of a row list while the reader moves it; iOS and macOS do, so a card whole at only one of the two places is not judged `done` but moves (§2.6). (7) Whether AppKit carries momentum on after a second flick ends a live scroll is unmeasured; the macOS host's 300 ms quiet interval after a move outside a live scroll stands on it. (8) Whether AppKit sends `touchesBegan` for touches already resting on the trackpad when a list's document starts to accept indirect touches (as a request begins) is unmeasured: if it does, with a fresh timestamp, a resting contact cancels the request. (9) The JS target (`host/web-js/list.js`) does not look a nested request's inner target up before moving, as the runner does: it takes the first outer list with a row of the key, not the one whose row holds the inner list, and refuses an inner part only once its outer row is built, the outer row then left in view (a request that waits is refused where its wait ends, not when it is made). Matching the runner needs this target to build an unmounted row aside, as the runner's `inner_target` does. (10) The web glue keeps each list's own copy of its scrollport's offset and sequence (`offset`, `sequence`, `exact`, `clamp`), and `echo` copies one list's write into the others in that port; held once on the port's activity record, they would need no `echo`.
- **The desktop modules, what is owed** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.025 §14's as-built notes). (1) **macOS:** the XCTests beside `MenuBarMacTests` (a status item's title, image, menu, dimming and removal; the Dock menu's order; `focus()` showing a hidden window; the unpresented first frame); the cost probe (D13) and the time from launch to the status item's placement; §12.4's timer title and its App Nap drive; the sample host's two sessions reading one chord; a document app's status item moving to its next session. (2) **A hot key's press on macOS has the device's origin:** the Carbon handler is the package's (`packages/global-shortcuts/apple/GlobalShortcuts.swift`) and a press is its `emit`; D11 items 2 and 3 want an ExactKit `hotkeys` service that raises it as a person's activation, as `exact_painted::desktop` does for the Rust hosts. (3) **Q9:** whether `SMAppService.mainApp`'s login launch carries `keyAELaunchedAsLogInItem` needs a logout and login on a Mac (`packages/dock/test/checklist.md`, Mac 6). (4) **Windows, stage 2's rest:** the notification-area icon and its flyout, `TaskbarCreated`, `keepRunning`, the launch with no window, one instance and the activation record (Windows App SDK), the startup task, the pair's `WH_KEYBOARD_LL` hook, and the session running while its window is hidden (D11 item 8); then the NucBox drives. (5) §12.1, the T3 Code clone dropping its Carbon registration, is on `daehyeon/t3-code-main`.
- **A layer node on the Android reader can lose its drawing the way a slot did** (2026-10-28, found reviewing `host/android/canvas/.../Slots.kt`). HWUI discards the display list of a node that leaves its tree, and exact2 sends a layer's drawing (op 30) only when it changes. A layer whose drawing rows were all freed before the row taking it over was drawn (a waiting row, or one made on the worker) would draw empty, with any slots inside it.
