Slots now keep their drawings and are recorded again (`Slots.refill`, also when a layer's drawing is recorded); `Layers` could keep each layer's ops the same way and record a layer again as a row drawing it is drawn. Not seen in Excord.
- **Android presents no dialog or popover its author lays out** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.001 D10's Draft paragraph). Linux and Windows now paint one in the window (LLP 1115.004 D10.7, `host/painted/src/presenter/alert.rs`); Android still refuses one ("not yet in Android's windows"), so Excord's switcher, profile, member list and pickers do not open there. The Draft names the five pieces owed, each its own work: the subtree's op stream in the surface's window (a modal bottom sheet in a compact window, a basic dialog wider, a `PopupWindow` for a popover), the page's contact path in that window, a hosted `EditText` per window, TalkBack through D13's provider, and the framework's motion.
- **Linux and Windows: what an authored dialog or popover still lacks** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.004 D10.7; the last review round of `presenter/alert.rs`). (1) A field in one has no edit menu (right click, the Menu key, Shift+F10): `edit_menu.rs` refuses while any surface is open, and the menu is itself a surface, of which one is open at a time; the edit menu would have to stack above the box. (2) The page's scrollbars are painted after the box and its scrim (`paint.rs`, `paint_scrollbars` after `paint_alert`), so where one crosses them it shows on top; presses on it stay inert. (3) A `focus` handler that closes the box while it opens queues `open>closed` and then `closed>open` for the same toggle, so the `toggle` event reports it open (`queue_toggle` keeps the last state). (4) Desktop Linux presents no menu popover yet, and its refusal still says "Linux does not support popover presentation" (`navigation.rs`, pinned by `tests/pinned/host.rs`); the agent's `contextmenu` op needs a shell that makes windows.
- **Windows: an authored dialog and popover wear the neutral look until D12's Fluent column** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.004 D10.7). The kernel's starts for them are the neutral column's (`kernel/src/arena/alert.rs`, contexts 13 to 15); WinUI's ContentDialog and Flyout metrics (an 8 px corner, a 1 px stroke, 24 px padding, 320 to 548 px wide; a Flyout's 16, 15, 16, 17 padding on Acrylic) are listed in D10.7 for that column. The popover is painted in the app's window, so it is clipped by it where a Flyout is not; and D10.7 asks Charlie whether an unsaid popover opens above its invoker, as a Flyout does.
- **Excord's Mac transcript drops frames while it scrolls on a real account** (2026-10-27, Charlie, by hand, on his account; first seen as a jerk past the end). A ⌥⌘T trace from his MacBook (120 Hz, 83 s of late frames) settles where it is not: the stretch past the end is free, since its hundreds of scroll events commit nothing. Of 82 missed frames, 8 followed the lists re-asking on every change elsewhere on the account, which the lists' own versions now stop (`live.ts`). The other 67 followed scroll events that moved the content: each took about 10 ms of the main thread (about 4 ms runtime, 4 ms apply, the rest unattributed) across about four kernel transactions, all with no tree change. An agent drive cannot stand in for it: under the agent nearly every batch the app applies is the agent's own (its clock steps and position reports; `docs/agent-pitfalls.md`), and no frame is presented, so a `sample` of one says little about a real scroll. A second trace from his account with that fix in (e2fc73048e, 2026-10-27) puts the rest in building rows: of the 66 frames missed in its last 2.7 s of scrolling, 58 came in frames that built rows as they scrolled into view (five or more row segments), each about 5.6 ms of apply, 4.0 ms of runtime and 7.8 ms no phase claims (Core Animation's commit and text drawing are not wrapped), about two frames at 120 Hz; frames that built no rows seldom missed, and a row's hover in or out cost about 5 ms of apply too. The fixture's rows (plain text) are cheap enough that no drive there shows it. The rows his trace built are the same sites as the fixture's (one text run each, few pictures), so the cost is the host building rows on main as they scroll in, not his content: macOS still fills synchronously, and LLP 1072 stage 5 (below, parked) is the fix.
- **A full-screen route over a Mac split is drawn in the list's column** (2026-10-27, Excord's image viewer). `SplitHost.read` ends the detail's pane at the first `modal` or `fullscreen` route, and `sheetRoutes` puts only a `modal` one in a sheet, so a `fullscreen` route stays in the page's view, which is the list's column: Excord's picture shows there, beside the chat, at the column's width (the root overlay it replaced did the same). It could cover the whole window, as Discord's desktop viewer does, or at least the detail's pane.
- **LLP 1072 stage 5: asynchronous fills on macOS** (2026-10-26, parked 2026-10-27 on `parked/mac-async-fills` and 2026-10-28 on `parked/mac-async-fills-seq`). One flag (`ExactSession.asyncFills` on for every Apple host); on the M5 a build with it had no late frame in the last 600 of a scroll, against a p99 of 143 ms without, and it is what Excord's Mac transcript still misses frames for (row builds on main, about 2 ms a row on a real account). The branch also orders what a landing sets off behind the batch that landed it (`holdBatch`, `publishedSeq`, `Collection.holding`); the Mac and iOS XCTests pass. Three more blind rounds (sol, Grok) each found new interleavings, several also on iOS today (its commit message lists round 3's). The second design, that one ordering rule (every batch keeps the owner sequence number of the job that made it and main applies in that order), is parked on `parked/mac-async-fills-seq` (2026-10-28) after three more rounds: its Mac and iOS XCTests pass, and round 3's review (sol) still found three interleavings, one a list's pending fill dropped while a tick is in flight (its commit message lists them). Both designs break the same assumption, that `apply` has presented before it returns, which commands, focus restore, reveals and collection reports all lean on. A third attempt should start from those callers, or take the cost off main another way (building a row cheaper there).
- **A native host refuses a data-module answer holding a lone UTF-16 surrogate** (2026-10-26, Excord). `JSON.stringify` writes one as `\udXXX`, which a browser's `JSON.parse` accepts and serde_json refuses ("unexpected end of hex escape"), so the same answer loads on the web and is refused on Apple. Excord's came from cutting a preview with `.slice`; its cuts are now by code point (`clip`), but any app's string from a server or a cut can carry one. The reader could take it as U+FFFD, as WHATWG's UTF-8 decode does for an unpaired surrogate.
- **Qualify the 65 s warm gate budget** (Codex, 2026-10-26, LLP 1142 landing). All six checks passed at `faaf45c373`, but their total was 66.61 s on a shared build host; the earlier `9369bf2a8a` run took 63.38 s. Source revisions and shared-host load both changed, so the excess is not yet attributed. Profile under controlled load without dropping coverage or weakening assertions.
- **A drawing in a widget is a picture leaf, when a widget needs one** (2026-10-26, from closing `issues/closed/20261019-widget-circular-gauge-and-svg.md`; no consumer waits). The snapshot compiler refuses `svg` (`contract/cli/src/snapshot.rs`). Its shape is LLP 1126 D4's picture leaf: one static `svg`, drawn into SwiftUI's `Canvas` from the scene `host/apple/src/svg.rs` already flattens (paths, paint, stroke), not a SwiftUI path per shape.
- **AppFunctions for Android** (2026-10-26, LLP 1133.023 D8, §14 stage 3). Not built: the AppFunctions SDK and KSP in the reader's locked Gradle graph, an `AppFunctionService`, the generated `@AppFunction`s, and their enabled state. Until then Android has no tool door, and the search-index mirror lives in the package rather than the host.
- **Windows: the app's own shortcut list and the jump list drive** (2026-10-26, LLP 1133.023 §14 stage 4). `setAppShortcuts` has no Windows implementation, so only the manifest's tasks are set. The NucBox drive (the jump list read back in the console session, and `shelf.exe /add` landing at `/add`) has not been run.
- **Excord's fling and image numbers on a device** (2026-10-27, LLP 1144 D5, LLP 1145 §4). On the simulator (`apps/excord/diary/2026-10-27-fling.md`) six flings of #general had 2 late frames of 724 and no image shown blank, so this run gives 1145 D1 no late image to rank. A phone's run decides the budget. Under platform timing `fling.mjs`'s `--to` needs taps between its waits for Excord's data to land (LLP 1080.000 §12); a `--to` verb that lands pending `then`s without taking the clock would say so directly.
- **Qualify the GPUI rendering backend in production Excord with accessibility active.** Production uses the CPU renderer. The isolated candidate retains font source bytes and preserves sprite insertion order. With screenshot inspection before each run, two matched 200-message, 3,600-step Windows chat pairs on prototype `3d5a4bf06b` used 25.48% more CPU time and 22.40% more CPU cycles than GPUI; private memory fell 7.87%, but working set rose 10.91%. The earlier CPU lead did not qualify active accessibility: inspection activates Exact's adapter even without requesting accessibility text (below). Native UI Automation inspection of the GPUI benchmark exposes only the window controls, while Exact exposes the visible chat text; this is not an equivalent accessibility workload (`gpui-accessibility-observation.json` and `exact-accessibility-observation.json`). Font retention alone reduced private memory by 11.63% and working set by 23.07% in the earlier same-executable comparison; it did not show a CPU improvement. The candidate passes 128 font pixel cases and 864 cached-drawing cases without extra GPU batches. Four earlier frame traces had no display gaps over 25 ms at 60 Hz, so a smoothness lead is unproven. Local evidence is `external-ownedfonts-layout-performance-qualification.json` and the `external-ownedfonts-diagnostic3-*-qualification.json` reports under `target/windows-chat-comparison/`. Production clipping, SVG, opacity groups, gradients, variable fonts, device recovery, other refresh rates and cold startup remain open.

- **Reduce Windows accessibility synchronization cost without dropping behavior.** Screenshot-only inspection activates the Windows adapter; listing and activating the window do not. The adapter stays active after the helper exits. One instrumented executable used 30.26% more CPU cycles and 32.12% more CPU time with accessibility active than dormant. Its 3,600 synchronization calls took 906.78 ms elapsed, including 564.72 ms in the shared tree path. Two phase profiles attribute 41.9–42.5% of synchronization cycles to the semantic tree walk, 23.0–23.5% to AccessKit updates, 8.7–8.8% to the box lookup rebuild and 1.4% to serialization. Borrowed child traversal removes ID snapshots and repeated child lookups: two reversed-order, same-executable pairs with accessibility active used 4.07% fewer process CPU cycles and 5.80% less CPU time on average; tree-walk cycles fell 10.06%. Ten accessibility tests and 3,600 native scrolling comparisons returned identical nodes, identities and item positions. Production Excord passes scrolling, menu and composer smoke checks. Checking visibility before computing text and button names reduced process cycles by 5.60% and CPU time by 13.67% in two reversed-order prototype pairs on `b3c26d3c4e`; tree-walk cycles fell 42.86%, while private memory rose 0.67%. All 3,600 native comparisons preserved nodes, identities and item positions (`external-access-visible-quiet-qualification.json`). The profiler buffers snapshots and writes them from workers after 3,600 accessibility calls; two earlier samples with synchronous logging did not finish the workload and are excluded. These are prototype chat measurements, not an Excord-wide or GPUI lead. Continue reducing repeated tree work while preserving behavior; `external-access-borrow-qualification.json` and `external-access-phases-qualification.json` hold the measurements. Two reader-payload bypass prototypes remain isolated: serialization alone showed a small gain in two pairs; additionally skipping unused change records lowered cycles but increased CPU time by 5.62% on average. Neither establishes a reliable overall speedup. Their source patches, binaries and results are preserved under `target/windows-chat-comparison/`; see `external-access-activation-qualification.json`, `external-access-refresh-performance-qualification.json`, `external-access-refresh2-performance-qualification.json` and `external-access-refresh-adoption.json`.
- **Establish native keyboard behavior after system-menu Escape.** In Excord, the End key never reaches the HWND, and menu exit arrives only with the next click, despite the popup disappearing. Compare a native edit control before adding a keyboard workaround. First clicks after system and editing menus now use the button message's position and pass the native drive. See `menu-input-trace-qualification.json` and `button-position-qualification.json` under `target/windows-chat-comparison/`.
- **Qualify the isolated Windows GPU renderer before adoption.** The approved fresh three-round clipping/SVG pass is exhausted. Its final candidate draws nine Excord GPU frames before an opacity group causes CPU fallback. Native readbacks expose hard fractional clip edges at 100–200% scale; SVG blending differs by up to three channel levels, exceeding the unchanged one-level threshold. Keep it isolated (`excord-gpu-renewed-qualification.json` under `target/windows-chat-comparison/`). Creation, resize, presentation and glyph-failure injection recover to CPU and complete scrolling. Actual driver removal, embedded CFF/Arabic/color fonts, atlas pressure and an imperceptible late fallback remain unqualified. Keep SSE2: the isolated AVX2 release sampler gains an uneven 4.3% and has exhausted three rounds without native qualification. See `recovery-features-qualification.json`, `glyph-recovery-qualification.json`, `embedded-font-qualification.json` and `image-pair-release-qualification.json`.
- **Attribute Windows idle work.** Notification-driven image decoding eliminates the measured decoder polling. Foreground activation still triggers recurring accessibility/text queries from an unidentified client; do not subtract that cost from comparisons or assume Inspect caused it. Inspect has exited and normal release launches work. See `idle-images-qualification.json` and `wake-stages-qualification.json`. The separately rejected history-fixture launch and original fixture shutdown have not been retried.
- **Finish Windows menu, text-size and contrast qualification.** Scrolling menus pass native Home/End, wheel, thumb drag, item selection, Escape and live 225–100% reflow at 360×500. High-contrast and touch menu checks remain. Long embed metadata wraps in renderer tests but still needs native validation. Header icons grow with text; the fixed-icon attempt exhausted three rounds. Qualify contrast selection colors: the attempted repaint failed and was removed. See `menu-overflow-qualification.json`, `embed-qualification.json`, `icon-scale-qualification.json` and `target/excord-contrast-round3.log`.
- **Finish Windows dialog and display-scale checks.** Live 100–200% scale changes preserve the restored window above the taskbar, and the 100–150–100% regression preserves transcript anchors. Dialogs pass keyboard/pointer, long-content scrolling and all four contrast presets. Cross-monitor scale changes and brush transitions remain unqualified. See `target/excord-dpi-checks.json`, `dpi-scroll-qualification.json` and `target/excord-dialog-feedback-checks.json`.
- **Finish Windows regression-suite portability.** Symbolic-link privilege, Apple-host filesystem assumptions, a Tamber import and terminal fixture path escapes failed the pre-integration tests. Linux-only workspace code and Harmony's missing vendored dependency also failed. Full-workspace formatting exceeds Windows' command length. Reassess using the integration logs before changing coverage. Separately, seven `exact-js-bake` integration cases fail path or reproducibility comparisons; preserve their equality assertions (`portable-js-bake-tests.log`).
- **Make Excord app tests observe what they wait for.** Fixed real-time waits remain in three places. The Windows composer test (`fixture/composer.e2e.test.ts`) launches each of its tests with `clock +500 real`, not a wait for `gateway.state == "ready"`. Another member's vote in `app.test.contract` gets `clock +100 real` to arrive: its counts are in state only inside `win.rows`, at an index the window decides (`win.rows.138.poll.0.answers.2.count` on the web at 420x900). `fixture/routing.e2e.test.ts` waits `plus(1000, true)` for a list to go quiet (three tests) and after an arrival (`arrive`); ending these on a change of state would stop the clock short of the full second and of any timer due in it. The mutation clock also waits for held sends to time out before exposing the pending row, although the native window shows it immediately. Retain per-mutation timestamps while allowing that observation. See `find-focus-app-suite-concurrent.json` and `target/excord-held-send-trace.json`. Cancelling a held history lookup preserves a new draft; successful reuse of its late response remains unqualified (`history-hold-qualification.json`).
- **Make the Excord fixture's speed assertions hold on a loaded machine.** `world.test.ts` asserts generation under 300 ms and `transcript-world.test.ts` under one second on the wall clock, and a loaded machine fails them.
- **Finish Windows text interaction and accessibility.** Double-clicking `word,` includes the comma, unlike Notepad. Inline links are clickable but lack Tab/Enter navigation. Clicking a transcript message leaves focus unset, so Page Up does nothing until Tab reaches the list. Each attempted fix exhausted three rounds; retain current behavior until a scoped follow-up. UI Automation text geometry, ScrollPattern and Narrator behavior remain unqualified; the geometry candidate is in `target/excord-access-geometry-candidate/`.
- **Place partial GIF frames on the Windows logical canvas.** WIC reports first-frame dimensions separately from the logical screen. The isolated source handles the basic offset frame, but reduction and native use remain unqualified after three rounds. Candidate: `target/excord-gif-canvas-candidate/`.
- **Finish Windows IME qualification.** Japanese conversion, cancellation, committed clauses, undo, focus changes and 150% candidate placement pass native checks. Composition uses dotted/solid underlines without the blue selection block. Pointer placement under the prediction popup remains untested because the UI driver refuses a point owned by Windows Input Experience. Other clause decorations remain unqualified; non-text composition updates have native dispatch regression coverage.

- **Agent words for an extension an app uses only through its view** (2026-10-26, LLP 1133.027 §10 drive 1). `prefer extension camera.picture=…` is refused in Shelf, which uses `@exact/camera` only for its `exact-camera` viewfinder: the agent's words resolve through a plan module row of that extension, and a view-only use makes none. So drive 1's lookup (the ISBN fixture shown in the viewfinder, the Open Library answer, Use This Book) runs in the vision fixture app (`packages/barcode-detection/test/agent.mjs`) but not in Shelf, whose scan screen is driven with the camera's default agent picture. Resolve the words through the package's views too.
- **Apple text detection must preserve an explicit script when matching languages** (#254 review). `packages/text-detection/apple/TextDetection.swift` truncates `ms-zsm-Arab` to Malay and matches Vision's `ms-MY` on macOS, despite the requested Arabic script. Apply LLP 1133.027 D6's unsupported-language refusal before truncation, with Apple regression coverage.
- **Android for vision and translation (LLP 1133.027 §12 stage 4)** (2026-10-26). Barcode detection has the non-consuming track observer and bundled ML Kit implementation; text detection reads Latin text from still images with bundled ML Kit. QR and PDF417 decoding, concurrent scans, rotation, camera release/resume and a photo during scanning passed on an Android 16 emulator; physical-phone qualification remains. The camera agent picture reaches observers (D17 item 2). Owed: translation in the locked Gradle graph (D14), Excord's Google attribution (D10), and the remaining drives 1 to 8 on an emulator.
- **What a scrolling list's batch still costs on iOS besides its rows** (2026-10-27, bake-off r29 feed-exact and tasks-exact, feel probe; the scrub is 0 to 9 ms/s now, SwiftUI's feed 17). Each batch of a list that is its route's `navigationScroll` syncs the navigation projection (`onlyListRows` reads the named scroller as projected, so none of its rows' batches skip it: about 13 % of `Presenter.apply` through tasks-exact's status-bar scroll to the top), and `SwipeActionsHost.sync` visits every swipe row each batch. A new post's like button is a heavy leaf, made and configured for every row the pool hands out (`ButtonConfigurationIOS.apply`, most of it UIKit updating the symbol's image view); a pool of buttons by face would keep the making. Rows that show as they are made still raster their text urgently on the main thread. An authored smooth scroll far down a list shows nothing between its ends (LLP 1070.000 §6.2, declared); it could glimpse as the indicator's drag does.
- **Should exact2's games declare `android:appCategory="game"`?** (2026-10-25, LLP 1133.029 D6, §12). Android 16 ignores orientation and resizability restrictions on screens 600 dp and wider for apps targeting API 36, and exempts games "based on the android:appCategory flag" ([behaviour changes](https://developer.android.com/about/versions/16/behavior-changes-16)). exact2's Android build sets no category, so a game's manifest `orientation` and an explicit `orientationlock` type are ignored on a tablet (`current` is not). The category also changes how Play lists the app, so it is a decision for every game, not one module's.
- **`wakelock` on Linux and Windows** (2026-10-25, LLP 1133.029 §12). Both journal `wakelock: unavailable` (`docs/reference.md`, "Keep the screen awake"), and both platforms have the capability: Windows' `PowerCreateRequest` and `PowerSetRequest(PowerRequestDisplayRequired)`, and on Linux the Inhibit portal's idle flag (8). Fill them when an app on either asks.
- **A reused canvas id on the Windows game host answers with no world** (2026-10-25, LLP 1143 §4 D4). Two diaries kept a canvas id across leaving and re-entering a scene; `state <canvas>` replied without a world instead of refusing. Refuse a gone canvas by name and name the live world canvas. Not reproduced off Windows.
- **One app's `verify` reports 500 to 625 standing findings on every run** (2026-10-25, LLP 1143 open question 2; the Scores/foldable app's diaries, exact2 63914d6c4). Classify its standing set by kind and fix the `check` heuristics that misfire on its design. The ten diaries whose `verify ios` stayed red name six refusals of targets a native title view or bar replaced; each refusal should say so.
- **Film UIKit's presented frames on iOS** (2026-10-25, LLP 1143 §5; measured 2026-11-12, `experiments/ios-film-cadence/README.md`). `screenshot … over … every … window` films the agent clock's drawings, not UIKit's presentation: a sheet filmed under agent timing is unanimated, and a diagnostic run under platform timing missed 38 of 44 recorded sheet-edge positions. A capture of live display updates with presentation timestamps remains to be chosen, and the A→B→A candidate in the probe's recording to be explained, on a device with and without observation.
- **A drag's release velocity under the agent's frozen clock** (2026-10-25, LLP 1143 §5, the Bluesky port, 8 incidents). Every sample of a drag shares one time, so `transformrelease` reads 0 velocity even with `--timing platform`. It needs its own design: the agent's clock owns the runner's.
- **Windows host and game build friction from the diaries** (2026-10-25, LLP 1143 §5): window geometry lost on each rebuild and occluded-window screenshots (about 9 diaries); disk exhaustion with no view of the build caches' size or place (about 21); `test-rust` not saying which crates it covers or how far it is (about 12).
- **Plain-iframe apps load the whole partitioned browser on Apple** (2026-10-26, LLP 1020.002 stages 1–4). The web arm (`ExactWebArm`, `host/apple/build.mjs`; built only for an app with an `iframe` and dlopened at its first create commit) grew from about 164 KB to about 537 KB stripped with the partitioned browser, its windows, dialogs and downloads, capture, the PDFKit view and automation, and an app whose frames are all plain loads all of it. Building `FrameBrowser`, `FrameWindows`, `FrameCapture`, `FramePDF` and `FrameAutomation` (`host/apple/webarm/`) into a second arm loaded at a partitioned frame's or a PDF's first use would return those apps to the base size.
- **Watch: `InlineAtomProbeMacTests` failed one Mac suite run of two** (2026-10-25, 81c0f578c7, `bun host/apple/build.mjs --test excord-apple`). It passed in five runs since (the last a full suite at 65577096ed, 2026-10-27), two with Excord's archive and two with Caltrain's. It boots no plan, so the archive does not matter. 2026-10-30, the dev Mac (load 30 to 90): a full suite hung in it for 45 minutes (24 CPU-minutes in its `xctest`), until the process was killed. In the same run every `AccessibilityClientMacTests` case and `ComboboxAccessibilityClientMacTests` failed at the client's element lookup (`attributes(of:)` nil), as did one of them run alone on a bundle built 2026-10-23, so that is this machine's AX client, not a change.
- **JPEG, GIF and WebP on Linux and Windows** (2026-10-25, LLP 1011 §2, the remote fetch). The Linux presenter now downloads `http:` and `https:` images on Linux, Windows and Android, but on Linux and Windows decodes only PNG and SVG (`host/painted/src/image/png.rs`; `jpeg.rs` reads JPEG, GIF and WebP headers and hands the pixels to Android's `AImageDecoder`, refusing them elsewhere). No decoder for the three is in Cargo.lock; the candidates are pure-Rust `zune-jpeg`, `gif` and `image-webp` (first frame), or WIC on Windows. Excord on Windows is the consumer: its avatars, server icons and still emoji are PNG and draw, but attachments and embeds through Discord's media proxy are mostly JPEG or WebP, and an animated emoji is a `.gif`, so those are blank boxes. Until a decoder lands, Excord could ask the CDN for PNG where the host decodes nothing else (an animated emoji's still is `emojis/<id>.png`; whether the media proxy's `format` parameter gives PNG is unchecked).
- **Excord's GIF links and video attachments are still** (2026-10-25). A Tenor or Giphy link arrives as a `gifv` embed whose moving form is only an MP4 (`video.url`), and a video attachment is a file; Excord draws the first's still `thumbnail` and the second as a file row. Discord plays both in the row: a `gifv` muted, looping and without controls, a video with controls. Exact has `video` (LLP 1042; AVPlayer on Apple, the browser on the web; the Linux presenter's other backends draw nothing for it), so a still under the player would stand in where none plays. Before rows hold players, check on a phone that a muted, looping AVPlayer neither takes the audio session (pausing the reader's music) nor publishes to Now Playing (`NowPlaying.swift` leaves AVKit publishing when no player claims), and measure scrolling with several on screen.
- **A disk cache for remote images on the Linux presenter** (2026-10-25, LLP 1011 §2). Bodies are kept for the process only (an LRU of 256 spool files and 32 MiB, `host/painted/src/image/remote.rs`), so a relaunch fetches every avatar again; Apple keeps a 64 MiB `URLCache` on disk under the HTTP cache headers' policy, with `stale-while-revalidate`.
- **Contacts access picker is blank on iPhone 16 Pro, iOS 27.0.1** (2026-10-26, LLP 1133.020). With limited access, `showContactAccessPicker` opens an empty sheet. Waiting for the SwiftUI host to be presented does not resolve it. LLDB shows a 402×812 presentation with `_EXHostViewController` containing only `EXPlaceholderView`; ContactsUI is linked. Apple confirmed the same symptom as an OS bug in [FB21812568](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/814292), with reports on 27.0.1. An OS cause is likely, not independently proved here. Use the app’s Contacts settings to change shared contacts; picker completion remains unqualified on this phone.
- **Watch: document-render.test's two text-style cases time out under the full web-js suite at --parallel=3** (2026-10-25, dtool2, the lead's ask). "bare text styles keep their fills and inherited rows through server adoption" (at its 180 s cap) and "keyed text styles track independent shorthand and longhand fields" (at 60 s) failed together on one of two full runs on each of c9ec81b413 and a685b7b317. The file passes alone, 2/2 (23 tests). Each runs a server-rendered page and adoption through the agent, so it is load on the slow part, not a race found yet. Repro: `bun test --parallel=3 $(ls host/web-js/*.test.js host/web-js/*.test.mjs | grep -v "conform\|bundle-size\|document-agent") --timeout 900000` with contract and exact-web-js built (/tmp/dt-rv/clean-run.sh does it). If it fails alone, it is a main red.
- **Mac XCTest failures at 61ed608c** (2026-11-12, a full `build.mjs --test` on the dev Mac under load with its screen locked, the same 30 cases with and without 54073e7c): `PixelGridMacTests`, `FieldInkMacTests`, `HostBlockMacTests`, `ScrollOriginMacTests`, `TextAreaLeadingMacTests`, `ModuleQuitHoldTests`, `NativePropsTests`, one dark-mode case of `SheetSurfaceMacTests` and one of `InlineAtomEditorMacTests`, besides the AX client classes below; `InlineAtomProbeMacTests` hung again. The screen was locked during that run (`ioreg -n Root -d1 -a`: `CGSSessionScreenIsLocked`), which stops window activation, capture and full-screen transitions; rerun these classes in an unlocked session before suspecting a change.
- **macOS XCTests in the async lane** (2026-10-25, LLP 1142 D7, §12). `scripts/async.mjs` runs only `build.mjs --test --ios`, so a failing Mac class is never reported. The Mac suite takes 6 min with its compile on an M5 Max (279 classes). Add it beside the iOS step under `appleTouched`, with its name in `retainSkippedFailures`, once it passes on main. At `65577096ed` (2026-10-27) two classes failed; `InlineAtomProbeMacTests` and `TableFeedKeyMacTests` passed. (1) `NativeButtonFidelityMacTests.testAllFixtureRowsPixelDiffAgainstHandConfiguredAppKit`, 0.028 against 0.02 for `a-plain`, in every run. Its text is identical. The difference is the `arrow.up` symbol: both buttons draw it as an SF Symbol (`NSSymbolImageRep`), with the same symbol configuration (13 pt, regular), `labelColor` tint, aqua appearance, bezel and border, and their stems match. But the native arrowhead is about a pixel wider on each side and starts a row higher (`target/1104-b3/pixels/a-plain-{native,hand}.png`). Find what else the native face sets on the image or the cell (`ButtonConfigurationMac`). (2) `BorderParityMacTests.testEveryCaseMatchesChromeOnScreen`, whose failing case changes between runs: `current light` mean 3.02 with 2.8% of pixels beyond 48, then `square4 light` mean 4.12 with 4.5%, against a band of 3.0 and 4%. It reads the window server's picture, and other apps' windows were in front during these runs.
- **A pull that starts at a Mac scroller's end bands nowhere** (2026-10-27, LLP 1142 D3). `tap <s> wheel 0 3000 hold` from the middle of Caltrain's page stretches AppKit's band past the end. From the end itself the gesture chains to the scroller around (`ChainingScrollView.routing`'s "a gesture that begins at this view's end goes to the scroller around it"), and the outermost has none, so a person's pull there does not band either. Safari's page does. Decide whether the outermost scroller (or one with nothing around it) keeps such a gesture, and judge it against a hand-built AppKit scroll view.
- **Watch: press.test's chromium "a native button eases out of its press as the press ends" fails at high load** (2026-10-24, main 768cae6767 and after). `bun test host/web/tests/press.test.mjs` failed it 1 run in 6 at load ~37, and in every run at 80–157, on main's own neutral.css as on branches: `expect([engine, finger, dim]).toEqual([engine, finger, [0.65, true, true]])` (press.test.mjs:550) got `false` for the last, the fade's opacity at `duration - 1` not above 0.95. At load under ~20 it passes. Likely the box's fade (the 0.2 s ease-out after a ≥0.1 s hold) is read after a click whose events and frames a loaded machine spreads out, not a product fault; when it fails at low load, read where the fade's `currentTime` stood.
- **iOS: a queued hatch focus cannot keep the keyboard through native Back.** `ExactElement.focus()` queues through `ElementHatches.later`, so a claim from `willShow` arrives after UIKit starts hiding the outgoing keyboard. Duo Lab now labels and exercises a synchronous native field claim in `animate(alongsideTransition:)`, which keeps the keyboard visible through completed and cancelled swipes. The queued hatch API has no synchronous transition handoff. Keep its existing batch-safety contract; any transition-aware delivery needs its own regression before changing it.

- **Foreground services and calls: what stages 0 to 4 left** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.033 §12). Built: the Android foreground service and its claim, the recorder's background line on iOS and Android with Android's crash-safe part writer, the runner's kept call list handed to a bound implementation, CallKit on iOS with the call claim and voice processing, Core-Telecom on Android with its `CallStyle` notification (compiled; `packages/calls/test/android.mjs`), and the web's Media Session call actions. Not built: from D9 and D10, the hold gate on a held call's tracks, pausing its players, holding the first call when a second is answered, the interruption-end activation in the call's configuration; on Android, the microphone type added at a resume, a second call's notification, a lock-screen activity, mute through Telecom, Bluetooth endpoints; the cost probes; the recording notification's Paused text and its hatch (D4). On the iOS 27 simulator CallKit ends a test call about 60 ms after starting it, with no `didActivate`: drive 12 on an iPhone says whether the simulator or the module does it. Not run: anything on Android (drives 1 to 3, the fixture's 4 to 9 with `--bind calls`, on an emulator that boots), drive 10, and `packages/recording/test/device.md` items 4 and 10 and drives 11 to 13 on phones.
- **Background work: what is left** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.017 §14). Not built: Android's headless session (a worker with no activity in its process finds no session: a `sync` retries, a `periodicsync` waits), the host's `ExactWakeWorker` and the `android.` registration words; `@exact/background-transfer` on Android (D4's user-initiated job and worker, headers in the secret store); D10's staging before the first unlock; the cost probes and the owed update check riding the refresh request (D8, D18); the transfer's runner substitute (§10: `pending`, `fail fetch`, progress overlays, `--wake` with a finished transfer), for which the interface is its own substitute today; Harmony's photo upload (§11 drives 5 to 7), which needs a photo column and Snapback's upload route; stage 4. On an iPhone: `packages/background/test/checklist.md` step 5 (the task's expiry, now wired to the wake), the notification's look and tap, and `packages/background-transfer/test/checklist.md`.
- **Harmony's mention switch asks for notification permission; not yet tried on an iPhone** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.004 stage 1). Turning on Notify Me of Mentions now sends `requestNotificationPermission()` from `@exact/notifications` before it registers `catch-up` (on 2026-10-26 it asked nothing, and the device test granted authorization through LLDB). Test it on an iPhone from undetermined authorization: the prompt at the switch, then a catch-up's notification opening the room.
- **Harmony in Firefox loses a message written offline** (2026-10-24, `bun packages/background/test/harmony.mjs firefox`). Under Playwright's offline switch the message shows pending, no sync round fails (so no `outbox:` tag is registered), and it is not delivered within 30 s of the network's return; Chromium and WebKit deliver it, Firefox online delivers it, and under the agent in Firefox (`EXACT_WEB_BROWSER=firefox`, failing fetches) every drive passes. Look at Snapback's web device's round while `navigator.onLine` is false in Firefox.
- **Calendar’s first live store updates were stale on an iPhone** (2026-10-26, iPhone 16 Pro, iOS 27.0.1). After the first full-access grant, events added or removed in Apple Calendar appeared only after toggling Show my calendars or changing dates. Following permission revocation, restoration and an app restart, another addition updated automatically; its trace shows EventKit announcements and both readers refreshing. A separately installed copy with fresh authorization subsequently updates both an external addition and deletion without toggling or changing dates. The initial failure is not reproduced; no cause or fix is established.
- **Contacts and calendar: what the four stages left** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.018 §14). Built and driven: the substitute, the web (drive 7 in three engines), Contacts and EventKit on an iOS simulator and macOS. Built, not run: Android (`packages/contacts/test/android.mjs` checks the fixture's APK; its `--emulator` drives wait for an emulator that starts, which this Mac's does not), and the two checklists (`packages/*/test/checklist.md`), which need a person and the phones of LLP 1133.020 row 9. Not built: the button view `exact-contact-access-button` (an implementation's own names and views are not read yet), iPadOS's popover, D13 item 1's anchor, D16's cost probe, sizes and first-answer time.
- **The web's agent keeps no app storage between launches** (2026-10-24, found driving the Calendar). `agent.mjs web --app calendar --storage <name>` with the same name, with or without `--port`, opens a calendar without the item the last launch saved, though `agent-playwright.mjs` says Chrome keeps a named store between drives; so LLP 1133.018 drive 3's relaunch runs on native hosts only. Find where the page's SQLite store is lost (the profile, the origin, or the dev server's storage).
- **Photos by a person, and what the simulator cannot show** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.019 §12 stage 2). PhotoKit is built and driven on an iOS simulator (`packages/photos/test/apple.mjs`). Not driven: the system's prompt and the limited selection with Manage (`simctl privacy` grants full access only), a Mac granted by a person (and which text its prompt shows, D4), iCloud downloads, an HDR save's gain map, and a photo added from outside while the app runs (`simctl addmedia` does not return while the driven app runs). Drive 9 is `packages/photos/test/checklist.md`.
- **An HDR edit of a 24-megapixel photo falls back to SDR on Apple** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.019 stage 0). The HDR path of `data/src/image/apple_transform.rs` charges its decode twice (the half-float source and the half-float output) against the codec's 512 MiB scratch budget, so a 24 MP Ultra HDR or iPhone HDR photo is written SDR with `hdr: false`. Measure what ImageIO and the context actually hold for such a photo on an iPhone, then charge that, or draw the output in bands.
- **Check an HDR edit's gain map on an iPhone** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.019 stage 0). The iOS simulator's ImageIO writes no gain map at all (`writes_gain_maps()` in `data/src/image/apple_transform_tests.rs` probes for it), so `transformImage` of an HDR photo there returns `hdr: false`; macOS writes one and the test reads it back. Run `cargo test -p exact-data image` on a phone, or edit an HDR photo in the photo editor and open the result in Photos.
- **Apple's HDR edit has no Core Image tone-map fallback** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.019 D16). When the gain-map encode fails, the codec retries SDR through ImageIO's own decode, which tone-maps; LLP 1100 D13's Core Image step is not built. Add it if a drive shows ImageIO's SDR decode of an HDR source clipping.
- **Photos on Android: what the emulator drive leaves** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.019 stage 3). `bun packages/photos/test/android.mjs --emulator` passes on API 29, 33 and 34. Left for a person (`packages/photos/test/checklist.md`'s Android part): the permission dialogs, "Select photos" and Manage, a selection's expiry, the resume re-read after Settings, the `owned` path under the add line alone (a build with other grants), `locationRedacted` true, and whether `ACCESS_MEDIA_LOCATION` shows a dialog of its own (D4); an Ultra HDR photo's gain map through a quarter turn on an API 34 phone (the repo's ISO 21496-1 fixture decodes with no gain map there). Under `--bind`, a read decided at launch waits on the runner's agent ticket, since modules bind after first pixel; binding before the first read would let a drive skip the `tap @recent allow`.

- **Watch stage 1b and 1c, after the live driver landed** (2026-10-24, LLP 1126.002 §4; PR #881's "Left for 1b"). The host says it draws `time` and `progress` live, so the runner sets no `due` for them (a countdown wakes the runner each second besides SwiftUI's own redraw); D9's runtime log line, which now also has to cover what the bake leaves to the runtime (computed or design-token paint values, a computed image source); the bordered button and `List`, control measurement, the face's parts, the whole screen and insets with `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, the reader's other settings, the `time` width, `scroll` and `scrollend`; then the per-node model and the startup budget (1c).
- **Watch: two driver nits left from the stage 1a reviews** (2026-10-24, PR #881). A pressable node inside another pressable one is a `Button` in a `Button`, so the inner never gets the tap; the early boot always passes `.dark` and nothing calls `setPreferences` when `colorScheme` changes (moot while watchOS is dark-only). A three-round attempt (2026-11-12) put the outer pressable's `Button` behind its content in `host/apple/Sources/ExactSnapshot/LiveDriver.swift`, and its reviews found the hit testing that follows: a tap on the outer's text, image or path icon reaches no button; a painted box laid over an inner control no longer takes the hit; and gathering the outer's label walked `display: none` subtrees. Hit testing has to be decided per leaf (what takes the outer's action, what passes through), as UIKit's `hitTest` does, before the nesting is changed.
- **Android reader: a module source that failed before the extensions were bound is not asked again** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.008 drive on a Pixel 5). The camera fixture's `devices = enumerateDevices()` fails `NotSupportedError` at launch and stays failed, so it counts no cameras: the activity's `Native.bindExtensions` is applied at the host's first `requests()` (`host/painted/src/canvas/requests.rs`), after boot and `data_ready` have asked through the substitute, and `bind_extensions`' announcements re-ask only `cameraStatus` (the log: "modules bound here", then "changed (1 asked again)"). A runner test that boots unbound, calls `data_ready`, then binds re-asks it, so the difference is in the reader's boot (a baked plan, a data source not ready at boot). Bind before the first data activation, or have binding re-ask every reader of a newly routed module.
- **Android reader under the agent: a `clock` step waits out its 20 s bound while a bound module is polled** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.009 drive on a Pixel 5). With `--bind recording --bind media-capture` and a take running, each `clock +N real` and `clock settle` took about 20 s and `clock settle` reported not settled: the agent's op runs on exact2's thread (`Owner.kt` `o.sync`), so the Kotlin requests the clock waits for (`host/painted/src/agent.rs` `wait_for_polls`, `SETTLE_BOUND`) cannot be dispatched or answered until it returns. Let the agent's wait turn the reader's request queue, or wait off that thread.
- **Android reader: a force-stop leaves the permission dialog in the app's task** (2026-10-24). The reader carrier force-stops the app before each drive (`scripts/agent-android.mjs`); a `GrantPermissionsActivity` the last run left up stayed in the recorder's task and covered the next launch (removed with `cmd activity stack remove`, unanswered). Stop the app's task too, or say that a permission dialog is up when the drive starts.
- **Deploy the auth fixture's public origin, then drive passkeys for real** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.011 §11 stages 2 and 3; §13 question 4). The domain is approved (`exact-auth-fixture.expo.app`) and `bun apps/auth-fixture/origin.mjs` deploys and checks it; it waits on Charlie running that, as an agent's session refuses a production deploy. Then: build with `EXACT_BACKEND_ORIGIN=https://exact-auth-fixture.expo.app`, run drives 1 to 6 with the real sheet on the iOS simulator and an Android emulator, record the Apple origin in D6, and run `packages/passkeys/test/checklist.md` on an iPhone, a Mac with a profile and an Android phone.

- **Sign-in's native requests and seams** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.014 §11 stages 2 and 3; D10 items 2 to 4). The substitute, web, and standalone Apple identity request are built; still to build: the per-session credential request table in `ExactPackage` (and its Android twin) through which `SignIn.swift` puts the Apple ID request in passkeys' controller, the native auth session for an implementation (Google on Apple, with the token exchange and its local stub), a data-code call's lifetime (a superseded sign-in's sheet stays up until answered), `getCredentialState`, the hatches, Android (`SignIn.kt`, `googleid` in the locked graph), and passkeys' conformance tests for members. Then drives 9 and 10 with the clients Charlie creates (§13 question 6), which also record Apple's web `nonce` claim (D1).

- **Investigate the intermittent collection animation completion timeout**. `SmoothCollectionIOSTests.testAStoppedAnimationsEndLeavesTheNextOneRunning` exceeded its two-second wait for `animating` to clear in a full UIKit run (`/tmp/duo-back/safe-area-ios-green.log`). The isolated test and complete bare suite rerun passed unchanged. Capture the animation serial, offset driver and `FrameClock` callbacks if it recurs; the cause and baseline behavior remain unproved.

- **Watch: a Duo swipe injection finished without a recorded touch beginning** (2026-10-23, Back qualification). A content swipe reported `the runner finished and no touch had begun` after its native injection completed (`/tmp/duo-back/gestures-both-smoke.log`). Later edge and content drives passed, including explicit keyboard restoration after cancellation. The driver now rejects missing, ambiguous or changed display mappings, fixing separately reproduced wrong-display input. This swipe's cause remains unproved. If it recurs, the failure (`no touch has begun: {…}`) carries the aim, the injection result and the dispatch log; also capture the keyboard state and navigation transition before starting another gesture.

- **Watch: TableFeedKeyMacTests' held click showed no press, once in a full Mac run** (2026-10-24; dtool2's /tmp/dt-rv/latecl-mac4.out, 2026-10-23 ~11:00Z). `testAuthoredPressedPaintFollowsTheTablesClickWithoutReplacingSelection` failed its five asserts inside the click's `held` closure (`row.pressed`, `pressPaint.down`, the selection paint suppressed), 50 ms into the click; after the release everything passed (AppKit selected the row, the press action ran). Not a late release (reload1's PressPaint fix): the click holds longer than `PressFeedback.unseen`, so no release is held. Two hypotheses, neither reproduced (25/25 alone at load 11–12, the class 6/6): (1) `TableHost.pointer`'s guards missed at the mouse-down, the row not yet mounted when the fixture's `settle` returned (`feed.pressing(row)` nil); (2) another session's local event monitor, left in the shared test process, took the mouse-down first. The asserts now say which guard misses at the held moment, or that all pass then (which leaves the cause open: a guard that has recovered, hypothesis 2, or a release taken early); when it fails again, read that message and fix where it points.
- **The icon-path guard's JS scan is a tripwire with a limit** (2026-10-24, LLP 1121 stage 14 C2; theme2's ruling on Astra's round-5 finding 3). `contract/cli/tests/it/icon_path_readers.rs` scans only the JS/MJS/TS files that name a symbol source (`data-symbol`, `symbolSource`, `symbolPath`, `symbol-source`, a quoted `symbol:` or `icon|`; 10 files, tests aside), so a reader in a file that names none passes: `node.props.imageSource.split("|")` in an unselected file is not caught. The enforcement is Rust's `IconPathId` type and `iconForm` (host/web-js/symbols.js) as the one JS reader, held to `Icon::parse`'s cases. Option: widen the selection to every scanned JS file; the cost is allowlisting the 388 lines tree-wide that hold a bar or colon literal today (11 now, 8 of them on main).
- **Bound an SVG filter's island on Linux as Apple does, and charge composited layers** (2026-10-23, LLP 1011.001 D2). Linux sizes a filter's island to the filter region, up to 4,096 pixels a side, however little of it shows (`host/painted/src/paint/island.rs` `svg_filtered`); Apple caps a filter's work at 128 MiB by drawing the island smaller (`SvgIsland.budget`), and Chrome works only over what shows. One drop shadow whose region is far past the drawing costs Linux about 2.2 GB at 3× (`exact-svg-raster`'s `f32` working images; measured 845 MB for one blur at 4,096², 0.34 s), so the scene resolver's surface limit is 2.5 GiB to keep `apps/svg-gallery`'s `fx-huge-region` drawing. Linux with Apple's budget, or islands sized to what shows, would let it drop by an order of magnitude. The layers a host composites for nested opacity, blending and clips are not charged at all: 120 nested `<g opacity>` in a 2,048-pixel picture took Linux 1,087 MB and 1.3 s; a budget for them belongs in each host's painter, where their sizes are known. The same holds for three things the resolver's estimate misses (Astra, round 3): what draws inside an island the host rounds up (a pattern tile under a pixel magnifies its content, filters included); Apple's CPU morphology over a long, thin island its area cap admits (the resolver caps a radius at Linux's 4,096-pixel side); and `SvgIsland.extent`, which clamps each reduced side to one pixel and so can pass its own 16,777,216-pixel cap.
- **macOS: two cases where AppKit's popover keeps its opening gap when its margin changes while open** (2026-10-23, theme2 22:25, stated limits of the live-margin fix). (1) A toolbar item's popover: AppKit anchors it to the item (`show(relativeTo: item)`, `PopoversMac.swift`), and the host takes that anchor only while the popover's margins are zero (`usesToolbarAnchor`). Repro: a manual popover opened from a toolbar item with no margin, then `margin-top=12` set while it's open. The popover stays where it opened, since an item anchor has no rectangle to move short of closing and reopening it. (2) An unflipped source view: the gap toward the invoker applies only when the view it's shown against is flipped (`NativePopover.positioning`), as it did at opening before. A node is always flipped, so only a window-content anchor that isn't a node reaches this. A three-round attempt (2026-11-12, `PopoversMac.swift`) reapplied the gap while open; its reviews found where it still fails: a trailing toolbar button's popover opened through `showPopover` falls back to an anchor outside the visible rect, which AppKit refuses to show; a toolbar popover's cached gap is not reapplied when resizing the window moves its item; and a flipped node opened with every margin 0 does not move when `margin-top` changes while open. Seen there: `ToolbarMac.swift:426`'s comment calls the viewport's bottom edge its top.
- **iOS: a painted popover's glass blurs a little less than UIKit's own popover** (2026-10-23, Harmony revision 11's Pins; theme2 20:05, and the bounded probe of 20:06). Measured on real display pixels (`simctl io screenshot`, iPhone 18 Pro simulator, iOS 27) over 42 lines of busy text, luminance mean / sd inside the panel: the native UIPopoverPresentationController is 238.5 / 2.5 light and 65.6 / 4.8 dark; Exact's default surface (UIGlassEffect .regular) is 238.2 / 3.0 and 65.3 / 5.7, the same brightness with about 20% more of the text behind showing through. Every public route was tried against a band of 2 levels and 10% of the sd: SwiftUI's `glassEffect` (.regular, .regular.interactive(): the same numbers as UIKit's; .clear: 226.9 / 35.4); UIGlassEffect's isInteractive (no change at rest) and .clear (224.1 / 36.7); a full UIBlurEffect material under the glass (ultraThin 239.5 / 0.5 and 76.9 / 0.9, thin, systemMaterial, thick: the text erased, dark's mean moved) or alone (thin 229.0 / 0.6 and 46.4 / 0.6); a glass tint (systemBackground at 25%: 247.5 / 48.9). Only a thin blur at alpha 0.1–0.2 under the glass fits (0.2: 239.3 / 2.4 and 66.3 / 4.6), and Apple's guidance is to avoid an effect view's alpha below 1 (it may render wrongly in snapshots and animation). The native surface is a private SwiftUI GlassPopoverView (`_UIPopoverGlassBackground`) with no public knob. Hosting a real UIPopoverPresentationController matches by definition; D2's painted path doesn't take it, because the painted top layer is the host's own: its placement (`position-area`, `PositionArea.swift`), its nested popovers' stack, its light dismiss and the frames it reports to the runner (`openTopLayer`) are the host's, the same on iOS and the Mac, and a UIKit popover would take placement and dismissal from them.
- **The Bluesky clone's symbol icons lose their tints when it moves its exact2 pin** (2026-10-23, found checking x2/path-icons' Mac tint). Since 130e009940 an Apple `symbol:` image draws in its computed `color` and ignores `-exact-tint-color` (`docs/agent-pitfalls.md`). The clone at `~/.tuft/projects/bluesky-exact2-v2` writes `-exact-tint-color` 136 times, many on `symbol:` images (`white_100`, `primary_500`, `contrast_700`, … in `drawer.contract`, `graph.contract`), with no `color`. It builds against `~/.tuft/projects/exact2-bsky` at 02dd4316e2 (2026-10-18), which predates 130e009940, so its builds are right today; on a newer exact2 those icons draw in the text colour. Before bumping the pin, migrate each to `color=`. The lint theme2 ruled (a warning for `-exact-tint-color` on a `symbol:` image, naming `color=`) will flag them all.
- **The agent's `fail fetch` should match a long poll's individual requests** (2026-10-23, LLP 1016.000 D8; theme2's ruling Q4 of 2026-10-22). A fault armed for a URL is taken only by a request that isn't a stream (`host/web/http-body.js` `takeFault`; `runner/src/runner/faults.rs` `fault_dispatch` on the other hosts, LLP 1103 D5), so a long poll's backoff and `retrying` can't be driven end to end from the agent: the tests use stand-ins (`host/executor/src/longpoll_tests.rs`, `host/web/http-body.test.mjs`, `host/web-js/longpoll.test.mjs`). Let each of a long poll's asks take a matching fault as a network failure, then test a backoff and its reset through `scripts/agent.mjs`.
- **A design's token count is not capped** (2026-10-23, theme2's Q3 ruling on the checker bound). The checker bounds each value (`tamber-value-size`, `tamber-value-depth`) and each length's residual (`tamber-length-size`), but each `set-token`/`add-token` may add a token, and a check is linear in the design (but for the three walks of the PRIORITY below), so a paired client can grow the design write by write. The rate is the request limits' to bound: R3's relay limits and the serve doors' (`tamber_serve.rs` LINE_LIMIT, `tamber-server.mjs` BODY_LIMIT); a count cap comes only if those prove short.
- **Re-import's per-dial checks are dials x check** (2026-10-23, what stays of the PRIORITY "checker work in token, param and dial counts" once the rest landed; theme2's ruling (5)). `export_dials.rs` `dials` and `marks` apply each dial change and check the design again, so a re-import's cost is the changed dials times a check. Dials grow only on disk (no operation adds one), so D is the developer's own design; charging the budget would refuse large legitimate designs. Fold all the dials' changes once and diff per dial, if a real design shows the cost.
- **Dial influence on overlapping closures is dials x dependents** (2026-10-26, Grok's round 1 on the counts). Each dial's probes refold the tokens that read it or name one that does; where those closures overlap (token k reading dial k and naming token k-1) the work is the sum of the closures, about n^2/2 folds: 500 dials and tokens 1.5 s, 1,000 6.7 s, 2,000 50 s in a release build, times the tuples. The answer itself (each token's dials) can be that large. Dials grow only on disk: no client operation adds one (`tamber/tests/it/limits_counts.rs` no_client_operation_adds_a_dial; a `restore` is no client's to send, `tamber_ops.rs` parse_ops), and a design asked about again unchanged is answered from the last few checked (`tamber/src/checked.rs`), so a client can't make the server pay it per request. The clone has 0 dials and Harmony 0 (the clone's `contract tamber check` about 2 s, no influence to speak of). Pick it up when a real design passes about 500 overlapping dials, or when any client path adds dials. An incremental influence (each token's dials from its names' dials, probing only where a read could be masked) is its own design.
- **A batch's blame rounds re-check the whole design** (2026-10-23, theme2's ruling (4) on the PRIORITY). Each round of `export_batch.rs` `take` is a full check, charged to the re-import's budget, so a chain of refusals each exposing the next is refused in the budget's words (nothing applied). An incremental re-check of only the reverted tokens' dependents would answer such a chain exactly and in bounded time: its own design (an incremental check API) and review.
- **A pull to refresh inserting two rows above 330 still makes three frames late on iOS** (2026-10-26, r22 and r24 inbox). r24's inbox, a plain `list` of 330 swipe rows whose `refreshing` ends a frame or two after the pull, had UIKit's control fire again in the same held pull (up to seven inserts a pull, 8 to 11 late frames); a control now ends only when the finger lifts (`ScrollViewIOS.swift`), and a row the insert only moved keeps its swipe row unresolved (`SwipeActionsHost.sync`'s `reshaped`). What is left, three late frames a pull (load 50 to 110; four before 2026-10-27): the large title's scroller no longer reports the pull's stretch of its safe area as a cover (`NavigationHost.stretched`: each frame of a pull had laid the page out again and synced the bar, 5 to 20 ms each, and left the inset 60 pt too large after it), and the text scans visit only what is near each scroller (`TextIndexIOS.swift`). The insert's frame is still 45 to 60 ms of main thread: the batch arrives inside UIKit's refresh event, beside the control's own state change (`_snappingMagic`, ~18 ms); `NavigationHost.sync`'s layout of the bar for the changed subtitle and Refresh enabled again (~20 ms, all UIKit's: `_UIButtonBarButton` configuration and the bar's SwiftUI); the rows' frames ~2 ms. A frame next to it is late with the main thread idle; it is not the moved rows' commit: with the moves of rows two ports away skipped (an experiment) it stays, and the insert's frame was ~10 ms shorter. Earlier, on r22's grouped list: Longest frame per pull ~50 ms, the main thread's own: of ~27 ms in `Presenter.apply` for the insert (Time Profiler, load 60-90), the navigation sync's layout of the bar after Refresh is enabled again ~5 (UIKit reconfiguring the bar button), the pixel grid's walk of each moved row's subtree ~3 (every row has a `frame` op; its children keep their drawn frames, only their absolute origins move), UIKit's diffable apply ~3, `focusedNode` asking all ~5,000 views whether each is first responder ~1, `ContextMenuHost.sync` reading `contextPopover` of every row ~1. A row's `y` moving under an insert reaches the host as 330 `frame` ops; a grid kept relative to the parent, or origins brought up to date when read, would make the walk lazy. Measure with the feel probe's `pull-top`, or the feel recorder over a few pulls with Time Profiler attached. r27's inbox (a grouped list, `refreshing` while a write is pending): one to four late frames a pull, 60 to 135 ms late, the same in r27's build and after `scroll-r27`, nearly all main queue (the insert's batch) and UIKit.
- **A grouped list builds its rows off the screen** (2026-10-23, LLP 1141.001 D13). Hackers' feed (60 posts in a `list appearance="auto"`) keeps 47 rows' views hidden at rest: 1,005 of its 1,562 layers; the D13 fixture grows 5.9 MB from 12 rows to 60. LLP 1084 D1 builds every row of a grouped list and leaves a long feed to a virtualized collection, which UIKit does not draw as cells, so a builder wanting native rows takes the grouped list. Deciding it amends D1: build a custom row's views only near its cell, or draw a virtualized collection's rows as cells. The swipe actions' buttons are built hidden at rest too, 3 layers a row, and could be built when a swipe projects them.
- **Reproduce: a Day 2 tap that did not take** (2026-10-23, LLP 1141.001 D12; GPT's React Conf Exact port, `~/ports/react-conf/runs/1g-exact`, light and dark at the largest size). Tapping the day picker's "Day 2" left Day 1's list and the large title showing; the Opus build of the same brief took the tap. Drive with the port's journey (`~/ports/react-conf/flows/journey.yaml` step 4) and read `logs`. Checked 2026-10-23 on a scratch day picker (a `radiogroup` iOS draws as a segmented control, sticky under a large title): a tap takes at rest and once pinned; one made while the scroller is still decelerating only stops it, as UIKit's does. The port's picker scrolled away under the bar until d69a50fd8e, which would have put its tap elsewhere; drive the journey again on current main.
- **Reproduce: the device's zone prints "GMT (Local)" in an Exact app on iOS** (2026-10-23, LLP 1141.001 D12; GPT's React Conf Exact port), where SwiftUI prints "GMT-07:00 (Local)" on the same simulator. Either `Intl`'s long or offset zone name on Apple hosts or the device zone the runtime reads. Read 2026-10-23: under the driver the session's zone is `EXACT_AGENT_TIME_ZONE`, else UTC (`SessionLaunch.swift`), for deterministic drives; outside it, the device's (`TimeZone.current`). So a driven Exact build prints GMT where a SwiftUI build on the same simulator prints the device's. A grader's journey that compares them should set `EXACT_AGENT_TIME_ZONE` to the simulator's zone; not a tell a person sees.
- **A release drive that checks a masked SVG image's pixels** (2026-10-23, LLP 1141.001 D1's lasting check). The bake now lists the SVG island module for a bundled `.svg` with a `mask` or `filter` (`asset_islands`, `bake/src/receipt.rs`), and the unit test covers the receipt. Nothing yet drives a release build that shows one, so a regression in the host's loader or the bundle's layout would show only in a port. Verified once by hand: `apps/hello` with React Conf's `gold-revenue-cat.svg` and `gold-redwood.svg`, `build.mjs --ios hello --release`, `Frameworks/libexact_svg.dylib`, both logos drawn. Needs a human's go-ahead (a new check): an app under `apps/` with such an image and a `smoke.mjs` step.
- **An app's iOS and macOS builds rebuild each other's JS host crates** (2026-10-23, found measuring LLP 1141.001 D2). `host/apple/build.mjs` gives an iOS build's cargo `HOST_CXXFLAGS` (`-isysroot` the Mac SDK, ~713) and a macOS build none, and the build scripts of `exact-js` and `ibex2` record it (Cargo: `EnvVarChanged { name: "HOST_CXXFLAGS" }`). Both builds share `target/host-dev`'s host units, so each build after the other compiles `ibex2`, `ibex2-sqlite`, `exact-js`, `exact-js-bake` and the app crate again: Weatherlight `--ios --no-install` after a macOS build, cargo 20.1 s against 4.5 s. Giving the macOS build the same value would end it.
- **`ElementScrollTests` (macOS) fails when the display is asleep** (2026-10-23, dtool2's overnight report). On clean `8ba85e2428` with the display awake it passes 9 of 9 alone and in the full run. `testUnsaidAndAutoRevealUseAppKitAnimation` asserts that even a hand-built `NSScrollView`'s `animator()` scroll has intermediate frames, and AppKit's display-linked animation can land at once with no display to drive it: an environment flake, not a main red. Repro: the class alone (`macTests`' class list filtered to `ExactKitTests.ElementScrollTests` in a scratch edit) with the display asleep; rerun with it awake (`caffeinate -u -t 600`) before reporting it.
- **Excord's Android emulator lane** (2026-10-23). The APK builds on expo-build-1000 (`. ~/opt/excord-android.env`, then `bun scripts/agent-android.mjs build excord --apk`), and on its x86_64 emulator the app reached the fixture's READY and op 11, but only with three scratch edits (`~/opt/excord-android-emulator-scratch.diff` there): that machine has no KVM (`sudo usermod -aG kvm <user>` would give it), so the emulator runs unaccelerated and translates the arm64 library, which then never sees the `EXACT_*` variables the reader sets with `Os.setenv` (a JNI `setenv` copy fixes it), the agent's 20 s ready wait in `scripts/agent-stdio.mjs` is too short, and the data module's turns run 20 times over the 100 ms budget. With KVM, the `setenv` copy and a longer ready wait may be all a drive needs.
- **Android's TLS trusts the compiled-in roots, not the phone's** (2026-10-23). ibex2's rustls transports (`websocket.rs`, `rustls_http.rs`) ask `rustls-native-certs`, which on Android looks only where `openssl-probe` does and finds nothing, so they fall back to `webpki-roots`: public sites verify, but a user or enterprise CA and Android's own root updates are ignored. `rustls-platform-verifier` asks Android's trust manager.
- **Excord's "a send while the list is still moving lands at the live end" fails now and then in the whole web run** (2026-10-25): once in about eight runs of `agent.mjs web --app excord --test`, `anchor` is still a history id after the send; alone it passed five of five. It drags with a real 60 ms fling (`tap "transcript" drag 0 500 over 60`), so the scroll's settling time races the send under the suite's load.
- **Excord's "paging continues while the pin request is out" test fails about half its runs on the web** (2026-10-23, also on main before Excord's sign-in): `win.firstId` stops a page short. Run it alone six times to see it.
- **Desktop processes, after stage 1** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.007 §17). (1) A Rust source's `fs.flock` is refused: its storage has no runtime to hold a lock (`data/host/src/native.rs`); give it an owner whose end releases it when a consumer needs one. (2) Linux's `fork.rs` path (death signal, `close_range`, `/proc` members) is compiled, not run: drive the fixture on a Linux machine before Linux leaves provisional. (3) D5's Nushell form is untested. (4) No agent operation swaps a native app's plan, so a plan swap with a live child is covered by `host/executor/src/process_real_tests.rs` alone. (5) Stage 2, the secret field.
- **Desktop processes: a leader's pid can be reused while its group is signalled** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.007 D9, review of #968). The waiter reaps the leader with `waitpid` as soon as it exits (`host/executor/src/process_real.rs`), and release, `kill` and the escalation then signal the group by that id; if every member has ended, the id can name a new group. Waiting with `WNOWAIT` would keep the zombie holding the id until the supervisor is done, but `group_alive` then sees the zombie as a member, so the escalation and the drain need another test of "ended".
- **Desktop processes: a tool can write a program where `process.spawn` runs one** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.007 D13, a design question). A tool's `writeFile`, `chmod` or `symlink` into a directory a `process.spawn` line covers is not held to the person rule, so it can change what a person's later spawn runs. Decide in D13 whether such writes need the app's origin.
- **Desktop processes: after a crash, a leaderless group in a shared session survives** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.007 §10 stage 1, r2 #1, unmet there). With the leader gone, the next launch ends a recorded group only when the app led its session; a Mac app launchd starts is in session 1, and one started from a terminal shares the shell's, so a grandchild that outlived its leader is left. Meeting it needs a mark on each member the next launch can read: start each child in a session of its own (`setsid`, which loses the terminal for a CLI child) and record that session, or record every member's pid and start time as it appears (a per-group watch), or on Linux a cgroup per child.
- **`__exact_gate_decode` goes when `VideoDecoder` comes** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.003 §15 stage 0). The throughput gate's stub decode is a global under the agent only, natively (`js/src/binary.js`, installed by `js/src/prelude.js`; bytes door op 8, `js/src/crypto.rs`) and on the web JS target (`host/web-js/agent.js`), called by `apps/stream-fixture/app.ts`. Stage 1 (the web) and stage 2 (Apple) replace it with `@exact/webcodecs`'s `VideoDecoder.decode`; delete the global, op 8 and the fixture's declaration then, and point the fixture and `gate.mjs` at the module's names. Until then a native production binary launched with `EXACT_AGENT=1` has the stub too: the runtime knows the agent flag, not the build's trust.
- **Web: a narrow sheet bar keeps both long names, where iOS folds the second into "•••"** (2026-10-22, LLP 1116.020 D2). On iOS 27, a bar whose items don't fit keeps the first whole, folds the next into a "•••" menu item and hides the title (measured, two 23-character titles on a 402-point bar). The web ends each word in an ellipsis. Its title is one clipped line since 2026-10-23 (`neutral.css`), so with no room it is hidden as UIKit's is, instead of spilling over the button beside it. Whether the web folds a bar's items into a menu is LLP 1116.020's call.
- **Android: library-gate's slot-rendered once missed two markers' glyph change** (2026-10-22, the s13c gate's stage 12 rerun, theme2 23:03). sample-cf-near and sample-bi-dot read "its pixels are the same with both glyphs" (26 of 28) on the x2-android emulator (swiftshader GPU, headless, under the gate's other hosts' drives and a release-identity build); run alone right after, 28 of 28, and the s13c profile's run of the same cells 28 of 28. Likely the marker's tap landing before the second shot; a wait for the glyph's re-render before the shot would end it.
- **The top layer as a computed-style default, (B)** (2026-10-22, theme2 10:42). How a box is positioned for layout is one accessor today: `style::top_layer::layout_position` in the kernel (`of` over rows and props), `NodeRef::layout_position` on the Rust hosts, `NodeView.layoutPosition` in Swift, with `scripts/position-reads.test.mjs` holding every layout read to it. (B) would make the rule part of the computed style instead: a structural UA default in `arena/defaults.rs` (`structural_starts`) giving a popover `position: absolute` unless its author wrote one, so `effective_style` and what the kernel publishes from it say absolute. The accessor is the seam: (B) changes its body and the allowlist, not its callers. Owed with it: the rows sent to hosts would carry the default, so the web export must skip it to keep the browser's UA `fixed`, and own-style readers (`arena.style`) need the same audit. Kernel change: 3 rounds. With it, a compile-time guard in place of `scripts/position-reads.test.mjs`'s source scan (theme2 12:59): the authored row reachable only through a named accessor, so no read of it compiles elsewhere.
- **The terminal, Linux and Windows place a popover without `position-area`'s region** (2026-10-25; LLP 1021 D2, which iOS and the Mac now follow as Chrome does: `PositionArea.place`, `Kernel::measure_block`, `BlockAxis::Region`). The terminal places a top-layer box by its insets in the viewport's block (`Kernel::top_layer_origin`, host/terminal/src/paint.rs), and Linux and Windows place a popover by its `position-area` against its invoker (`host/painted/src/presenter/top/area.rs`) but lay it out in the viewport's block, so a width-less popover there is the viewport's fit-content where the web's is its option's region's (host/web/tests/position-area-region.test.mjs has Chrome's table). To size the box as the web does, the same rule is there to take: measure each option in its region with `measure_block` (a `Region` block, fit-content with min-content winning), take the first whose margin box fits its region, and give that region as the box's block.
- **A painted popover's options start again when its size changes** (2026-10-25; LLP 1021 D2, theme2's sign-off). `PositionArea.place`, like `origin` before it, resets the remembered option when the box's size in the whole block changes (its content or authored size), so a box that flipped while wide returns to its base once that fits. CSS keeps the last successful option across such a change; its fallback-sensitive changes are the `position` value, the `position-try` longhands, the `@position-try` rules and the box no longer generating a box (css-anchor-position-1, statefulness). To follow CSS, drop the size key and keep the option while it fits. A three-round attempt (2026-11-12) dropped size from the remembered option's key and reset it on hide and reveal instead; its reviews found two paths where the reset is missed: a manual macOS popover kept open while an ancestor is `display: none` (`MenusMac.sync`), which keeps a flip the wider viewport no longer needs; and on iOS a popover in a dialog sheet, whose ancestry walk follows UIKit's sheet hierarchy past the dialog's hidden wrapper (`MenusIOS.swift`).
- **A popover's trial measure copies the whole page's state** (2026-10-25; LLP 1021 D2, `Kernel::measure_block`; the lead and theme2). Each measure copies the arena and the engine tree, so its cost follows the page, not the popover: in a release bench one measure is 0.06 ms at 500 nodes, 0.46 ms at 3,000 (arena copy 0.15, engine copy 0.31) and 1.0 ms at 10,000 (engine copy 1.1); on Harmony's #general (about 470 nodes, iOS 27 simulator) a placement's five measures were 0.7–1.5 ms. Painted popovers keep their measures until the next batch (`PositionArea.MeasureCache`), so an open pays about one placement's. The way out: run the trial on the live state and restore the popover subtree's engine nodes (style, cache, layout) and arena columns after it, so its cost follows the subtree. Take it only if an app still spends more than 16 ms an open with the cache. Conditions (theme2): the restore runs on every exit path (drop- or guard-based, so an early return or a panic restores), the purity digest test covers a trial that exits early, and it gets 3 rounds.
- **A Release build after a Contract edit that changes the archive's linked capabilities compiles the whole Rust world again under fat LTO** (2026-10-21, euxy port 21). A plain Contract edit's `ios --release` is ~8.5 s (the plan and bytecode are linked outside the archive, `scripts/linked-plan.mjs`). An edit that adds a capability (a gradient on a row, measured: 57 s, cargo 48.5 s; port 21's 84 s release was one) changes the bake's `linked.rs` (`EXACT_LINKED`, `host/apple/src/link.rs` `Links`), so Cargo compiles the archive crate and fat LTO runs over everything. Making it seconds means choosing the linked set at link time: the archive built once with every capability's entry exported, a per-plan object outside the LTO unit holding the table of the ones the plan uses, and the linker's dead strip removing the rest. Measure the binary, TTI and idle footprint against today's before taking it (LLP 1047.001 D2 decides what ships).
- **An `agent ios` drive still launches its app, ~0.5 s of its ~3 s** (2026-10-21). The rest is the window capture's three `simctl io` reads (~1.3 s; the touch runner's `XCUIScreen.main.screenshot()` takes ~0.12 s when it is up, measured), the app's own drawing (~0.45 s, mostly `settleMotion`'s quiet window) and the build check. A drive could find the next app already launched: once a drive's app has ended, a kept process launches another with the same environment (plan, facts, store and its fresh flag; never under `--touch platform` or platform timing), and the next drive with that environment connects to it instead. Its store's lock and the fresh store's emptying at boot have to move to that process, and a launch by any other drive on the simulator has to retire it. LLP 1012.000 proposes the resident driver this would sit beside.
- **iOS: a custom grouped row's leading symbol at accessibility sizes** (2026-10-21, the Harmony custom-row fix's round 3). A custom row's leading symbol and title follow UIKit's cell at every text size (`-exact-list-symbol-*` ramps; at the accessibility sizes `listSymbolPoints` in `Affordances.swift`, 2026-10-23). What is left: at the accessibility sizes UIKit wraps a title's later lines back under the symbol to the margin, where a slot beside the text keeps every line inset. That needs the text to flow around the symbol.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/c2): reproduced and parked: the retained grouped-rtl fixture shows a standard native row staying LTR beside a custom row laid out RTL (physical left padding and symbol margins from the UA sheet); the fixed symbol slot at root font-size 53 is a kernel font-size override, not Dynamic Type.
- **Web: exact2's menu rule takes a row as it is at the click** (2026-10-22, theme2 02:54, option B). `host/web/menu-rows.js` intercepts only a menu row that names a popover when it's clicked, and acts after the row's press on the popover it names then; every other row keeps HTML's own activation. So a press that retargets its row from a popover to a dialog's command still opens the popover (the click-time target), and one that retargets it from a dialog's command to a popover leaves that popover nested in the menu, closing with it. Replaying every command after the press instead (option A) would re-implement HTML's command dispatch, which its re-checks kept finding gaps in. The native hosts resolve a row when it's chosen.
- **Web: a page built ahead of time shows a pushed route's Back only once the navigation glue runs** (2026-10-21, LLP 1116.000 D4). `document.rs` mirrors the projection's route visibility but not the bar's Back, which is the header's shadow root's, nor the header-held Back control it does not show, so the Back appears, and that control goes, at hydration. A declarative shadow root (`<template shadowrootmode>`) with the Back in it, adopted by `appBarBack`, and the control's mark would show them as the glue will.
