A three-round attempt (2026-11-12, `host/web/src/document_back.rs`, 1,156 lines with tests) projected the Back and adopted it at hydration; its reviews left two cases where projection and live navigation still differ: a disabled Back under a `navigationBack` with an `aria-busy=true` ancestor, which `routes.js:487` exempts and projection hides; and a header Back whose label is Markdown (`text "**Done**" markup="markdown"`), projected as the literal source where hydration shows the rendered words. Seen there, older: a `fullscreen` route with a transparent background hides the route under it in projection and not live (`document_before.rs:133`).
- **A `sent` reader off screen still costs euxy 0.7-0.9% CPU while it plays** (2026-10-21, euxy port 18, iPhone 17 simulator, Release, no MIDI device; `ps` over 20 s, interleaved with builds before and after). A reader that declares `exactMIDI().sent` is answered at each message's time (LLP 1125 D6): about sixteen commits a second for the default pattern, each changing no node while the activity log is not shown. It was 2.3-2.6% before `Frames.tick` skipped their presenter pass, then 1.2-1.45%; the display link no longer runs for them (`timersQuiet`) and the host no longer lays them out (`inert_layout`). With `sent` left out of the shape, 0.05%. What is left is a timer's wake and a commit per message: the runner's settle and the host's turn. Reaching 0.1% needs fewer commits, which D6 decides: answer `sent` at most every 250 ms or so (four commits a second), or only while something mounted reads it.
- **A derive over `sent` that nothing shows costs euxy port 21 2.9% CPU while it plays** (2026-10-21, iPhone 17 simulator, Release, medians of 5; port 20, which reads `sent` only in the shown log, 0.5%). Its activity log derive (`logRows`: the 128 messages formatted and sorted) is evaluated at every message's commit though the log is not shown, about 118,000 VM instructions and 97% of the commit (985 µs in a release bench; 36 µs without it). Settlement evaluates every derive whose inputs changed, read or not, and a derive that traps refuses the commit (LLP 1005 §6; the web JS target's `settle` pulls every derive too), so evaluating only the derives something mounted reads would change what commits: Charlie's ruling. Within the semantics, what is left is the VM's own cost per instruction: `machine.rs`'s `pop_n` and `collected` build each list in a vector and copy it into its shared allocation, `first` allocates a `Some`, and the extent walk measures again each small record a body copies out of a list. `machine.rs` is extracted to Lean (`semantics/vm-extract`), so changing it needs Charon and Aeneas.
- **Text whose weight changes every frame: what painting paragraphs at once still leaves** (2026-10-24; ~/bench/weightbench, medians of three 10 s runs; mode 1 a 13-row poster of ~97 pt type, mode 2 twelve 17 px paragraphs, the weight eased 100 to 1000 and back with a 100 ms rest at each end). `TextRasterizer.ensureUrgent` paints a frame's urgent paragraphs in turn for 2 ms, then the rest at once (`DispatchQueue.concurrentPerform`), because the late frames are the ten after a rest, where the CPU's clock is low and painting in turn took 4.2 of the main thread's 5.4 ms. iPhone 17 Pro Max: body text 35 late frames to 14 (UIKit 3), CPU 546 to 629 ms/s (UIKit 430); poster 10 to 0 (UIKit 10), CPU 527 to 816 (UIKit 526). iPhone 16 Pro Max: poster 29 to 0, CPU 639 to 1,090; body text 31 to 29, CPU 566 to 673. The CPU is each thread making the new font's glyph bitmaps again. Next to measure: a budget tied to the frame's deadline rather than 2 ms, and sharing the glyph bitmaps between the threads.
- **Game pads have not met a real pad** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003 §4's hardware half). Every host's adapter is built (web, macOS, iOS, tvOS, Android, Linux evdev, Windows XInput) and the engine is tested, but no physical pad has been driven: none is attached to the Mac that built them. With a pad connected to each device, `bun game/dev.mjs beacons --lan` serves Beacons to a browser and prints Open in native for the Mac and for an iPad or a Pixel on the network: the left stick moves the player, `GamepadSouth` jumps, `GamepadL1` held runs, `GamepadWest` lights a beacon in reach, and the journal (`logs`) says `pad 0 connected standard` and `pad 0 disconnected` on unplugging. On the Mac, an iPad and a Pixel, check each: the delivery the driver reports as recognized, movement, both connection journal lines, focus lost while a button is held (the world releases it, and the pad reports itself when focus returns), and a disconnect while held. Linux needs an Xbox pad (xpad, letters) and a DualSense (positional) at launch: their X and Square are both `GamepadWest`.
- **A pad press and release that reach a game late together lose their length** (2026-10-18, LLP 1127.003's final review; #342). Android stamps a pad reading with its event's `eventTime` now, as the Linux display stamps a report, but the engine places a stamp from before the last frame, and every stamp of a frame that follows a gap over 250 ms, at the frame's start (`Sim::advance`, `Sim::input`), so a press and release delivered later than that read as one instant and a 210 ms hold reads as a tap. A three-round attempt (2026-11-12) carried `eventTime` through Kotlin, JNI and the engine's queue; its reviews found the timing it has to keep: a release that arrives after a hitch must apply at once rather than at its report time's place in world time (`game/engine/src/sim/queue.rs`, a held button stayed down about 730 ms); a pad report stamped in the future must not hold back later key events (`game/engine/src/sim.rs`); and draining every pad reading in `produce()` reorders it ahead of an earlier touch that moves focus (`Owner.kt`). Report time should feed the tap/hold duration, not the delivery order.
- **`@exact/gamepad` has met no real pad** (2026-10-26). Every registry (Linux evdev `FF_RUMBLE`, XInput, GameController with Core Haptics and `GCDeviceLight`, Android's `VibratorManager` and `LightsManager`) is driven only through injected pads' recorders. Run `packages/gamepad/test/device-checklist.md` on Chrome, a Mac, an iPad, a Pixel, a Linux machine (a `uinput` pad with `FF_RUMBLE` needs no hardware) and the NucBox.
- **`@exact/gamepad`'s shared index and multi-session claims are not driven on Apple or Android** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.022 stage 2). An injected pad reaches no canvas there (a snapshot `GCController` is never announced; Android's test pad has no `InputDevice`), so that an index names one pad to the canvas and the module is checked on Linux only; two sessions' claims in the sample host (`--host`) and an Android activity remade while the process lives ("Don't keep activities") are not driven either, nor is tvOS compiled with the module: `build.mjs --tvos --no-install beacons-apple` refuses Beacons' bake receipt ("names another target, platform or app").
- **Bluetooth (LLP 1133.022 §17) is accepted and not built** (2026-10-26). The desktop Rust route it rides on is built (D10 item 5); its stages, the Pulse drive and the heart-rate strap's checklist are in the RFC.
- **USB, HID and serial (LLP 1133.037): what is left** (2026-10-27). Android (stage 3: the host's USB broker and usbfs I/O, `SerialManager`); Windows USB over WinUSB; the Rust hosts' plug notifications and URB reaping; macOS HID's per-report blocklist and USB's bulk and interrupt transfers; the seam tests (stage 6); Bench 4 in the sample host; the Windows crates driven on the NucBox. Hardware drives are owed (LLP 1133.020 row 33).
- **One-colour text rasters on the Mac, and wide lines' rasters** (2026-10-20, after iOS's alpha-only rasters). (1) iOS and tvOS show eligible single-colour SDR paragraphs as 8-bit alpha coverage with a separately profiled tint through a public template `UIImageView` (`TextCoverage`, LLP 1008 §3). On a 13-row poster of ~97 pt type whose weight changes every frame, painting coverage alone rather than four bytes a pixel cut main-thread samples in 10 s from 7,410 to 3,019 (UIKit: 3,045), measured while a template image showed the coverage. The Mac paints into IOSurfaces (`TextRasterMac.swift`) and still uses four bytes a pixel, on purpose (measured 2026-10-20, weight bench's poster on this Mac, Time Profiler 10 s): its rasters run on workers through Core Animation's CG queue, where the glyph copy that cost iOS's main thread 65% (`CGSColorMaskCopyARGB8888`) is 2.2% of worker samples; the main thread's raster cost is IOSurface creation and registration and the commit. AppKit's public tint (a template image in an `NSImageView` with `contentTintColor`, shown with a multiply colour and no mask, and honoured by `cacheDisplay` and `render(in:)`) works only for an image view in the view tree, never its layer alone (probe: ~/bench/weightbench/a8mac), and shows a `CGImage`: a subview per paragraph, and the main-thread copy at commit that the IOSurface path exists to avoid. Not worth it unless a Mac profile shows the glyph copy on the main thread. (2) A raster is cut to a band vertically for a tall paragraph, never horizontally: a nowrap line wider than what shows (a marquee's) is painted whole every time it changes. Cutting it to the visible width needs the visible rect under a running animation, which `Presenter.textBand` reads from the model layer, so a row a keyframe animation slides would show blank past its cut. Not built: since (1) the weight bench's poster, rows about five times the screen's width, matches UIKit (119 fps, about 10 late frames in 10 s, 4.3 against 4.2 ms of main-thread work a frame), and a `UILabel`'s backing store also covers its whole width. The rows that would gain are the ones a keyframe slides, whose cut would have to cover the range the animation sweeps.
- **Files dragged in or pasted on the hosts that take none** (2026-10-20, LLP 1069.010 "Files dropped or pasted"). Web and macOS take `drop`, `dragenter` and `dragleave`; web, macOS and iOS give a paste its `files`. Linux (GTK's `drag-data-received`, `text/uri-list`), Windows (`IDropTarget`, `CF_HDROP`) and Android (`View.OnDragListener`, `ClipData` content URIs) refuse by name and paste text only; iOS's `drop` would be `UIDropInteraction` (iPad drags from Files). Each needs its host's minting of a `doc:` handle for a URL it did not pick.
- **`radius.full` squares a terminal box, so @exact/cards' avatar corner is 999px** (2026-10-20, the stage 12 E follow-up; x2-perf4's round 2). The base scale's terminal cell for every radius is 0 (`kernel/tables/schema.json` `_scale`), and the terminal draws a rounded box only for a radius above 0 (`host/terminal/src/paint.rs`), so a pill written `radius.full` gets square corners there where a px radius got rounded ones. `cards.avatar-corner` is `999px` until the terminal draws `full` as rounded (its cell `full`, or the painter reading `RADIUS_FULL`); then it can be `radius.full`, and the clone's 999px corners too. Needs a ruling first (checked 2026-10-24 on fc4fcedd3): LLP 1121 stage 11 B's approved cell forms have `kernel/build/scale.rs` refuse `full` in the terminal column (llp/1121-tamber.rfc.md, "The cell forms"), and `scripts/scale-check.test.mjs` and `contract/cli/tests/it/tamber_serve_base.rs` hold the terminal's radius.full at 0, while LLP 1101 (accepted) draws a positive radius with rounded glyphs. If theme2 admits `full` there, the change is that build check, the schema cell and those tests; the painter needs nothing.
- **The relay in Rust, once the hosted one needs it** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10c R3; theme2's 14:34 approval; owner rt1). The relay is one Bun file (`scripts/tamber-relay-server.mjs`): one EXACT-RELAY/1 implementation shared with the connector, an I/O-bound pipe. A Rust relay behind the same protocol buys less memory per socket and no GC pauses; the trigger is measured on the hosted instance: more than a few thousand concurrent streams, or memory per idle connector connection past what the host can hold.
- **A warning when a certificate for a relay account's names appears that this server didn't ask for** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10c R2; theme2's plan change 8 and the lead's answer to Q3; owner rt1). With the relay's default domain, its operator controls DNS for the account's names and could get a certificate for them unseen; a server could watch certificate transparency for certificates under `*.<label>.<relay>` whose key isn't its own and warn. That needs a CT search service at runtime (crt.sh, or a commercial monitor), since the logs themselves only offer whole-log scans: a developer's opt-in, naming the service. `--relay-name` with `--relay-dns-hook` (names of their own), the CAA records it prints set (`issue` and `issuewild`) and the names pointed by A/AAAA (no CNAME), already removes the residual, for the CAs that honour CAA (every public one must).

- **Harmony in conform, with a backend of its own** (2026-10-21, the lead's Harmony task; skipped for now, the lane's in-memory web tests cover it). Conform compares the JS target with the wasm runner step by step on each app's own data. Harmony's data is a Snapback client: with no backend it shows only the never-synced state, and with one, sync timing makes the two runs differ. Conform also builds an app's Linux crate at `apps/<app>/linux`, where Harmony's is `apps/harmony/app/linux`. Doing it means a conform mode that starts `snapback4 dev --memory` per run (as `scripts/memory-backend-test.mjs` does, the server proven its own and the build's grants checked), seeds it with fixed times rather than `seed.mjs`'s now-relative ones, waits each step for the device's sync to settle on both targets, and finds the Linux crate beside the app.
- **A refused design write can still cost a whole build** (2026-10-23, theme2's 09:24 ruling on stage 14 E; since 0a211db45 the design server names the build's first diagnostic for every write). A refused write is answered from the row check in about 41 ms of CPU once the background build of the current design has passed and the write reaches only rows the check judges (`rows::Left`, `contract/lower/src/theme/rows.rs`). Three cases still lower the whole app, stopped at its first refusal (about 4.1 s on a 73,392-row app against a 100 ms budget): a write made before the background build's verdict is in, which covers the seconds after any accepted write; a write whose tokens reach a use the row check leaves to the build; and a write that changes a motion value. For the first, the server could judge the token changes since the last design whose whole build passed with the same sources.
- **The server's write check: what it still leaves to the build** (2026-10-20, theme2's 14:19 ruling; narrowed by LLP 1121 stage 13 D, 2026-10-21). `contract_lower::rows::check` refuses a write that breaks an element's style row that passed, in the compiler's words, at the row: literal rows and every arm of a literal choice (stage 12), `font`/`font-family`/`font-weight`/`font-style` rows with the node's fonts through `type_tokens::tokens_named` and `fonts::font_use` (D1), computed rows (a name, a template, a choice with a computed arm, a record's field) judged whole where their form gives their type (D2), in the scope of the `for`, `match` or `if` arm around them and through a `let` (stage 14 E, 2026-10-23: the frames pushed by `contract_types`' shared helpers lowering calls too, `frames.rs`), and an `svg` subtree's rows renamed and coerced as lowering does (D3). Still only at the build: a computed row whose form doesn't give its type (a call, an index, a `let` inside another form), and every computed row of a `for` or `match` whose subject is such (both arms); a row that failed before the write; a `style`'s rows wherever one element applying the style is one whose class rows lowering rewrites or judges otherwise first (an SVG element's, a box's `transform`, a grouped list's, a `button`'s, an `audio`'s, a `delete=` row's), chooses it (`class=(c ? A : B)`), or sits under a `for` or `match` the check can't type (a style all of whose users are none of these is judged at each since 2026-10-22, theme2's 02:08 ruling; a write's refusals are said as the build reaches them, but a computed shorthand or `flex`, or a `transition`/`animation` row, the build refuses before them is not among them, so the first said may then be the build's second); a computed shorthand or `flex` (a written one is judged longhand by longhand since 2026-10-22, theme2's ruling on the shorthand plan; a computed one lowering expands at run time into another row set, which the check would have to mirror); `transition`/`animation` rows (their motion tokens the loader writes in before this map is read); a state part's row; a refusal outside a style row; a write that also edits a Contract source; font rows after a font file the build read changed. **The first refusal said** (theme2's 09:24 ruling on stage 14 E): where the build would refuse one of those first (a row the check can't type, a computed shorthand, a motion row), the first refusal the check says is the build's second; every refusal said is still the build's, in its words and place, and none is false (pinned by `tamber_rows_bound::a_row_left_to_the_build_before_a_judged_one_is_the_stated_limit`). **The window before the background build's verdict**: a file whose whole build fails before the write for a reason of its own (an element lowering refuses before its rows, its children never lowered: a positional `svg`, a leaf with children, a grouped list's malformed section, an unknown tag) has its rows left to the build once the background whole build says it fails (`Checker::seen_failed`), but a write in the moments before that verdict can be refused at a row in other words than the build's first refusal. Closing it means the walk sharing every pre-style exit of `prepare_element` (dozens), or the check waiting for the verdict.
- **Several native copies, and `navigate` on the native hosts** (2026-10-19, LLP 1122 T7; theme2's Q4 ruling). The design tool's grid of live copies is web copies of the dev page, each pinned to one mode; a native build stays the single connected build, pinned as before. Several simulators (a boot, memory and a runner slot each) would show the native rendering of every mode at once, and need each native host to take `navigate {seq, location}` through its own router (a deep link in place) and report `location` in its status, as the web pages do (docs/tamber-protocol.md, the build channel). Until then a native build lacks the `navigate` capability, and the server answers it 409 `unsupported`.
- **iOS: a root without tabs that shows one fixed route still gets a navigation controller** (2026-10-19). A tab follows its panel's structure (LLP 1075.003 §3.7): a tab whose panel can hold more than one route, or whose route can show a header, is a navigation controller, and one that shows one fixed route with no header is a plain view controller, as UIKit allows (`Runner::panels_need_navigation`). A root without tabs is always a `UINavigationController` (`installPrimary`), whose view in a window lays out the SwiftUI floating bar host. Its cost is unsettled. In euxy port 12 (Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, interleaved launches, each footprint after `simctl erase`), a barless tab measured 25,010 KB in a navigation controller against 23,986 KB in a plain container (`footprint`, medians of 5), and `EUXY_TTI` 1,234.5 against 1,223 ms (medians of 10). A UIKit-only app on iOS 27.0 (an iPhone 18 Pro simulator, six tabs each selected once, medians of 5) measures navigation controllers with their bars hidden at what plain controllers cost (24,754 against 24,770 KB), and the shown bars at about 2 MB more (26,754 KB). Measure the root case in Exact before building it. Reading the same plan fact (`panels_need_navigation`) for the root's own routes would let a root that shows one fixed route with no header be a plain controller under the primary owner (`placeOwner`, `ownerFrame`, the sheet's presenting controller).
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/i): an attempt to hold a barless root without a navigation controller (d66c68406d) was withdrawn (8a19f144fb) as unqualified; no change landed.
- **Library platform inputs not checked** (2026-10-20, LLP 1139 D6, stage 12 C3; theme2's ruling (3)). `lower-library-platform` refuses a library package's `exactDelivery`/`exactSurface` resources and `exactViewport().displayMode`; two inputs D6 lists are limits: an `inject` whose provider reads the host (a provider in the package is itself held to the rule; one outside it is the app's), and a package file named as an `app.json` `host.<platform>.entry` or per-platform variants of one component (no Contract syntax names a host entry from a package). Doing it means following an inject to its provider across files, and reading app.json host entries against package roots.
- **Base names and length tokens inside a Contract literal shadow row** (2026-10-20, LLP 1121 stage 12 B3, theme2's Q1). `box-shadow="0 space.md 4px black"` written on the row, not as a token, is refused, pointing to a shadow token (`x: box-shadow = 0 space.md 4px black`), which reads it. Taking it on the row means the kernel's shadow parse reading theme lengths at run time, each read recorded, and the same per-column check at build as a shadow token's; a literal stays px until an app needs it.
- **A Dynamic Type opt-in for the base scale** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 ruling 6, the lead's assignment to B's first commit). The base scale (`space.*`, `radius.*`, the schema's `scale`) is px (points) in every column and never scales with Dynamic Type, as a measured UIKit inset or corner does not. An app that wants its spacing to follow the text size has no switch for it: an opt-in (a design-level flag, or `rem`-based base values under it) scaling the space steps, never the corners, with a test per host at an accessibility size.
- **A 3,000-token × 64-context design cannot import: share a context's tree** (2026-10-19, iosfix's x2/import-rest; theme2's 19:07 ruling). The budget is calibrated to the isolate (`budget.rs`: `IMPORT` 3 × 2²⁴, `REIMPORT` 3 × 2²³, about 600 and 750 MB), so a design of about 650 tokens over 64 contexts re-imports and about 880 import fresh; 3,000 × 64 is refused with the budget's message (the export inlines every token into every context: 93 MB, 3.4 GB of RSS to re-import whole). The standard route: the DTCG Resolver Module's sets and modifiers can carry a context's differences instead of inlining every token per context, so the export shrinks within the format we already write (no new wire), and re-import reads one shared tree plus each context's differences. Needs its own plan and theme2's ruling: the export's shape (a base set, each modifier context its overrides), what a client sends back, and the budget measured again.
- **Tamber importers: the super-linear paths left behind the isolation** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 §5 stage 10b E2+E3 review round 5; owner rt1; the lead's 12:31Z ruling). Every import is now planned in a watched process of its own (`scripts/tamber-isolate.mjs`: about 1 GiB, 10 s, a 2 MiB answer, one at a time), so these cost a client its own import, not the server; `tamber/src/budget.rs` is a partial second layer (the DTCG importer and re-import; the CSS importers are bounded by their own caps, iosfix's x2/import-rest). A held re-import's marks still run in the shared serve, on operations the server built. Still to make linear, from Astra's round 5 (`llp/reviews/code-2026-10-19-s10b-e23-r5.astra.md`): generated dotted paths uncharged (`export.rs` `flatten`, `dtcg.rs` `path.join`); composite alias object keys uncharged (`kinds.rs` `inner`); resolver sibling overrides copied uncharged (`dtcg.rs` `deref`) and `deep`'s `a.clone()` charged after; `type_of` uncharged (its walk is linear now, iosfix's x2/import-linear, which also made re-import judge a proposal whole and look tokens up by name). From Grok's round 5 (`…-r5.grok.md`): `subsets` in `export.rs` materializes every subset of many one-value axes (walk combinations, cap axes); `reaches` in `dtcg.rs` per colour token; `deep`'s `token_like` clones the destination before it is charged. Left after round 9 (the lead's 14:09Z review cap; `llp/reviews/code-2026-10-19-s10b-e23-r9.*.md`): an import's per-client count is released when planning ends, not when its hold does (held proposals are capped at 64 and 32 MiB in all, a client below `write` making room only from its own); the design read's 2 s answer leaves its I/O running; the design's identity is the manifest's at the import, not the serve's own target (a rewritten app.json mid-run); the CLI's sources read synchronously before the planner (its own process, no server's); a probe in flight past the next tick could kill without waiting out `ps` (RSS overshoot during one probe); tests for slow snapshot readers, the Linux launch and a large real design, and for the design read's loop (a forced short read, a file growing mid-read, the 2 s deadline: theme2's 21:26 sign-off accepts them untested). (Done: an import stops with its request, its client's revocation or the server's close, leaving the queue at once; the design read to its end; `kept()` capped and never read through a link.)
- **`sortBy` with a computed direction** (2026-10-19, the euxy port's friction). The VM already takes the direction as a run-time bool (`Stdlib::SortBy`), so lowering could compile `dir == "desc"`; what stops it is the semantics, whose `sortDir` (`semantics/Contract/Eval.lean`, `Big.lean`'s `.sortBy` rule, `Lower.lean` and their proofs) reads a literal. Until then the refusal names `dir == "desc" ? sortBy(xs, f, "desc") : sortBy(xs, f)`.
- **Warm Beacons Rust-edit proof budgets** (LLP 1127.001, 2026-10-18): instrumented warm before/after samples were Linux 2.697 / 4.823 s and web 17.861 / 17.933 s, with maximum loads 38.18 / 39.86 and 35.93 / 37.63 respectively (sampled throughout, all below 50). The <3 s / <10 s budgets remain open; the earlier 4.362 / 25.396 s observations remain misses. Reusing the JS target's existing input-checked editor/text-flow products removes repeated Cargo/wasm-opt work: that phase fell from 1.038 to 0.170 s, without changing proof steps. There is no demonstrated full-loop speedup. Web's receipt/build span is 7.256 s (GPU 1.565, host 0.275, host wasm-opt 2.106, bindgen 0.174, input scans 0.402, shared leaves 0.170; the remaining 2.564 is tool/bake/packaging balance). Its proof is 10.518 s, including two browser opens 1.364 s (Chrome 0.846, page ready 0.149), 23 typing steps 6.868 s, 13 settles 0.274 s, other clock steps 0.158 s, taps 0.661 s and screenshots 0.564 s. Nested spans must not be added twice. Linux's receipt/build is 2.244 s (GPU 1.462, scans 0.501), proof 2.403 s; first Play varied from 0.177 to 1.805 s, with no cause established. Profile typing and first Play next; do not weaken the proof or change the budgets without Charlie's amendment.
- **App-supplied highlight ranges on LLP 1045's Markdown editor, for T3 Code's composer** (2026-10-18, LLP 1114.007 r3, Charlie's ruling). T3 Code's composer moves to `textarea markup="markdown"`, which refuses `decorations`; what it needs from app-computed ranges there (a language tag, a mention) is LLP 1045's to design, over that editor's own styling, never a second editor. Chips are LLP 1045.000's inline atoms (accepted 2026-10-23), built in its stages: 0, the TextKit 2 gate, measured 2026-10-23 (its §5.1); 1, crates, Contract, kernel and the web editor and reader, built 2026-10-25; 2, the Apple editor and reader, drawing the pill as the attachment's picture (§10 Q9, 2026-10-24), built 2026-10-25, its VoiceOver reading of atoms ruled as built and declared (§10 Q10, 2026-10-25); 3, the clone's adoption. Left: a `symbol:` icon on the web (an image URL draws) and an image icon on Apple (`symbol:sf/` draws); `atompress` from the Linux, Windows and Android readers, which show the link text; the Apple reader's atoms as elements and keyboard stops and in `state atoms`, and `rangeFrame` over a pill in a macOS region-reader paragraph (64 KB of text, or 16 KB in 256 runs or more, LLP 1072); adjacent atoms wrapping apart on Apple.
- **Paint a textarea's decorations on Android and the terminal** (2026-10-18, LLP 1114.007 r3 D5). Both keep the source and editing and paint nothing; Android as Compose `SpanStyle` colour over the field's `TextFieldValue` without changing its text, the terminal as each cell's colour and underline. Waits for a consumer on either.
- **An undecorated textarea's own `text-shadow` paints nothing on the painted host** (2026-11-14, Astra's and Grok's reviews of the decoration-shadow fix). `textarea value="H" text-shadow="0px 40px 0px red"` on Linux or Windows: the field draws its text through `Backend::text` directly (`host/painted/src/paint/field.rs`), not the shared painter that casts decoration shadows, so the element's own shadow is never drawn.
- D2 quiet measurement: three cold `gpu-dev` runs under load 5 with `game/bench/cold-build/probe.py`; wire `keptGameCrates` into `buildBake` if the eligible registry chain is over a quarter (LLP 1132 D2).

- **Water's shafts pass under a lid** (2026-10-19, LLP 1139.002 D4). The shafts are screen space: their source is the surface where the frame sees it, so an opaque lid above the water (hidden behind the surface from below) shades the floor and its caustics but not the shafts. Sampling the sun's shadow cascades at each march step's surface point (binding group 1 in `shafts.wgsl`, one comparison a step) would stop them; measure its cost on a phone first.
- **Game baseline release builds remain dominated by path compilation and LTO.** LLP 1132.A2 measured 902.1 s under load 71–222; the cache-eligible registry dependency chain was 62.6 s (6.9%), so release does not use the registry cache. Measure those path/LTO phases on a quiet machine before choosing the next build change. The web dev producer's profile measurement belongs with LLP 1127.001 D1.
- **A transport-driven timeline, so a playhead needs no re-sync task** (2026-10-18, euxy playing: 9–10% CPU with a frame task, 0.1% with `docs/contract-for-agents.md`'s "Following the transport"). The guide's lights are loops on a clock timeline (LLP 1055.002) whose phase the app sets from `exactTransport()` once per anchor: a gated `task … key=anchor` reads `performanceNow()`, and each light carries a negative delay. What that leaves to the app: a pause shows the head drawn as static nodes (the loops are removed); a one-off change at a beat restarts by alternating two identical `keyframes` names. A timeline whose time is the transport's position (`animation-timeline=transport`, ms along the beat grid, holding while stopped) would remove the task, the delays and the pause path. It adds a value to `animation-timeline`, so it needs Charlie's ruling.
- **Lowering checks an attribute in an `if` arm it can prove is never taken** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 9 H, theme2 02:15). After a component is inlined with a constant prop, an element under `if symbol != ""` whose caller passes `symbol=""` is still checked, so a value only that dead arm would build (here `symbol:${symbol}` folding to `symbol:`) is refused. Stage 9 H made an empty `symbol:` name mean no symbol, as an empty `sf/` one already did, which covers that case. The general fix would skip value checks in an arm whose condition folds to false. It is broader than it looks, because it would also hide real value errors written there, so it waits for a decision on how dead arms should be reported.
- **History of a template row: a key a node keeps across versions** (2026-10-18, LLP 1121 stage 9 D2, theme2 17:18). `history` and `revert` read a `.contract` file's state rows and a style's own rows by name; a node's own attribute, which `set-row` writes at its place, has no key that survives a commit (lines move, no node id is kept), so a commit of such edits is code: left out of `history`, and `revert` can't invert it (the session's undo still gives the bytes back). A key of the component, the tag path to the node and the attribute's name, refused where ambiguous, would let both read it. A component's `params` lines (stage 9 D3's `add-param` and `remove-param`) are code in history too: their revert needs the template row that reads them, so they wait for that key, and then join by (component, name).
- **Geolocation's options composed across readers on Apple** (LLP 1133.002 D5). Each request writes its own `desiredAccuracy`, `distanceFilter` and `activityType` to the one manager; D5 asks for the finest accuracy, the smallest filter, and `geolocation-options-conflict` for two readers' different `activityType`. Wants the runner to hand the module every live reader's options.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/u): Parked on runner and module inputs outside the assigned scope. No source change. D5 requires the options of every live reader, their release and generation transfer. The implementation is packages/geolocation/apple/Geolocation.swift, not the Apple presenter; its request currently changes the single manager. The runner's source-reader/module boundary must deliver the whole active reader set before finest accuracy, sma
- **Weatherlight's sky leaves a frame on Android** (Pixel 10a, Android 16, 2026-10-20). The sky `canvas` has `padding=26` and `background-color="#183e56"`; on Android the surface draws inside the padding, so a 26 px band of that colour frames the sky, where on iOS the sky fills the card to its rounded edge. One host disagrees with the other about the box a canvas surface fills.
- **A data-code module call's handles and argument groups are not tracked** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.009 D6, D10 items 10 and 12). Its reply reaches its answer outside the runner, which cannot see it dropped, and its arguments cannot be traced to the answer that read them, so a group data code asks for is released only with the whole name (`modules/handles.rs`, `modules/bound.rs`). Wants the data runtimes to report a forgotten answer's module replies.
- **The language model's web create starts a download for any origin** (2026-10-25, found reviewing LLP 1133.027 D16). `packages/language-model/web/index.js`'s `createLanguageModel` applies LLP 1133.016 D5's person's-act rule only on its agent path; in production it calls `LanguageModel.create()` whatever the call's origin and foreground, so the browser's activation check is the only guard, and the runner's admission reads no `download` (`runner/src/runner/modules/admit.rs`). A check of the availability first does not close it, since the browser reads availability again inside `create()` and may download a model evicted in between (LLP 1133.027 D16). The enforceable fix is a top-level `"origin": "app"` and `"foreground": true` on `createLanguageModel`, which narrows LLP 1133.016 D5 and so needs Charlie's ruling; then prove it in production mode over a stand-in `LanguageModel`.
- **Check on Foundation Models that a prompt after an abort replays the aborted turn** (2026-11-13, LLP 1133.016 D6). `packages/language-model/apple/LanguageModel.swift`'s `end` drops the platform session when the response generating aborts or fails, so the next prompt rebuilds it from the transcript; `test/sessions.test.mjs` holds that on the agent model. Prompt after an aborted streaming reply on a Mac with Apple Intelligence and check that the model sees the aborted input and its partial reply.
- **The web's Prompt API tool loop is held only by a stand-in** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.016 D9, stage 1). `packages/language-model/web/index.js` collects a stream's `tool-call` items as the explainer's open loop has them; no shipping browser offers it to test against, and `contextoverflow`'s eviction is not exercised. Run `test/web.mjs`'s checks against a Chrome whose availability reads `tools`.
- **Take LLP 1133.016 D7's measurement on a quiet machine** (2026-10-24, stage 2). On an iPhone simulator under load average ~700 on 10 cores, a scripted reply growing to 4,000 tokens in Agents cost the main thread tens of milliseconds an update (budget 2 ms), most of it rasterising the reply's one growing paragraph (`TextRasterizer.ensure` under `Presenter.paintChangedText`). Repeat it quiet, with the Contract `p` case the stage plan names; if it still fails, the fix is likely rasterising only what changed in a growing paragraph, not a higher poll floor. Also run LLP 1047's probe for the Apple implementation's size.
- **Finish driving @exact/language-model on Android** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.016 stage 4). On an API 31 emulator (`-read-only -partition-size 6144`, which booted here) `test/drives.mjs android` passes drive 3 only: the others read answers one request late, the reader's 20 s clock wait above. Once that is fixed, rerun it; the fixture's drives 7 and 8 have no Android runner (`test/apple.mjs --agent` is Apple's). ML Kit itself wants a Pixel 9 or later: `test/checklist.md`'s Android part.
- **Run the recording suites' real Apple paths** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.009 stage 2). This Mac's input answers since 2026-10-28 (a bare `AVAudioEngine` probe reads 48 kHz, and Chromium's leg of `packages/recording/test/web.mjs` runs), but `test/apple.mjs` has still run nowhere: on an iOS simulator CoreSimulator did not answer `simctl create`, and on macOS the build's signing failed (`errSecInternalComponent`) and the microphone needs a person's grant (`--ask`). Unrun with it: the hatch's mixer and `holdsCapture`, a macOS `deviceId`, `apple.formatID`'s ALAC and Opus takes, and the owner's XCTests.
- **Android has no session end for `destroy`** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.009 D6, D10 item 1). The host lives with its process (`Exact.owner`), so `ExactExtension.destroy` is never called and a recorder's `MediaMuxer.stop()` cannot run at an orderly end. Apple waits a second for each adapter's `destroy` (`ExactExtensions.destroy`).
- **The camera's open ends** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.008 §13). (1) Geolocation's and the microphone's Android permission histories move onto `ExtensionContext.permission` (D8 item 7), which the camera uses. (2) With the real Apple implementation bound, a screenshot of the viewfinder shows black: the preview layer is not in a view hierarchy's drawing, and §9 asks for a placeholder naming the camera. (3) The photo editor's `takePhoto` tool waits for LLP 1111's tool door; drive 6 is `contract/cli/tests/it/camera.rs` until then. (4) Android's agent picture is drawn in Kotlin, not the package's card, so its photo's SHA-256 differs from the other hosts'. (5) Every app importing @exact/media-capture links CameraX on Android (the recorder: about 0.9 MB of dex); splitting the camera's capture out of media-capture's Android implementation, or loading it only when `video=` is asked, would spare an audio-only app it.
- **A store write reconnects Excord's gateway when another resource wrote** (2026-10-23, found driving LLP 1133.010). The gateway resource reads `native.available` (`hostOf`, a device read), so the runner counts it a store reader, and any store revision another commit makes asks it again; `connect` then opens a new socket. Seen when Lock Excord's move bumped the revision (now it does not, `Store::adopt_occupancy`); a rotated token does not, since the gateway's own turn writes it. A launch also asks the gateway twice (`data_ready`), and the second socket is refused `native executor admission limit reached` until the backoff's retry. Fix in Excord: read the host identity once, in `loadSession`, so the gateway reads no device fact; or a runner rule that a device fact other than the store does not make a resource a store reader.
- **Sign a Mac build for locked secrets** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.010 D7). `host/apple/presence.mjs` finds a macOS profile granting the app's keychain group and refuses a release without one; signing with it (`keychain-access-groups`, the application identifier, the embedded profile, as `macWidgetEntitlements` does for widgets) is not built, since no Mac it was built on had a profile. Then drive 10's Mac part (`packages/local-auth/test/device-checklist.md`).
- **A package's tests make its consumers' builds stale** (2026-10-23). An app depending on a package by `file:` copies the whole package, `test/` too, and the drivers' freshness check counts it, so editing a package's suite refuses every drive until the app is rebuilt (and its copy reinstalled). Leave `test/` out of the package's build inputs. A fix must keep three cases its review found (2026-11-12): a file under `test/` that the package's exports reach through a symlink still counts, since retargeting the link changes the implementation; a nested `package.json` without `name` between an imported `test/` file and the package root still counts, since its `sideEffects` changes bundling; and a declaration an import names that does not exist yet stays a watch input, so creating it rebuilds.
- **Module use found in Rust and TypeScript** (LLP 1133 D6 rule 6). The plan names a module only when the Contract calls one of its names; a data-code-only caller's Contract has to read one, or the binding is missing and the runner refuses the call.
- **Flex line breaking compares floats where Chrome compares 1/64px layout units** (Chrome 151.0.7922.34, 2026-10-18, after Taffy patch 47). Each case is a `display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;align-content:flex-start` row in normal flow holding two `height:30px;flex-shrink:0` items. A `width:100px` row with `width:50.01px` items: Chrome 100×30 (one line, since 50.01 and 100 floor to 50 and 100 layout units), kernel 100×60. A `width:99.99951171875px` row with `width:50px` items: Chrome 100×60 (the width floors to 99.984375), kernel 99.9995×30; `width:99.9990234375px` likewise. With `width:50.02px` items in 100px, both give 100×60. Patch 47's fit test allows 1/1024px of excess so that decimal sizes (three `60.4px` items and a `0.1px` gap in `181.4px`, which Chrome keeps on one line) do not wrap on f32 roundoff. Comparing the line length and the limit as Chrome does, each item's outer size and the gaps floored to 1/64px before summing, would match all of these and would replace that tolerance. The same gap reaches Taffy patch 49's stretch test, which truncates the item's natural and stretched cross sizes to 1/64px but sums the natural height in f32: a normal-flow or absolute `display:flex;width:100px;max-height:110.105px` row holding a `width:100px;aspect-ratio:2` item whose children are `width:20px;height:100%` then two `width:20px;height:30.055px`. Chrome 151 gives the item 110.09375 and the percentage child 50, because Blink truncates each length (30.046875) before summing, so the cap equals the natural height. The kernel gives 110.105 and 110.105, treating the cap as a stretch; it was 50 before patch 49. Tried and parked on 2026-10-18 (branch `q24/flex-layout-units`, four commits, four review rounds): converting each item's components and the line limit to layout units inside `collect_flex_lines` fixes every case above, but Taffy only has the f32 sums, and each attempt to recover Blink's components from them regressed a case main and Chrome agree on. Measured in Chrome 151, branch against main: a `width:100px` block holding an auto-width wrapping row with `margin-left:.01px` and two `width:50px;height:30px;flex-shrink:0;margin-right:-.01px` items (Chrome and main one line, the branch two, because the parent's 99.99px arrives as an f32 border box); a `width:100px` wrapping row of two `display:block;flex-basis:50.01px;flex-shrink:0;height:30px` items each holding a `width:50.015625px;height:1px;margin:0 -.01px` child (Chrome and main two lines, the branch one, because a measured automatic minimum is f32); `aspect-ratio:0.7` items 100px high in a `139.984375px` row (an f32 ratio widened to f64 drops a unit); a flexed row whose f32 width equals its authored width by chance. Doing it right means carrying the sizes' layout-unit components from style resolution and from the parent (block layout's sums, `LayoutInput`'s known dimensions, measured content) into the flex algorithm, not reconstructing them in flexbox.rs; the branch's tests (`flex_line_*` in flexbox.rs's intrinsic_tests) and 17 Chrome rows are reusable.

- **The Material oracle's findings, and what it still owes** (2026-10-18, LLP 1115.001.001 §3; `bun host/android/reference/oracle.mjs` on `expo-build-1000`). Found and open: the navigation bar places its destinations from the left under a right-to-left direction (`Layout::bottom` is told none), and where no palette is told its indicator and container are Exact's `ButtonFace` and grouped background, not Material's baseline (review `code-2026-10-18-material-navbar-r1`); a field's label floats on a 150 ms ease where Material 3 1.4 springs it (`MotionSchemeKeyTokens.FastSpatial`); a field's texts are 0.6 to 3.6 dp narrower than Material's (its type ramp's tracking, 1115.001's reported type ramp). Fixed: a disabled field's container; the navigation bar's colours, gaps and 56 dp indicator; a field's indicator on the pixel grid; the top bar's title after the Up arrow (56 dp, not 52); the checkbox, the radio and the filled button are Material's, drawn; a checkbox and radio lay out at 24 dp and every Material control takes a touch over 48 dp, apart from layout (§5 Q5, ruled 2026-10-19); the tonal, outlined and text buttons are not yet (D9); the door names a field's painted texts; the oracle reads Robolectric's Roboto as `fonts.xml` does (the "bold" title was Medium drawn for 400). O2 calibrated on one Pixel (thresholds in `oracle.mjs`; layout and text geometry not yet measured on the phone, nor a second Pixel). Owed: O3, accessibility and motion; O4, the hourly job with its release watch.
- **Linux desktop locale for dates** (LLP 1126 D6): `time` and `formatDate` format in English whatever the viewer locale.

- **What Android 10 and 11 still lack** (2026-10-17, a Galaxy Note 9 and a Pixel 5; `host/android/README.md`, "Android 10 and 11"). (1) Android 10's overscroll glow is not drawn, so a scroller's end shows nothing where a hand-built Android 10 app glows: `EdgeEffect` draws the glow below API 31, only its distance and the stretch are Android 12's (`Scrolling.STRETCH`). (2) Outline text carries 1 to 4 % more ink (by coverage) than `drawGlyphs`' atlas glyphs on the same phone, differing along glyph edges only. (3) A COLRv1 glyph is drawn from its plain outline below API 31 (swash renders COLR version 0 and bitmap strikes), which Noto Color Emoji's COLRv1 build leaves empty; Android 10 and 11's own emoji are CBDT and draw. (4) Not tried: Android 11 (no device), and a TypeScript data module, whose Android Hermes bundle is built at API 30.
- **What a package's design can't do yet** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 A1; theme2's rulings Q1, Q6 and Q7; its icon tokens landed in stage 13). (2) References across packages: a package token names its own package's tokens, roles and base names only, never another package's (`tamber-unknown-token`), even one it depends on. (3) Scoped namespaces: a package's tokens are named by the last part of its name, so two linked packages `@a/reading` and `@b/reading` that both ship a design are refused (`tamber-package-name`); if a real case appears, names could carry the scope. (4) DTCG export: `export` folds the app's design file alone, so an app whose design names a package token can't export (`tamber-unknown-token`); an app-only document needs the join with the package tokens left out.
- **A package's multicolour (non-template) SVG icon on the web** (2026-10-22, LLP 1121 stage 13; theme2's 03:22 ruling Q2 (a)). A package's icon SVG is read from `<package root>/assets/`, and in v1 it must be a one-colour template (`tamber-icon-svg` otherwise), drawn from the plan's inline path on every host. The web alone draws a non-template file as written, as `assets/<svg>` from the app's staged `assets/`, and no web build stages a package's `assets/`. When a package needs a multicolour icon: stage each package's `assets/` under `assets/packages/<ns>/` (host/web/build.mjs, dev.mjs and serve.mjs, host/web-js/build.mjs) and give the plan's `svg` field that path. Apple hosts draw a multicolour file as written; a watch still needs an `sf` source.
- **A package SVG read across three swaps** (2026-10-22, LLP 1121 stage 13's narrow re-check, Astra RE-3; contrived). `contract/lower/src/icons/confined.rs` opens a package's SVG, resolves its path again and compares the resolved file's device and inode with the descriptor's. An attacker who can write the package's `assets/` and swaps the path three times inside a few system calls (a link out of the package when it is opened, a file inside when it is resolved, a link out again before the resolved file's metadata is read) passes the check: the resolved path is the SVG's own, whose metadata, read after the third swap, follows the link to the file already opened, so the outside bytes are read. Binding the walk with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `openat` per component (or `lstat` of the resolved path against the descriptor) would close it, if a real case appears.
- **Package-file layouts outside the read-only guard's definition** (2026-10-19, LLP 1121 stage 11 A2 part 2; theme2's ruling 21:32 defines a package file by real path: a named package.json above it in the app's tree, a node_modules segment, or a root the app's load resolved; the server also refuses under any directory a link inside a node_modules points to, walking the app's tree and the node_modules above it, failing closed past 400,000 directories or on a directory it can't list). Not covered, by that ruling: (1) `app/node_modules` itself a link to a directory outside the app whose entries link back into it (`app/node_modules → /shared/deps`, `/shared/deps/@acme/reading → app/vendor/reading`, no manifest, app.json's "tamber" naming `vendor/reading/reading.tamber`): writable. (2) A Contract source under such a manifest-less linked directory, imported by a relative `use`: stage 8 D's `owned()` (contract/cli/src/tamber_contract.rs) checks only the app's own top-level node_modules, so `set-row`/`set-state`/`add-param` write it. (3) Journal completion after a crash renames an already-checked write without checking again, so a link made in that window isn't seen. Each needs a layout no resolver produces for the app; fix if one appears in practice.
- **Apple: what euxy's tabs still cost over its UIKit port, about 1.8 MB** (2026-10-17, iOS 27 simulator, Release; idle → every tab shown → back on the first: Exact +9.2 MB, UIKit +7.4). Per kernel node, the host's `Mirror` (`host/apple/src/host.rs`) holds its style JSON shared with every view that heard the same text (2026-10-18, `StyleTexts`; a screen whose styles differ per node still pays about 640 bytes a paragraph), and taffy's per-node layout cache is 128 bytes plus the measure slots used (448 before 2026-10-18). Per `NodeView`, its `registerForTraitChanges` costs UIKit about 0.8 KB (registry, registration, records). Measured with `heap` and `MallocStackLogging=lite` against a tour that switches tabs through `ExactTabs` (AXe's taps load UIAccessibility into the app, which then walks every view: +10–14 MB in both apps).

- **A Rust update that changes the Rust blanks the app's Rust-drawn canvases until the next binary** (2026-10-16, Caltrain on an iPhone through AppDrop: the line map lost its white background, stations and line while release 2 renamed a station in Rust). A replaced module is a `Swappable` with no embedded crate, and its `draw` answers "a replaced Rust module does not draw Canvas 2D yet" (stage 1 owes, (2) below). Since `d1de59c31` an update whose Rust is the binary's own runs the native crate and draws, so only an update that changes the Rust shows it. The fix: a draw operation on the logic ABI (a new ABI version; older modules keep the rest): the module records with `exact_canvas`'s recorder and returns the list, which the host replays as it replays a TypeScript draw (`exact_canvas::seam`). Then check Caltrain's line map with a Rust update on macOS (native dylib) and iOS (wasm).
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/d2): logic ABI 4 with the draw and retirement operations, recorders owned per session kept across native promotion, and a real-cdylib native draw test are on branch `q1022/d2-151-parked` (this Mac; not landed). Through it the replaced module's draw list reaches the host (Caltrain's map: background, line, train) and replays into a zero-sized bitmap layer, so the map still shows transparent on the iOS simulator; the module seam also carries no image requests or platform text measurement yet. Land it with the layer sized from the canvas content box at replay and the environment carried through the seam.
  2026-10-23 (panel, orchestrator q1022; Charlie's veto open): until the draw ABI lands, `push.mjs` and `deploy.mjs` refuse an update that changes the Rust of an app whose Rust draws a canvas, naming the binary stream; the classifier cannot yet tell Rust-drawn canvases from the plan's other surface calls (`surfaceCalls` is the GPU roster, `dataCrate` is null whenever Rust is on), so the refusal covers every changed Rust module until the module's canvas roster is in its receipt. The ABI work on `q1022/d2-151-parked` also needs an environment protocol for image requests and platform text metrics (`logic/abi/src/draw.rs` installs an `Env` with neither).
- **A per-axis `scale` (`scale="0 1"`, CSS's x and y)** (2026-10-16, euxy's keep bar, FRICTION 9). Refused on a row (`expected number`) and in a keyframe (`lower-keyframes`, which says so): the kernel's `scale` row is one `f32` (`kernel/tables/schema.json` bit 61) and `exact_motion::Property::Scale` a scalar, so it is the row's codec (vec2), the motion value, the present op's wire, and each host's transform (`PresenterIOS`/`PresenterMac` `v.scale`, Linux `paint/presented.rs`, the web hosts), with press scale folded per axis. The workaround a porter takes is a bar translated inside a clip.
- **A hand-written swipe row in a grouped list, the forms still styled on the scroll** (2026-10-19, `contract/lower/src/swipe_rows.rs` `written`). A grouped section's `scroll swipeContent=…` whose content is a direct child with the same `id` expression is styled as `delete=`'s is: the content is the row (its inset, subtitle and accessory rules), the scroll takes its box in the card. Content inside wrappers (Messages' inbox shape) is read too, by lowering and by the kernel's `grouped/separator.rs` `swiped`. Content in an `if` or `match` arm is read too. Still styled as the row on the scroll: content produced by a `for` in the scroll (several elements of one `id`), an `id` that is another expression evaluating to the same name (known only when it runs), a scroll whose author padded its leading edge or that has a `class`, and two elements with the same `id`.
- **Android's pickers and Kotlin hatches, what LLP 1115.001.000 leaves** (2026-10-16, on a Nothing Phone (4a) Pro). (1) A view a Kotlin hatch hosts takes no touches (as a native module's view on this host). (2) Where Compose is not linked, the framework's dialogs ignore `accent-color`. (3) Not measured on a Pixel, nor with a date control on an app's first screen. (4) `contract verify apps/native-fixture/app.contract` diverges at boot on main too (the `tabs` element: runner `tabs`, Lean `route-second`).
- **Android's top layer presses with a state layer, not the ripple** (2026-11-12, expo/exact#214). The page, the top app bar and the navigation bar ripple (`paint/ripple.rs`); a menu's rows and an alert's buttons (`presenter/top/look.rs`, `Feedback::pressed`) still light the 10% state layer, since their windows are told again only on a change. The ripple needs `top.rs` to tell the open surface at each frame while it moves, as `bars.rs` `ripple_bars` does.
- **LLP 1125 stage 3 (MIDI), what was not run** (2026-10-16): nothing was sent to a real device or the macOS IAC bus (none present), Chrome's Web MIDI was not tried by hand, and the Apple arm's 3 s watchdog and iOS foreground re-enumeration have no test. Two edge cases the last review confirmation left (LLP 1125 As built, stage 3): the Apple arm and `midi-glue.js` forget handed note-on ids after about a second, so a retraction delayed past that (a stop begun while the main thread stalls) retracts a note-off whose note-on already sounded — keep ids until their paired note-off is sent; and an edit at exactly the beat where a full queue parked the fill keeps that beat's skip (`transport.rs` `fill`, `transport.js` `fill`), so a note the edit retracted there is not re-issued — reset the skip when the edit retracts at that beat.
- **The data module's Hermes heap: what is left after boot's collections and the between-calls cap** (2026-10-17, `js/src/shim.cc`, `Adapter::harden`). Hades keeps every young-generation page it has touched and no `GCConfig` field bounds it, so the shim collects before the freeze, between its guard and `harden.js` (vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md), after it, and between calls once the module has allocated at least 512 KB and as much as it holds live. At idle after launch the young generation keeps 528 KB in the template and in euxy (Release, iPhone 17 simulator), about 80 KB of it the segment's own mark bits and cards, the rest the guard's and `harden.js`'s 0.45 MB each; a session of dice rolls in euxy holds it near 720 KB where it reached 3.6 MB. A collection right after the runtime is created saves nothing (measured): the runtime's own 0.26 MB is mostly old generation. Left: (1) the render host's fresh realm per page pays the boot collections (made while a worker is idle) and, for a page whose module allocates past 512 KB, one collection on the request path, with no memory to save since the realm is dropped; measure RealWorld's CPU a page and skip them there if it shows. (2) An answer's reply is serialized with the capture replacer (`ok` in `js/src/prelude.js`), which makes a key string per array element and an ancestor record per object: euxy's seeded library (22 KB as the runner keeps it) allocates 151 KB to settle. A plain `JSON.stringify` first, falling back to the replacer only when the text is 64 Ki characters or longer, would avoid that, but runs getters and `toJSON` twice for such replies. (3) Below 528 KB idle needs a smaller guard or `harden.js` allocation, or a lean VM built with a smaller `HERMESVM_LOG_HEAP_SEGMENT_SIZE` (a CMake cache variable, default 22; objects past a segment go to jumbo segments). Measured 2026-10-18 on euxy (Release, iPhone 17 simulator, the iOS-simulator lean VM rebuilt from the pinned receipt's flags plus that option, linked through `HERMES_LEAN_SYS_DIR_aarch64_apple_ios_sim`): the heap's two segments 1120 KB dirty at 22, 928 KB at 20, 864 KB at 19, and `EUXY_TTI` unchanged (five interleaved launches each, medians 1284, 1285, 1288 ms). Shipping it is a release outside this repo: in expo/ibex, add `-DHERMESVM_LOG_HEAP_SEGMENT_SIZE=20` to the common CMake arguments of `scripts/build-hermes-vanilla-release.sh` and `scripts/build-hermes-windows-vanilla-release.ps1` (it is one option for both VMs), move `RELEASE_TAG` in `.github/workflows/hermes-vanilla-publish.yml` to `-v6`, dispatch `hermes-vanilla-build.yml` on main and let the publisher (environment `hermes-vanilla-release`) attest and publish; then here, the new tag and eleven digests in `vendor/ibex/crates/hermes-lean-sys/build_support.rs` (`RELEASE_TAG`, `PINNED_BUNDLES`), the bundle line of `vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, and the tests that name the tag. No `GCConfig` setting releases young-generation pages: `ShouldReleaseUnused` is read nowhere in `lib/VM`, and `HadesGC::youngGenCollection` resets the segment with `AdviseUnused::No`; patched to `Yes`, the dirty size did not move. (4) Hardening's readers are not all captured before bootstrap: `harden.js` reads `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor`, `getOwnPropertyNames` and the rest when it starts, and the deferred intrinsic snapshot its readers when `capture_intrinsics` runs, so a bootstrap that replaces one with a function that lies is believed (the guard's are captured when `install_with` builds `Reachability`). Capturing them at adapter construction and refusing to harden when the live ones differ would close it (Grok, review of the 2026-10-17 memory change). (5) Why `AdviseUnused::Yes` moved nothing: the lean VM's `oscompat::vm_unused` is `madvise(…, MADV_FREE)` (5, disassembled from the pinned iOS-simulator `libhermesvmlean_a.a`), which on Darwin leaves the pages in the footprint until the system needs them; `MADV_FREE_REUSABLE` (7) takes them out at once, as `releaseStackBelowCaller` does. A VM patch that advises the young generation unused with that after the shim's full collections could give back most of what it has touched at idle (euxy port 12, 2026-10-19: 53 of its segment's pages, 848 KB, about 80 KB of them its own mark bits and cards). That is a Hermes release outside this repo, as (3) is, for about three times (3)'s saving.
- **What euxy's first screen still costs on iOS that its UIKit port does not** (2026-10-16, euxy memory lane, Release, iPhone 17 simulator, idle 10 s after launch: Exact 26.7 MB, UIKit 21.1; later 2026-10-18, a box of flat boxes is one layer tree (LLP 1068 §6.3): `NodeView`s 243 → 116, 0.3 MB less; measure on a simulator no agent drive has touched since it was erased, since a drive leaves accessibility loaded in every app it launches after (about 8 MB more for each, UIKit's port included); 2026-10-18, 25.7 MB against 20.4 (medians of 10 and 5): of the 5.3 MB, malloc 2.75 MB (Rust and Hermes heap 2.85 MB more than UIKit's, NodeView 292 KB, style dictionaries and strings about 0.3 MB, against UIKit's Auto Layout), the Hermes heap 1.1 MB, thread stacks 0.4 MB (an 8 MB owner stack 176 KB dirty, two raster workers, three Rust threads), the executable's `__DATA` and `__DATA_CONST` 0.54 MB, a vImage colour-conversion cache 128 KB that a `CABackdropLayer` filter image makes at commit, and `Network` 80 KB (the app hatch's `onLine` fact starts the path monitor). Text rasters equal UIKit's label backing stores. The larger Rust items, after the 2026-10-18 cuts (the mirrors' style texts shared, 322 KB to 122 KB; the site index's constant cells kept only for the bindings evaluated, 96 KB; taffy's caches allocating measure slots as used, 137 KB to 50 KB): the plan's tables and 1,331 strings decoded from the linked plan (0.21 MB: 64, 48 and 32 KB table vectors, 40 KB of strings in 1,331 allocations under a 32 KB `Vec<String>`; a pooled `Strings` type would save about 45 KB and reading them in place from the static bytes 80 KB, across some 60 `plan.strings` sites, most in tests), the runner's site index (0.19 MB) and realized instances (0.3 MB), the kernel's 94 interned `StyleProps` at 896 bytes (82 KB), the node arena's and taffy's node vectors (0.23 MB, up to half of it growth slack); Swift's: CSS animation specs kept as `[String: Any]` per animated node (115 dictionaries, 0.1 MB, and their lowered arrays).) Beyond the Hermes heap (above; 2.4 MB dirty then, 1.4 MB since 2026-10-17, plus 0.6 MB of its malloc) and the executable's dirtied `__DATA`/`__DATA_CONST` (1.3 MB more), one smaller thing is Exact's own: each `NodeView` registers for trait changes itself (`_UITraitChangeRegistry`, about 64 KB over euxy's 134 views). Text rasters are not it: ink-cropped BGRA bitmaps (0.74 MB) against UILabel backing stores, 1.18 MB of both kinds of pixels to UIKit's 0.99 (A8 needs a mask layer, below). 2026-10-19, after the bindings' bytecode read where it is linked (91 KB), the owner's stack under the boot given back (144 KB), the plan's strings read in its bytes and the VM's string values made as read (the binary's own malloc 2.87 to 2.68 MB): 26,210 to 25,730 KB (`footprint` bytes, medians of 5 interleaved launches on a quiet machine; 26,210 to 25,874 over 10 pairs), `EUXY_TTI` 1181 to 1175 ms (medians of 10). Measured there and left: the app's own `apple/src/lib.rs` still passes `BYTECODE.to_vec()` (76 KB; an app made before 8471fb5f9 keeps copying until its entry passes `APP_BYTECODE`, and `exact.mjs update` does not rewrite it); the executable's `__DATA_CONST` (304 KB) and `__DATA` (19 of 23 pages) are dirtied by fixups and ObjC metadata, so only fewer pointer-bearing constants would cut them; the runtime `Entry` is one 48 KB allocation (`Bridge` holds `Host` and a `PreparedHost` inline); taffy's slotmaps grow by doubling in `new_leaf` (80, 20 and 14 KB buffers); the kernel's 94 interned `StyleProps` at 896 B; Hermes keeps 105 chunks of pinned JSI values (47 KB) from the boot's peak; two raster workers start with the first image source and sit on 16 KB each. 2026-10-19, later, against the SwiftUI port on one fresh simulator (`footprint`, medians of 5 launches in 5 interleaved rounds): 25.17 MiB to 24.33, SwiftUI's 24.91; `EUXY_TTI` 1153 to 1133 ms (medians of 10, three rounds); playing 0.1% CPU either way. The cuts, in `malloc_history` against SwiftUI's: CoreUI kept every symbol the kernel measured, its glyph's SVG documents about 120 KB a symbol (SwiftUI's whole CoreUI is 1.05 MB), so symbols in a box covered whole (the closed pattern menu's six) are measured when a layout places it, and the root tablist a tab bar replaces is covered from boot (`closed.rs` `cover_root_tablist`; the presenter adopts the cover or gives it back), which also ends `ButtonMeasureCache`'s tab probes; `GCMouse` and its notifications are asked only on an iPad or a Mac (GameController's keyboard and mouse service, 0.26 MB of malloc and 90 KB of `__DATA`); the raster workers and Ibex's host-task workers give their stacks back when they go idle (Ibex local patch 17); the launch image's digest is CommonCrypto's (CryptoKit's `__DATA`, 40 KB). What Exact still spends beyond SwiftUI, by `footprint` category: text rasters in `CG Raster Data` 0.62 MB, less SwiftUI's 0.48 MB more `CoreAnimation` backing stores; malloc 0.34 MB (`NodeView` 285 × 1,280 B, 365 KB, its stored properties beyond `UIView`'s; Rust 1.5 MB and Hermes 0.4 MB, of which taffy's slotmap doubling 0.16 MB, 30 hashbrown tables 0.15 MB, the kernel's interned `StyleProps` 93 KB, the runtime `Entry` 48 KB, Hermes' pinned-value and weak-slot chunks 92 KB; `Presenter.setLanguage` gives every view an `accessibilityLanguage`, an associated object each, 59 KB); stacks 0.18 MB (the owner's, the raster workers', Hades', Hermes' finalizer executor's, `exact-io-0`'s); the vImage cache 128 KB; the executable's `__DATA_CONST` 0.13 MB more. SwiftUI's own `__DATA` is 1.6 MB more than Exact's. `Network` (80 KB of `__DATA`) is the app hatch's: `appFacts` reads `onLine`, which starts the path monitor, whether or not a hatch reads it. 2026-10-19, euxy port 12 against its UIKit port on one fresh simulator (25,138 against 20,162 KB, `footprint` medians of 5): the Hermes heap 1.37 MB and its malloc 0.4 MB (its young generation's touched pages are (5) of the entry above); Rust's malloc 1.42 MB (runner 340 KB, kernel 323, taffy 208, plan 168, `exact_apple` 158, triomphe 113, `exact_num` 58, Ibex 52); the Sequencer tab's navigation controller 0.93 MB (the entry at the top); the presenter's Swift objects about 0.9 MB (`NodeView` 250 × 1,280 B, style dictionaries and their `BatchValue` arrays 186 KB, CSS animation specs 70 KB, strings 102 KB) against 0.3 MB more of UIKitCore's own in the UIKit port; Swift's runtime metadata 0.35 MB, mostly the navigation controller's SwiftUI; the executable's `__DATA` and `__DATA_CONST` 0.64 MB; stacks 0.16 MB; the keyboard machinery 0.11 to 0.22 MB (the first-responder entry below); the vImage cache 128 KB; `Network` 80 KB. Text is in Exact's favour: its `CG Raster Data` 0.4 MB more, the UIKit port's label backing stores 1.0 MB more. The UIKit port loads its audio engine at launch (about 0.2 MB) and links no GameController, whose images cost Exact nothing dirty. 2026-10-19, later (euxy port 12, Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, `footprint` medians of 5 interleaved launches, each change against the one before): 25,090 to 24,626 KB in four cuts. The path monitor starts at the first read of `onLine` (the plan's, or a module's `app.onLine` through the hatch host table's `on_line`), so `Network` is no longer loaded: -161 KB (`__DATA` -109). A node answers `accessibilityLanguage` from its presenter, as UIKit stored it per view: -80 KB (20 KB of it malloc). A `NodeView` is a 768-byte block, from 1,024 (its flags together, the transform, image, paint ghosts and scroll extent in `NodeAside`, the paragraph raster in `TextSpecs`, one shared first-draw receipt): -80 KB (102 KB of malloc). A box's CSS animations are kept as `CssAnimation` structs, not the batch's dictionaries: -144 KB (70 KB of malloc). `EUXY_TTI` 1265 to 1251 ms (medians of 10), playing CPU 0.075 to 0.05 %. Tried and dropped: reserving a batch's creates in the node arena and Taffy measured 176 KB worse, since the first screen's batch is followed by smaller ones that double the exact capacity past the power of two the pushes reached; it needs the final count, which the kernel does not know at the first batch. 2026-10-20, euxy port 15 against its UIKit port on one fresh simulator (`footprint` bytes, medians of 5 interleaved launches: UIKit 21,317,936, Exact 24,643,960): only a `color-scheme` boundary overrides its appearance (the root's scheme was inherited by every node and each of 312 views carried its own trait overrides, trait collections and associated object) and `applyProps` sets accessibility properties only on change: 24,021,368, `EUXY_TTI` 1167 to 1165 ms (medians of 10), playing CPU 0.1% either way. Tried and dropped: watching a tab stack's pops at its first show and laying out only loaded stacks in `replayHatches`, so a tab not shown loads no navigation view: 166 KB less live malloc (`malloc_history`), yet the footprint median 130 KB higher over 10 interleaved pairs. What is left beyond the UIKit port, by `footprint` category: malloc 2.1 MB (Rust 1.8 MB: runner 565 KB, kernel 518, plan 208, `exact_apple` 195, taffy 177, `exact_js_value` 129; Hermes 0.4 MB; NodeView instances 312 × 768 B); the Hermes heap 1.1 MB (the entry above); the executable's dirty `__DATA` and `__DATA_CONST` 0.57 MB more than the port's (Swift type metadata about 0.3 MB; Rust's relro 144 KB, of which the kernel's generated `METRICS`, 171 rows of `&str` cells, is 34 KB: names and text cells as offsets into one string would leave it in `__TEXT`); stacks 0.26 MB (`exact.owner` 64 KB, two raster workers 32 KB each, `ibex2-host-task` 32 KB, `exact-io-0`, `exact.kept`, Hades and Hermes' finalizer executor 16 KB each, all idle after boot); the vImage cache 128 KB, which is UIKit's scroll edge effect (a 1 × 384 Generic Gray mask converted at commit) under euxy's scroller, the platform's own. The larger Rust items: the plan's 27 table vectors decoded from the linked plan (153 KB; read in place they would cost nothing), the node arena's columns and Taffy's three slotmaps grown by doubling (85 and 114 KB), the site index's `Deps` (186 KB), the data module's reply values (110 KB of triomphe arrays), the kept answer copied into `Store::values` (48 KB), the 90 interned `StyleProps` at 896 B (88 KB). Of the hashbrown tables, Taffy's `node_context_data` is the largest (48 and 12 KB: a `SparseSecondaryMap` of 72-byte `MeasureContext`s, one per measured leaf), then the theme index (24 KB) and the measurer's memo (20 KB). Text is in Exact's favour (`CG Raster Data` and `CoreAnimation` 0.9 MB against the port's 1.65 MB).
- **Euxy port 18's non-object malloc on iOS, what is left after the 2026-10-21 cuts** (Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, idle 10 s after launch; `heap -s` against the UIKit port: non-object 5.00 MB in 25,250 blocks against 2.72 MB; `MallocStackLogging=lite`, `malloc_history -allBySize` through `c++filt`). The difference is Rust (1.9 MB before the cuts) and Hermes' own malloc (about 0.4 MB: code blocks 100 KB, pinned-value and weak-slot chunks 90 KB, the identifier table 48 KB). The default zone also holds 3.2 MB free in pages it keeps against the UIKit port's 2.4 MB; `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` returns none of it (no page is wholly free), and an untouched `calloc` (the MIDI input ring, 80 KB) costs nothing. Cut: the dependency table holds each distinct read set once (194 to 78 KB), the plan's `bindings` and `nodes` are read in the linked plan's bytes (162 to 34 KB), a node's `notEnabled` trait is set only on change; footprint 24,430,968 to 24,160,608 bytes idle and 25,373,036 to 25,094,508 playing, `EUXY_TTI` 1232 to 1229 ms. Then a realized `Child` at 128 bytes: Apple's allocator rounds a request above 128 bytes up to a class (160, 192, 224, …), and about 450 one-child vectors moved down one; footprint 24,217,976 to 23,996,780 bytes idle (medians of 12) and 25,233,784 to 24,906,104 playing (9), warm `EUXY_TTI` 1227 to 1222 ms. How to measure: install each build in turn over one bundle id (one data container) and rotate the order each round. The same binary under two bundle ids read up to 350 KB apart, and the first build of a round read 50 to 200 KB high; footprint moves in 16 KB pages with a spread of about 150 KB, so a cut under about 100 KB shows only in `heap -s`. Tried and not kept: Hermes' `kReleaseUnusedYoungOnFull` (this Hades has no path that releases memory: no change); a full collection between calls after every 32 KB allocated (Hermes' dirty 1,728 to 1,504 KB, the default zone up about as much: footprint flat); every Rust allocation in a malloc zone of its own (the default zone 1.7 MB less dirty, the Rust zone 2.6 MB at 39% free: 0.9 MB worse, so the default zone's free space is mostly not Rust's); Swift's style decoder sharing nested values and long strings across styles (`BatchValue` arrays 571 to 162, string storage 1,297 to 799, live heap 77 KB less, footprint 150 KB more). 2026-10-21, later, euxy port 20 (one bundle id, rotated order; live heap by `heap -s` with no stack logging, since `MallocStackLogging` moves blocks up a size class; medians of 12): a node keeps the last values of only the bindings that can change (3,652 of euxy's 3,974 are constant and read from the site index), -45 KB idle and -98 KB playing; `aspect_ratio`, `column_width` and `placeholder_color` are rare rows (`StyleProps` 864 B, the 896-byte class), -27 and -23; a `Mirror` is 128 B with static prop names, -34 and -40; Swift's `StyleCache` keeps two hashes of a style's bytes, not the bytes, -26 and -43; Taffy's grid fields and `multicol` are allocated only when set (`Style` 256 B, EXACT PATCH 62), -20 and -28. Together -155 KB idle and -232 KB playing; `footprint` 23,832,940 to 23,398,764 bytes idle and 24,643,936 to 24,201,580 playing (spread about 150 KB); `EUXY_TTI` 1,138.5 to 1,136.5 ms (medians of 20). Tried and dropped: reserving 512 slots in the arena columns and Taffy's slotmaps at the first create measured no different (medians of 8), so the doubling slack in those large blocks is not resident: their untouched pages are never dirtied, and only the bytes written count. Left, by owner (KB): Hermes' young generation, whose pages Hades keeps at their high-water (960 dirty, set by Ibex's hardening, 966 KB allocated in two steps, and euxy's `factoryPatterns` call, 550 KB); the runner's realized children 125 (a `Child` is 128 B, the `NodeInst` inline; its rarely set `last_surface`, `collection` and `implied` behind one pointer would bring it to 104) and its constant values 39 (a `BTreeMap` of the bindings evaluated), the kernel's interned styles 82 (88 `StyleProps` of 864 B; under 752 B they would take the 768-byte class, but what is left is common or inherited, `FontVariations` 68 B inline by design), Taffy's caches 86 and replay records 48 (one hashbrown table of `Replay`s, each a whole `LayoutInput` and `LayoutOutput`), the host's mirrors 64 and their props maps 25, the host's `StyleTexts` 48 (the presenter's cache no longer copies them), the store's kept bytes 48 (two values read whole, beside their parsed copies), the data module's reply values 77, the plan's other decoded tables 34 (`LENT_TABLES` in plan/build.rs takes more), the runtime `Entry` 48, the theme index 24. Swift's typed objects are the rest of the gap: `NodeView` 256 × 768 B, style dictionaries and `BatchValue` arrays 175 KB, and the associated object table at 96 KB (UIKit associates one per first `addSubview` of a view, so it follows the view count). 2026-10-21, later still, euxy port 21 (same method, medians of 10 to 12): a realized `Child` is 112 B, its list engine, surface inputs and implied rows in one boxed `Rare` (-12 KB idle, -18 to -25 playing); a style's own arrays and objects, its keys and long ASCII strings are shared across styles by their bytes in `BatchReader`, and a style with an escape is no longer cached (-50 and -60; footprint flat within its spread, as the earlier two-bundle-id trial's 150 KB more was not); a parent whose children all paint at rank 0 keeps no `DensePaintRanks` table (115 on the first screen; -15 and -12). `footprint` 23,267,680 to 23,128,428 bytes idle and 24,643,948 to 24,291,692 playing; `EUXY_TTI` 1,234.5 to 1,229.5 ms (spread about 20). Measured and left: the kernel's interned `StyleProps` reach the 768-byte class only at 752 B or less, which no single row move gives; `Paint::Url`'s id and fallback boxed (-32 for `fill` and `stroke`), `MarkerRef` thin (-24) and `font_variation_settings` boxed in the style row (-60, though runs carry `FontVariations` by value) together make 744 B, for about 12 KB. Taffy's `Replay` table holds about 220 records of 120 B in a 256-bucket table (33 KB written): boxing the record saves under 5 KB. The runner's constants as a sorted vector would save about 15 KB at an O(n) insert per constant. `TextSpecs` is 1,280 B (25 on euxy) for its two inline `Spec`s, each with an inline strut `Run`.
- **Euxy's footprint grows 14 MB when its Patterns tab first opens** (2026-10-17, development build on an iPhone 17 simulator, driven by the agent: 26.9 MB on the Sequencer tab after playing, 40 to 45 MB once Patterns and Settings have been shown, about 42 MB after; Hermes is not it). Unmeasured on a Release build and not yet attributed: find what the list's first presentation keeps (`vmmap` by region, then `malloc_history`).
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/d3): euxy is not on this Mac; Caltrain Release on an iPhone 18 Pro, driven by real touches, loses about 11 MB on first list presentation (CoreAnimation, CG raster and CG image allocations) and returns to 32 KB above launch; no host leak shown, so nothing changed.
- **Files in a share on Android, and what LLP 1125 D12 left unseen** (2026-10-16, stage 5): Android refuses `share(files=…)` by name (`host/painted/src/presenter/requests.rs`); carrying it needs a `FileProvider` in the Canvas host's manifest, the presenter's app roots as content URIs on an `ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE`, and a picture from the Canvas renderer. Not seen: a real browser's `navigator.share({files})` (only a fake navigator), the outcome line of a file picked in a real Apple sheet, and Safari's activation window after the store reads. An Apple share's scratch PNG directory stays in the temporary directory when the app quits with the sheet open. Two sessions of one app in one process (the sample host, LLP 1031) share one set of pinned app roots, keyed by the roots' paths: if the app's data directory is swapped between their activations, the first session's share reads the second's pinned tree (Astra's confirmation of `46efcc152`; keep the handles per storage activation).
- **Lean navigation tabs still need reload carry** (2026-10-17, LLP 1075.003 §9.14).
