Model `carry_panels`: retain a built panel’s component state across reload even when that panel is not rebuilt at the reload, including successive reloads before its next selection. `runner/tests/it/tabs.rs` covers it; the semantics has no reload at all (`Contract/Runtime.lean`, `Contract/CompSem.lean`, the observation's events), so a reload comes first. Prerender's `build_every_tab` is modelled (`Oracle.everyTab`, the corpus's `(prerendered)` variants).
- **LLP 1125 D11's host wake locks** (2026-10-16, stage 1b): Android's kind-8 `setKeepScreenOn` was written and not run on a device or emulator, and neither web target's lock was seen held in a real browser's wake lock (the glue's test stands a counting `navigator.wakeLock` in). Natively an element the host shows as its own control (a header in the navigation bar, a confirmation as a native alert, a menu as a native menu) holds nothing, because a whole host cover marks both what the host hides and what it shows natively and the kernel cannot tell them apart; the web holds there.
- **The wasm page still loses a `play` or `ended` an autoplaying element fires before its data executor is ready** (2026-10-16). The media glue now attaches once `moduleReady` resolves and reports the metadata, duration, opening seek, `canplay` and an error the element already has (Video Player's lost opening duration, reproduced on main with activation delayed 1.5 s: 37 failures, 0 after). The presenter sets `autoplay` as an attribute at once, so a muted autoplaying element can start, and a short one end, before then; before this change the report callback dropped those too. Holding the attribute until attaching does not work (HTML acts on `autoplay` only on reaching HAVE_ENOUGH_DATA); reporting `play`/`ended` on attaching would.
  Tried 2026-10-16 and parked (branch `q1013/w5-wasm-autoplay-events` on the Mac Studio, four review rounds). Inferring what already happened from `paused`/`ended`/`readyState`/`played`/`seeking` at attach and suppressing the queued duplicates failed every round (a `waiting` before attach, seeks, a pause during attach, HTML's completion interval). Recording the events instead (a recorder set where `glue.js` and `host/web-js/media.js` give the element `autoplay`, replayed in order by `media-glue.js`) is small and fixes those, but replay still needs: recorded history dropped when the source changes or reloads (else an old source's `error` reaches the new one), recorded `timeupdate` not written back through a `currentTime` binding (it rewinds the playhead), the attach-time `ended` guard not consuming a `paused` true→false the app set before attach, and no `currentTime` write while `readyState` is 0 during replay. Take the recorder, with those four, as the next attempt.
  Tried again 2026-10-17 with the recorder and parked (branch `q24/wasm-media-early-play` on expo-build-450b, four commits, four review rounds). The recorder holds: a one-line `recordMedia` at the end of `host/web/navigation.js` (the only boot module with room; glue.js is at 1,499 lines), replay through `media-glue.js`'s listener path, history dropped on a `src` change, `load()` or `emptied`, a replayed `timeupdate` kept from seeking the player back, retirement during replay, the opening seek reported as main reports it; 47 media tests pass on the branch. What did not converge is replaying `play`/`playing`/`pause`/`ended` into handlers that write `paused`: the replay arrives after the app moved, so history and the app's newer request collide, and every rule tried (per-event versions, a pending-request counter) met a new case in review: a request made by a replay handler during the replay, a request the element already meets (no fresh `play()`/`pause()` event follows), a `pause`/`ended` HTML still has queued when the listeners attach. Next attempt, if any: deliver the early reports in order with the app's own inputs (the runner or glue queues them while `inputReady` is false, as one timeline) instead of replaying them at attach.

- **iOS cold start: euxy's Exact port still reaches its boot animation's end later than its SwiftUI and UIKit ports** (2026-10-16, Release, a fresh iPhone 17 simulator, 10 interleaved launches to `EUXY_TTI`: Exact 1455 ms, SwiftUI 1221, UIKit 1317; process start to `main` is not it: Exact 173 to 187 ms, UIKit 3 to 8 ms less, SwiftUI about 20 ms more, with the same 506 images loaded as UIKit's; what Exact has more is its executable's own 34,000 chained fixups against 6,600, mostly Rust and Swift pointer tables in `__DATA_CONST`, and stripping its symbols changes nothing measurable). Exact's first draw is about 195 ms after `main`, the boot animation's 900 ms follow it. On main in that first frame, by Time Profiler: (1) `UINavigationBar` loads SwiftUI on first use (30 to 60 ms in the simulator); background-loading it does not help, its image registration holding the Objective-C runtime lock while main waits. (2) euxy's module builds an `AVAudioPlayerNode` and sets the audio session's category in `init`, made with its `firstFrame` views (about 15 ms; `docs/recipes/native-views.md` now says not to). The routes' blocks no longer take a batch of their own: a resize lays out its viewport and route blocks in one batch, which measured no total-startup gain on the simulator. What is left: (1), (2), and the interleaved `EUXY_TTI` comparison again, on a physical iPhone.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/d3): euxy and its hand-built ports are not on this Mac; measured on Native Fixture instead: a route resize applies two batches (median 11.2 ms, 2 navigation syncs); skipping navigation projection during geometry-only reports did not reduce the syncs and was reverted. A fix needs the view and route blocks under one runtime layout with UIKit's containing bounds established first.
- **Exact's game holds 160 MB of graphics memory on a Pixel 5 against the WebView's 40** (2026-10-16, `game/bench/README.md`, "Memory and CPU at 60 Hz"). The direct path's 4× MSAA targets are already `TRANSIENT_ATTACHMENT`, but this driver (2020-11-30) has no lazily allocated memory type and no `VK_EXT_multisampled_render_to_single_sampled`, the Vulkan form of the GLES extension that keeps the WebView's MSAA in tile memory; a newer Adreno driver may have either, and wgpu-hal would need the second added. The hardware-buffer ring's six slots are another 60 MB: five collapse 80,000 cubes to 26 fps.
- **Exact's occlusion at 256,000 cubes on a Pixel 5: 30.6 fps exact, 35.0 with unshrunk occluders** (2026-10-16, `game/render/README.md`, "Occlusion"). Shrinking each occluder by a texel keeps the frame identical but costs a texel-wide rim of every small far cube: 42,000 cubes drawn against 18,000. Closing the gap without giving up exactness: occluders drawn from an 8-vertex mesh (the pass's 4.7 ms is vertex work), a cull that reads less than two 40-byte transform records per item (it is memory-bound: 9.1 ms), and presenting the canvas's buffers through `ASurfaceControl` rather than drawing them as a bitmap in the reader's pass (2 ms of GPU a frame, measured by not drawing it).
- **A 60 Hz tick of 256,000 moving entities takes 5–6 ms on a Pixel 5, bound by memory** (2026-10-16, the cubes bench's `tick`): 56 bytes read and 40 written per entity, and its 10 MB upload another 3 ms. Two ticks and two uploads then fill a display frame. Copying the pages into staging on another core while the next tick runs is the next lever.
- **Castle Town faults the Pixel 5's GPU, and Android does not recover a lost device** (2026-10-17, castle-fixgpu). Since round 10 the Adreno driver reports recovered GPU faults in the module's context (`IOCTL_KGSL_GPU_COMMAND ... errno 71`, the error KGSL gives a context's next submission after a fault): cold starts with `screenrecord` running, round 9's freeplay build 0 of 5 runs, round 10's 3 of 5, rounds 13–16 and this branch every run (2 to 8 a run; fewer without `screenrecord`). About one cold start in nine the device is then lost; that used to abort the process (vendored wgpu-hal patch 12 fixes the abort), and now the canvas stops, because the Android presenter never calls `gpu_recover` nor attaches its windows again. Round 10's "absent shadows on launch" and round 13's "buffered canvases stop producing frames" below may be the same faults; the round 16 SPIR-V validation errors (workgroup array strides) are a lead. Bisect round 10 (`c96ac1c9d` render scale, `15edcd90d` ground cut, or what they exposed), and give the Android presenter recovery as Apple's has (`Module::recover` reattaches Apple layers; an Android canvas would need its `ANativeWindow` attached again). Logcats: the castle-fixgpu lane's scratchpad `fixgpu-phone/`.
- **What castle-fixgpu left in the GPU module** (2026-10-17). (1) Under the agent's clock a canvas acquires on the module's thread and waits for the image, so an Android `surfaceDestroyed` arriving then waits on the compositor through `Owner.sync`; live frames no longer do (detach hangs up on the acquisition thread, a resize waits for its image to land). Attaching a window joins retired acquisition threads first (Android refuses a second swapchain on a window still connected to one), bounded by wgpu's one-second acquire timeout. (2) The cached sun-shadow copy kept for GL and WebGL2 (`shadow_cache::seeds`) is tested only by forcing it on Metal; exact2 compiles no GL backend today.
- **WebGPU canvases configure as opaque whatever they promise** (2026-10-17, castle-fixgpu2; Astra's review of castle-fixgpu, finding 4). The pinned wgpu 30.0.1 WebGPU backend lists only `Opaque` in a canvas's capabilities (`src/backend/webgpu.rs:4176`) although its `configure` maps `PreMultiplied` to the browser's premultiplied mode (`:4231`), so `target::alpha_mode` picks `Opaque` for every browser canvas and a translucent surface loses its alpha over page content. List `PreMultiplied` there (a vendored patch, as for wgpu-hal), keeping the module's choice among listed modes.
- **Castle Town 90 Hz headroom** (2026-10-17, `game/bench/README.md`, round 16). In-pass sun-cache seeding (`e922c05f7`) saves 0.184/0.182 ms in views 0/1 and preserves fixed-pose pixels. Final full-resolution 1080×2340, 24 players: view 0 is 89.39 fps with five missed slots in ten seconds, view 7 misses one, all others miss none. View 0 mean delivery is 11.187 ms, about 0.076 ms over the nominal budget before variance headroom. Omitting avatars plus hulls removes 0.772 ms of its 1.266 ms camera-cost difference against view 2; separate body and hull cost next. COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT acquire waits leave the median ~0.23 ms GPU gap and do not improve fps; latency 3 allocates five images (default already four) and regresses. Keep the prepass, Depth32Float, 35 m shadows and full resolution. Hardware utilization and the remaining gap's cause remain unmeasured.
- **Pixel 5 Vulkan SPIR-V validation errors in the existing game shaders** (2026-10-17, Castle round 16). Khronos Android validation 1.4.363.0 reports `VUID-StandaloneSpirv-None-10684` for workgroup array strides and `VUID-VkShaderModuleCreateInfo-pCode-08737` for a vector constant with mismatched component types. Both occur with the original TOP_OF_PIPE acquire path as well as the rejected COLOR path; synchronization validation reports no SYNC hazards. Reproduce and fix the generated SPIR-V, then rerun validation. Evidence: `castle-port:game/games/castle-town/artifacts/perf/r16/validation-summary.json` and its logcats.
- **Android buffered GPU canvases sometimes stop producing frames** (2026-10-17, Castle round 13). The baseline attempt, 30 m trial at view 8 and original-assets 35 m tour at view 5 stalled while the app stayed in front and the phone awake; CPU fell to about 52 ms/s and no next tour log arrived. Relaunches run normally. No cause is established; invalid rows are excluded. Three SurfaceView tours completed in round 13; no foreground stall recurred in round 14, whose bench captures debuggerd and logcat before restarting a stalled run. Evidence: `castle-port:game/games/castle-town/artifacts/perf/r13/final-buffer-stall-logcat.txt` and the receipt.
- **Castle Town intermittent absent shadows on launch** (2026-10-17, round 10). The first 0.9-scale tour lacked shadows; the identical APK restored them on relaunch. The forward shaders match the full-size build, and the failed launch still reports a sun pass. Its performance rows are marked visually rejected. Investigate startup/resource ordering; no cause is established. Evidence: `castle-port:game/games/castle-town/artifacts/perf/r10/{shadow-compare,shadow-repeat}.png`.
- **A GPU canvas over children draws nothing on the Android reader** (2026-10-16, Pixel 5). With Caltrain's module now packaged (`agent-android.mjs build caltrain --apk`), its sky (`glass`, a canvas whose content is its children) is blank and logs nothing, while the cubes game's world (a canvas without children) draws. Likely cause, read and not confirmed: the Linux host's presenter hands each child to `gpu_child_view` with no pixels (`host/painted/src/surfaces.rs`), and Android runs that presenter. Capture the children's pixels there, or have the reader hand the module its own layer of them.
- **Document watches still owed after LLP 1069.010.000 stage 1** (2026-10-16): iOS has none (`NSFilePresenter` on the document's URL, a vnode source for local files; `host/apple/src/doc_watch.rs` is FSEvents on macOS only); a Rust source's `doc:` watch on the web JS target announces nothing, because no Rust topic is wired there (`native.watch` from Rust is not either); the agent cannot announce a change (`changed <path>`, D2); `exactPage().canWatchFiles` is not built (see the RFC's "As built").

The same runs twice failed a render doctest with `E0463` (the logic crate not found), which a focused rebuild passed; those runs shared the SDK's `game/target`, which `test-rust` no longer defaults to. - **iOS: what a popover over a dialog's sheet still lacks** (2026-10-18). A popover a modal `dialog` holds is drawn over that dialog's UIKit sheet, in a `PopoverLayer` in the sheet's presentation container (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PopoverLayerIOS.swift`); `SurfacesIOS.swift` lists the session's surfaces and resolves a view's controller, and whatever enumerates either goes through it. Only a dialog that holds a popover hosts it, which is narrower than HTML: inertness does not decide paint order, and a popover opened later outside the modal paints above it there though inert ([whatwg/html#11195](https://github.com/whatwg/html/issues/11195)); here it stays under the sheet. A native screen in such a popover has no parent controller: UIKit raises `UIViewControllerHierarchyInconsistency` for a parent whose view does not hold the layer. the popover follows its sheet's scroll (2026-10-22) but a drag of the sheet itself only at the next batch; inertness authored around the popover inside the dialog does not cross the lift; content taller than the room above the keyboard needs its own scrolling.
- **Finish native clear-colour projection (LLP 1115.000 D2).** Button faces select title rows by value mask, losing explicit clear-only colour intent: UIKit retains accent and AppKit retains platform ink instead of the inherited colour.   2026-10-22 (lane q1022/f): button faces, UIKit titles (a transparent bar composed with its title ink) and Mac toolbar buttons (symbol and title ink resolved independently) now carry an authored colour, `transparent` included; what is left is the Mac window title's ink, withdrawn after three rounds (a436d42db7, "Withdraw the unqualified Mac window-title projection").

- **`hover` and `pan` on a presented route's logical ancestor are not carried into the sheet** (2026-10-14, LLP 1123 stage 1). Pointer observers, `contextmenu` and `dblclick` on an ancestor hear a sheet's touches through its mount (`MountRelay`); `hover` does not: the ancestor's own `UIHoverGestureRecognizer` ends as an iPad pointer moves onto the sheet, and a mount-level one would race it within the same event, so the ancestor would flicker between hovered and not. Wanted: one owner of the innermost-hover decision across the document and its presentations, then the relay. A `pan` on such an ancestor would compete with the sheet's own detent drag and scrollers; it needs arbitration rules before a relay, and the agent's pan follows the real touch's physical lookup meanwhile. A `swiperight` on such an ancestor stays out: it moves the node it is on, which is behind the modal sheet.
- **LLP 1123 stage 5 owes the keyboard design review's findings** (2026-10-13, Codex's review of stage 1's keyboard handling). Stage 1 only ends a sheet's route at its keyboard layout guide; the RFC's stage 5 lists what D7's executor must answer: scroller inset versus wrapper accommodation, owner-local occupancy for reveal and the toolbar, policy precedence before a sheet's keyboard may leave the document alone, undocked and floating keyboards, reveal for every editor, and composing with `PinnedRoom` and the toolbar.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/g, a699805429): a native module's UIKit editor resolves to its node and reveal uses the native caret; a keyboard toolbar reads the keyboard's window position with zero overlap; hiding the keyboard removes only the toolbar's room. D7's accommodation executor, a `keyboardAvoidance` row, floating/split-keyboard avoidance and the fixed-editor owner wait on Charlie's decisions (listed in the program log of 2026-10-22).
  2026-10-23 (panel 2, Astra, Grok 4.7, Opus; `~/lanes/q1022/panel2/`): no `keyboardAvoidance` row before acceptance; the executor ships as implicit behaviour and removes `OwnersIOS.block`'s guide cut in the same change. A fixed-position editor's residual overlap shortens the innermost owner whose block is its fixed containing block, once, only under effective `resizes-content`; under `overlays-content` it stays covered, as on the web. Ruled (Charlie, 2026-10-23): an undocked, floating or split keyboard gets nothing beyond the editor's own caret scrolling (`followsUndockedKeyboard` is false by default, and no UIKit app moves content sideways); the both-axis reveal this bullet promises is withdrawn, to be revisited only if a real iPad shows a caret covered that a hand-built app would reveal.
- **The canvas through a trusted tunnel** (2026-10-19, stage 10 B2; theme2's Q3 ruling). `--trust-tunnel <port>` carries the design wire only: C's canvas gateway is TLS-only in stage 10, so a developer working through a tunnel edits the design without the canvas (LLP 1122 D12 allows it). Wanted, if asked for: a second tunnel port for the gateway (`--trust-tunnel <port>[,<canvas port>]`), with the gateway's grant and cookie rules unchanged.

- **SVG lengths, what the viewport-unit change leaves** (2026-10-13, SVG viewport review; main already has these differences). (a) Static math and environment geometry: `host/web/src/element.rs:767` omits dimensions other than points, percent and the four admitted viewport units. A nested SVG's `height="calc(10vh + 10px)"` or `height="env(viewport-segment-height 1 0, 25%)"` uses the initial viewport on the web, including its document, while native resolves the length. `min()`/`max()`/`clamp()`, safe-area `env()` and `sv`/`lv`/`dv` units have the same gap unless the kernel simplifies them to points or percent. Dynamic JS geometry keeps main's CSS path (`host/web-js/svg-transform.js:13`): a nested SVG's CSS `height` does not size its viewport, while a `foreignObject` takes it; browser support for segment `env()` fallbacks also differs. (b) Paint-server templates: omitting an undefined dimension (`host/web/src/element.rs:767`) exposes the `href` template's value. A radial gradient with `href="#base"` and `cx="env(viewport-segment-width 1 0)"`, where `base` has `cx="20%"`, uses 20% on the web when segment (1, 0) is absent; the kernel selects the authored row and uses its initial 50% (`kernel/src/svg/server.rs:136`, `kernel/src/svg/server.rs:176`). (d) Static font-relative geometry and hairlines: the kernel converts `em`/`rem` and `-exact-hairline` to points before `host/web/src/element.rs:748`; main emits these numbers rather than omitting them. The JS template (`host/web/src/template.rs:56`) keeps the build's pixels: a nested `svg height="2em"` stays 32 when its font size changes, and a pattern's static hairline stays 1 at 2x, while native and the wasm host recompute them. Dynamic JS hairlines use main's CSS resolution steps, so 1.25x uses 1 CSS px instead of native's 0.8.

- **Chrome retains SVG viewport attribute geometry after window resize** (2026-10-13, SVG viewport verification; browser limitation reproduced without Exact). In an 800x600 window, a fixed 400x400 SVG containing `<svg width="10vw" height="10vh"><rect width="100%" height="100%"/></svg>` draws at 80x60. After resizing the window to 200x400, Chrome's length API reports 20x40 but its drawn box remains 80x60; WebKit and Firefox update it. The literal viewport attributes written by `host/web/src/element.rs:754` and `host/web-js/svg-transform.js:12` inherit this limitation. Main omitted static viewport attributes and wrote dynamic geometry as CSS, so this is a limitation of the newly admitted form, not a regression of a working main case. Native scenes resolve against the resized window.

- **Resolved: EX3 native unbound-handle logout — one-round waiver (2026-10-12).** The coordinator acting for the repo owner granted exactly one additional round after the RULES.md three-round limit, with a stop-if-NOT-READY instruction. The real-server regression covers private cached reads, authenticated and manual-headers siblings, same-account relogin, and old-handle closure. Full live suite: 24 passed / 285 assertions; dev and serve scratch browser tests passed. Both fresh confirmation reviews are READY: Codex's and Muse's (`llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-waiver.codex.md`, `llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-waiver.muse.md`). The P1 is closed; no further round taken. This resolved record is retained at the coordinator's explicit request. Original finding: round 3 (`llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-r3.codex.md`).

- **A shell's wrong directory still costs a build a minute** (2026-10-11, wave 2: 019 048 061 093 094 096 099). `exact.mjs` resolves a named file from either directory, but the slips left are shell redirects (`cat > app/app.contract` from inside the app): bash refuses them, and the next `contract build` checks the untouched file and prints `[]`. - **The Mac's split, what is owed** (2026-10-13, LLP 1115.002 D4, stage 6b's as-built; `runner/src/runner/split.rs`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Mac/SplitMac.swift`, `SidebarViewsMac.swift`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-split/`). (1) **The rule cannot tell a push from a Back or a sheet** when a row's `press` is an action: the plan holds an action's write set and no more, so a list whose rows only open sheets, or go back, splits with a pane that stays empty until its author says `navigationSplit="none"`. The route table also lacks an unmounted destination's presentation, and the recursive outer-press rule has no logical row-accessory boundary. Evaluate static modal destinations and nested row accessories across all hosts before changing this shared rule. The compiler knows which verb an action calls; the table's arrow key wants the same fact (below). (2) **Shelf now splits, but selecting another book pushes its route over the previous detail.** Its library is a `list` (LLP 1115.000 D16) and its unchanged test file passes 12 of 12 in both Mac chromes, including swipe deletion. Its `follow` still uses `go`; changing the selected book should use the list-detail pattern's `replace`. (3) **The page's view is the list's column**, so `vw`, and a width an app reads, are the column's while a stack splits (LLP 1115.002 D8.2 item 7 says the window's): the kernel's viewport is the page's view, and telling it the window's needs the root's block to be its pane. Chrome an app lays out around its stack inside the navigation root (a bar under every route) stays in the list's column for the same reason. (5) **The selected row** (a table's, or a painted list's drawn by `SplitHost.mark`). (a) A route is a row's detail by a `link` or by the row's press: one that comes any other way while the rows' press is an action (a link that arrives, Excord's ⌘K switcher) has no row selected. (b) Whether a press writes the router is its action's write set, not what the press did: a row whose action navigates only sometimes takes the shown detail when it does not. The runner knows what an activation wrote. (c) The host names the row by its press's view, so a windowed list's row that unmounts and mounts again comes back unselected; LLP 1127's association, by plan site and instance path, would keep it. (d) A click on a painted row leaves the keyboard where it was (LLP 1115.000 D7.1), so after a field had it the selection is grey, where a table takes first responder. (e) A painted selection does not slide with its swipe row. (f) A person has not compared the painted selection in a key window, or inside a grouped list's card, with a hand-built screen. (6) **Dark Mode's grounds differ in a split:** AppKit tints the window's own ground with the desktop (`#2c2a29` on this Mac) and a box painted in the window's background role is flat (`#1e1e1e`), so a detail that is a grouped form is darker than the list beside it. The same was found in a sheet (LLP 1115.002 D7.4); a painted role cannot be the tinted ground. (7) **The driver:** a plain `screenshot` is the page's view, the list's column; `check`, `sample`, `hover` and `tree --ax` were not run across the two panes; the pane's routes are the pane's accessibility children, not the page's. (8) **The other reading of Q10** is `SplitHost.listOwnsWindow`: run once on a scratch app with the constant turned (pictures in the records), held by no test. (9) Needs a person at a Mac: a drag of the divider and of the window's edge, the selected row's emphasis in a key window, the bar's items moving as a detail comes, a real Command-click to take the selection off, full screen, a 2x display, VoiceOver across the panes. (10) Not built: the iPad's half (the same rule, `UISplitViewController`). The web's stage 6d move and its test boundaries are recorded in LLP 1115.002 D4; integrated browser receipts live under `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/web-split-prep/`.

(6) A row's chevron is not drawn but its box is still laid out, so a row's text ends that much earlier than a hand-built list's. (7) Not built: an inline run's own context menu in a row, `magnify` and a drop over a row (the table is what AppKit finds under the pointer), a native control's look in a selected row, an `svg`'s role paints in a selected row, and a swipe as a Mac user expects it (Delete, or the context menu) and multiple selection, which are vocabulary (LLP 1115.002 section 4). (8) Under load the table's tail has less room than a painted list's: its row views are made in the slice that builds rows, and the same travel back up at 5,000 pt/s was over the frame in two runs of six at the default quality of service (14.2 ms at worst).

- **A phone's captures lack the keyboard, and `agent check` does not model sticky boxes** (2026-10-12, found verifying LLP 1116.008 D8). (1) `withKeyboard` (host/apple/devices.mjs) puts the keyboard into a simulator's window capture; a phone's (`--device`) has none, since the phone driver captures the app's window. Xcode 27.1's `devicectl device capture` captures the device's screen, keyboard included. (2) `AgentCheckIOS.swift` reads `style["position"]` for sticky and fixed, but the key is `position_type` (which has no `fixed`), so that test never matches. Reading `position_type` instead is not the fix: whether a covered control can be scrolled clear depends on how the covering box and the covered one each move under the scroller being judged. A box declared sticky may never stick (its natural and limit boxes are equal, its insets are `auto`, or it has an inset only on the other axis); a header with 10 points of sticky travel stops sticking before what it covers scrolls out; two controls in one stuck bar, or two bars stuck at the same edge, move together; a constraint can belong to an inner scroller while the outer one is judged; and sticking clears no sideways overflow. An approach that holds: over the room the scroller has in the clearing direction, move each box inside that scroller's content by the content's offset plus the change in `StickyConstraint.offset` of the box itself and of each sticky ancestor, counting only constraints whose scroller is that one (a box outside it stays put), and count the control reachable if at some offset its middle is inside the scroller's visible port and under no cover. A stuck row is not drawn over the form when the keyboard comes up (iPhone SE simulator, iOS 27.0, platform chrome and timing, 2026-10-16): a `position="sticky" bottom=0` row at the end of a long `scroll` sits just above the keys while a field above it has the focus.
- **RealWorld's app.js: hunt for link-by-use cuts** (2026-10-12, Charlie raised its budget 38 → 45 KiB with this line): 43.45 KiB brotli-11 at fe5b26798. Find runtime code shipped to every app that only some apps use (as the file-body path and Caltrain's grant/media/event families were moved), and lower the budget when a cut lands.
- **A subtree's containing block, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D8.2, stage 6a's proof). (1) A change inside a block is laid out from the block's root: the replay at a clipping box (`LayoutTree::prepare_boundaries`) is taken under the page's root only, because the page's pass sees every change first and a box under another engine root is none of its boundaries. The same has always held for a second arena root. A long list scrolling in a split's pane will want it. (2) The Mac reads the `block` op through the adapter path (`BatchOp.Kind.unknown`, `BlockHost.applied`), so no shared Swift moved; the iPad's half adds the kind to `Batch.swift` and takes `Runtime.blocks` out of `Mac/BlockHostMac.swift`, with the iOS tests and a tvOS build. (3) `env(safe-area-inset-*)` is one environment, the window's: an iPad sheet or column has a safe area of its own, and no block carries one. (4) A subtree in a container's view has left the page's key-view loop, its accessibility parent is the container's view, and the driver's `tap` and coordinates know one window (read, not run; the document's "As built"). Since stage 6b a split's pane is in the page's Tab order and the driver taps in it; its routes' accessibility parent is still the pane. (5) `browser_host_block.tsv` was measured by a script that is not in the repository, since an agent adds none: modelled on `fixtures/browser_paint_order.mjs`, it also reproduces `browser_containing_block.tsv`'s 155 cases. Beside the fixture it would let the cases be measured again when the rule changes. (6) A content region and a block are refused together; an exclusion shapes no text across a block's edge.
- **The Mac's text and scrolling, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D7.7, D7.9, D8.2, D8.5). (1) Selected text's ink, the rest: an underline under a part-transparent role colour (a label tier) stays part transparent when selected, where its glyphs are solid (the recolouring keeps the line's own alpha); and a content region's role colours are its pair's, resolved in Rust, where a paragraph's are AppKit's own (before this too). (2) AppKit's own selected-text menu, the rest (`TextMenuMac.swift`): the text view it is asked of lays the selection out itself, so Look Up's highlight is on the page's glyphs only for a selection inside one paragraph (its first line exactly, later lines by the paragraph's pitch); across paragraphs, or in a giant paragraph, it is near them. Nobody has opened the menu by hand, chosen Look Up, Translate, Share or a Service from it, or seen its Writing Tools items act on read-only text. (3) The localised standard menu titles are not built, and wait on Charlie's choice (LLP 1115.000 D7.9; measured 2026-10-12 with probe bundles run in Japanese for the process only, macOS 27.0.1). AppKit localises no title an app supplies, by action or otherwise: Xcode's own Mac template is a nib with English titles (`MainMenuApp.xib`) that its developer translates. What AppKit does give in the person's language is what it builds itself: the items it adds (Writing Tools, AutoFill, Start Dictation, Emoji & Symbols, Close All) and the menus it hands out (`NSTextView.defaultMenu`: Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste and Match Style, and the Spelling and Grammar, Substitutions, Transformations and Speech submenus whole; `NSFontManager`'s Font menu), which leaves the application menu, Undo, Redo, Delete, Select All, Find, File, View, Go, Window and Help in English. SwiftUI's `App` does build every standard title localised (About, Settings…, Services, Hide, Hide Others, Show All, Quit, the whole Edit menu with `TextEditingCommands`, Minimize, Zoom, Bring All to Front, Help), so the one supported way to all of them is to let SwiftUI own the main menu: the Mac executable's entry becomes an `App` and Exact's File, Go, shortcut and Develop items become its `Commands`, which is a rewrite of `DevMenuMac` and `ShortcutsMac`'s menu half and belongs with LLP 1115.002. The other ways are the two ruled out: ship translations, or read AppKit's and SwiftUI's tables by key (`MenuCommands` has 25 of 48). Beneath all of them: an Exact app's bundle declares its localisations only when it grants a device (`usageKeys`), so every other app runs in English whatever the person's language, and then AppKit's own strings are English too (its alerts, panels, the text menu). A bundle that declares the app's `lang` and its strings tables' tags is the first step of any of these. (4) AppKit's focus ring has never been seen around an Exact field: it is drawn only in a key window of an active app, and neither the driver's picture, `cacheDisplay` nor the window's layer tree holds it (probes outside the repository, macOS 27.0.1; a screen capture needs a permission the lanes do not have). So three things rest on AppKit's contract and want one look by hand in a key window: a bare field with a border its author wrote shows AppKit's exterior ring around its box and the border under it (LLP 1115.000 D7.9, built); a bare field with no written border shows Exact's two-point ring on its box and, if AppKit draws too, a second ring outside it (the audit's MAC-31 infers it; LLP 1104 D6's note that "the masks alone never drew" was from the driver's picture, which shows no AppKit ring on a native field either); and a native textarea, whose stand-in ring may double its scroll view's. If AppKit draws them, Exact's rings go. A textarea with a written border draws Exact's ring outside its box in AppKit's colour, since its text view's own ring is cut off by its clip view, which is also inferred. (5) Under the agent a wheel is applied at once, with no momentum, and a window has no opening animation; a live gesture test passed 22 or 23 of 27 runs and is kept outside the suite. (6) The page hit test from #327 costs about 10 ms a pointer move on a 400-paragraph page in an unoptimized build. (7) `CTRunDraw` draws no underline, so a paragraph with per-run shadows or a hidden run loses its underlines. (8) By hand on a Mac, none done: a trackpad scroll's momentum and bounce in Shelf's list and a mouse wheel's step; the I-beam over prose, the hand over links, the arrow over buttons; clicking a row, a card and a button in a background window; hover in a background window and after a drag; the window's opening animation and no Show Tab Bar; spelling marks in a plain field; keys in a key window, with a custom `DefaultKeyBinding.dict`.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/b3, 75951c1102, b6f99c5171, be21561641, 31b12ee4ca): selected text keeps underlines and decorations on shadowed runs, propagated decorations at full ink, its measured fragments over exclusions, and flow truncation in source offsets. The rest of the list remains.
- **The Mac's grouped form, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D6, stage 4's as-built). (1) Three controls in a row are not the reference's: a `select` is AppKit's pop-up button (its row 44 points where SwiftUI's menu-style picker makes 38), a number field keeps its field beside the stepper, and a text area keeps its bezel. A `button` row that is not `destructive` stays a row, where a Mac form draws every button as a push button: D6 ruled the destructive one only. (2) The destructive row's bezel is AppKit's push button drawn under Exact's pressable (`Mac/FormRowsMac.swift`), so the whole row takes the press where the reference takes it on the button; its key look, a real click and VoiceOver's reading were not seen by a person. Not covered, each found by review and none a few lines: (1) an SVG descendant that authors its own `accent-color` paints under its `svg`'s accent (`svg_item` opens no scope); (2) a retained content region's replayed SVG and gradient stops resolve with no scope (`ink`, `Gradient::resolved` at replay), so under a region-local accent they are the app's tint; (3) a native button's face parts (title, subtitle, symbol) resolve under the button's accent, not a child's own; (4) a theme alias to a tint role (`CanvasText = "LinkText"`) with a theme `AccentColor` resolves to the theme's colour before the scope is read (`painted_override`); (5) `accent-color="AccentColor"` keeps the accent around only inside the walk, so a hosted field or a region capture under it, with a nearer accent above, reads the tint; (6) colour motion targets (`kernel/src/motion.rs` `color_targets`) still resolve with no scope, so a transition into `AccentColor` under an authored accent eases to the tint and snaps at its end (a fix must keep Apple's `@tint` set, not the web's); (7) a recorded row (Android's recording backend) under `accent-color="AccentColor"` keeps an ancestor accent that changes after it was recorded: invalidation stops at the authored row (`txn.rs`) and a recording's validity does not compare its effective accent (`paint/rows.rs`).
- **iOS after wave 2's keyboard and bars, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D9 and D11, seen on an iPhone 17 Pro simulator, iOS 27.0). (1) A pushed route's large title arrives expanded now (`9b3e3c110`; LLP 1115.000's row for D11.1 says what UIKit needs). What is left of it: a single stack installed partway through the first batch, where a cover cannot be reported, still gets its first screen's header cover after UIKit's first layout; UIKit opens a stack's first title all the same (in UIKit alone on iOS 27, a root's scroller moved up to its controller's top a turn after the first layout), but it is the cause the pushed title had. A header's cover held back by a list's slice in flight (`Session.whenIdle`) would reach layout after the push had begun, and that push's title would arrive collapsed: read in the code, not reproduced. Under the driver's own drag (no `--touch platform`) a scroller cannot be pulled past its top, so a collapsed title does not open there; a real touch opens it. A selected tab tapped again over content at its top under a collapsed title opens it (2026-10-20); but a scroller that never saw its title open holds only the collapsed inset in `scrollOrigin`, and an inset kept from another scroller goes stale when a hatch turns large titles off or a search field leaves the bar. (2) `user-select="text"` on iOS is built for a selection inside one paragraph (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000's row "D11.6, `user-select`", `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/TextSelectionIOS.swift`). What it leaves: **(a) a selection across paragraphs.** UIKit's `UITextInteraction` takes a touch only when its view is the touch's hit view (measured: on a container whose text is in child views it never begins), so one selection over several paragraphs needs one view that takes every touch on their text and hands a tap on to the paragraph's own press and links; the document it would answer from is there (the Mac's order of paragraphs in `TextSelectionMac.swift`, the runner's `list_text` for a virtualized list's rows). The Markdown reader selects one block at a time until then. **(b) VoiceOver's text selection rotor**, which a `UITextView` offers because it is its own text input: the paragraph is a static text element. **(c) The highlight is over the glyphs**, a text view's under them: the container below the text is asked of the display interaction's delegate, which `UITextInteraction` owns (the reference's input was not asked for one). **(d) A triple tap** selects a paragraph in a text view; not measured here (the touch runner has no triple tap) and not seen. **(e) Linux, Windows, Android and tvOS take `text` as `auto`**, said in LLP 1001 and the guide but not by the host: one line in the Linux driver's log, as `font-stack-fallback` has, would say it where it happens. Selecting text there is the Mac's whole selection again (an anchor and a focus over the painted lines, the fill and the selected ink in the painter, a pointer drag, the clipboard), and `host/painted` has none of it. **(f) The driver's plain `tap` on text with a selection up** presses, where a finger's tap clears the selection and presses nothing; a real double tap needs the touch runner to make one (`XCUICoordinate.doubleTap`), and `tap <id> dblclick` is the recognized selection until then. **(g) Before iOS 18** nothing asks a text input whether it is editable; an empty input view is there to keep the keyboard away, on no simulator this Mac has. Not seen on a phone: the loupe under a finger, the haptic, a handle dragged, VoiceOver. (3) The page's own scroller (the viewport) takes the keyboard down with a drag as D9.1's default does (2026-10-22); no prop reaches it. (5) Under the driver's frozen clock, `tap <grouped list> drag 0 -300` on a list under a large title collapses the title and leaves the list at its top (the collection view puts its offset back over the bar's change of inset, as `KeyboardAvoidanceIOS.swift` `reveal` already works around); a real touch scrolls it. (6) `exact new`, the patterns and most recipes still write `navigationScroll` on a route with one scroller, which iOS no longer needs. D11.4 (a borderless field in a grouped row) landed as LLP 1116.005 D2 while this lane built its own, which was dropped; one thing that one had and D2 has not: `appearance="auto"` written on a field in a row does not bring the bordered field back. (7) Not tried: a real finger pulling the keyboard down in a `resizes-content` page (the driver refuses a drag that leaves the viewport, which ends at the keys), VoiceOver speaking a link, the ⌘ overlay on an iPad with a hardware keyboard, pull-to-refresh on a grouped list by a finger.

- **Android's accessibility, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.001 D13, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), "Accessibility"; seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with Shelf and `apps/unsaid`). (1) **TalkBack itself has not read a screen.** The reader answers accessibility services and the framework serves its nodes (checked from outside the app with `uiautomator`); TalkBack found them and focused the first node of two screens. But a touch or key from `adb shell input` goes straight to the app on that phone and never reaches TalkBack, and its speech is not logged, so its swipe through a screen, its double tap, its scroll action, and its focus in an alert and a menu are unseen. It needs a person with the phone for ten minutes, or the owner's word on a virtual input device (`adb shell uinput`: a keyboard for TalkBack's Alt keys, gone when its process ends), which is not among the things a lane may do to a shared phone. Until then D13's gate is not passed. (2) TalkBack asks for the notification permission each time it is turned on there and the prompt does not close on Back: a lane that turns TalkBack on leaves that prompt in a background task unless someone answers it, which is the owner's to do. (3) `Tab` and `link` are English role descriptions: the reader has no strings by language. (4) With a service on, a scroll states the whole tree every frame; coalesce it to the scroll's end if TalkBack's own scroll stutters. (5) A swipe row's trailing action has no accessible action of its own (the row's menu has it). (6) A polite live region that arrives with a new route is spoken once where the app has no top app bar. (7) The reader's click, long click, scroll and set-progress actions were not reached on a phone (only accessibility focus was); a hosted web view or video under the provider was not tried.
- **Shelf's test file is 11 of 12 on the Android reader about one run in six, and the test that fails is not Discover** (2026-10-12; `bun scripts/agent.mjs android --reader --app shelf --test apps/shelf/app.test.contract`, two Pixel 10 Pro XLs). This entry said "discover shows an error, then tries again" passed on a reader built from `a0600e637` and failed on one from `79d468eaf`. Run again the same day it failed on neither, nor on `c48f4e0ea`: 26 runs of the whole file over the three builds (five with `79d468eaf`'s own driver) and 15 of that test alone or by hand, none failing. After `pass fetch` that test's retry is a live fetch of openlibrary.org from the phone (236 to 676 ms that day), so a network that stalls leaves `discover-error` in the tree as the entry described; "Android's bars" (8) below saw one such fetch time out. No commit broke it. What does fail, on all three builds (4 of the 26; 2 of 16 run alone), is "a book: progress clamps and moves the status, rating, notes" at its last step: `tap "detail-status-want"` straight after `type "notes" "Statues"` answers "view 316 is covered or not hit at its projected center". The keyboard that `type` raises is told to exact2 within about a second, between the driver's requests: the viewport goes from 443 by 896 to 443 by 548.5 and the page scrolls 137 points to keep Notes in view, so the control goes from y 212 to y 75. Of 16 drives that read the control's place and then tapped it, 12 pressed it where it was, 3 where it went, and 1 was refused. A person sees a correct screen throughout. Not found: which of the reader's turns the refused tap lands in (the window told, its paint, the scroll `reveal_owed` makes after that paint, that scroll's paint). `Presenter::boxes` does not repaint for an attached display, and the Canvas host's agent (`host/painted/src/canvas/agent.rs`) takes one display turn after a request and none before it. Find the turn, then either `type` answers only once the keyboard it raised has been told and shown, or the agent takes display turns until none is owed before it reads a request. A `CanvasHost` has no test in `exact-painted` (its boot sets the process's environment), so the fix wants a test binary of its own.
- **Android's bars, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.001 D11, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), "The bars"; seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with Shelf). (1) What makes a `tablist` the root's is `Kernel::root_tabs` and the Linux host reads it, but iOS and the Mac still read their own (`NavigationTabs.of`: candidates from an index ordered by view id, over views UIKit has moved, so the kernel's tree order is a change to rule on), and the web host (`host/web/src/document.rs`) and the runner (`runner/src/head.rs`) each keep a copy. (2) A route's content scroller (`navigationScroll`, else the first node that scrolls up and down) is written in Swift (`contentScroller`) and again in `presenter/bars.rs`: it belongs beside the header rule. (3) `HeaderTitle.holdsHeading` stays in Swift for one test that calls it; the bar no longer does. (4) The top bar's colour on scroll changes in one frame, where Compose animates it; a destination tapped again scrolls to the top in one step. (6) A header's avatar, subtitle and tappable title are not read on Android; nor are a `tablist`'s segmented buttons, in the page or in a header. A `radiogroup`'s are built (`kernel/src/segmented.rs`) and have been seen only through the Linux presenter on a Mac: nothing of them has been seen on a phone, the Material oracle's `segmented-buttons` specimen has not been run against Compose, and Compose's motion as the choice moves is not built. A group whose author places its radios with `grid-column` or `grid-row` takes its round ends from the radios' order, not from where each is placed; where the layout is not one ordered row, the segments should each be round on both ends instead. (7) Not seen on a phone: rotation, a cutout in landscape, three-button navigation, a page under `viewport-fit="cover"` with bars. (8) A light-scheme Discover that failed to load once (`TypeError: Failed to fetch — io: Try again (os error 11)`) loaded the next time: not looked into.
- **Android's text fields, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.001 D8, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with `apps/unsaid`'s form). (1) Composing text has not been seen on a phone: Gboard there, typed on by tapping its own keys, sent `commitText` a letter and never `setComposingText` (`x_log_fields=1` logs the calls), in this host's fields; whether it composes in another app's field on that phone was not tried, and no one has composed Japanese, Chinese or Korean. What a composition does across an app's commit is held only at the Rust boundary (`presenter/hosted_tests.rs`). A value the app writes while a field composes is held until the composition ends since 2026-10-12 (LLP 1115.000 §5, ruling 6: `Native.fieldComposing`, `presenter/hosted.rs`), built without a phone: that `Fields.flush` sees a composition begin and end (the composing span on the field's text, read after each of the keyboard's calls), that nothing restarts the keyboard inside a word, and that the action key commits the word before the app's value replaces it, are unseen on a device. (2) Every field's view is made as its page appears, 0.6 to 1 ms a field on the main thread: the frame that showed the form's twenty-one fields took 26 ms where painted fields took 5 to 8 (`dumpsys gfxinfo`). Make a view only once its field is near its port, or ahead of need while the thread is idle. (3) A swipe row, a reorder grip or a `pan` under a field never gets a touch that lands on the field. (4) A field inside a layer the reader animates is drawn moving while its view waits for the next paint. (5) A font declared by its bytes sets the field in the device's default family. (6) The reader has no test of its own (no Gradle test task is built anywhere): `Fields.kt`'s rules for a told value, the caret after a rewrite and the action key are seen only by driving the phone. (7) Not seen on a phone: the light scheme, a font scale other than 1, a hardware keyboard, autofill, a field under a bar or a sheet, `setSelectionRange`, a disabled or read-only field, TalkBack.
- **Android's colours and channel, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.001 D4 and D5, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with `apps/unsaid`). (1) The channel carries no file picker yet (`showPicker`, `saveFile`, the document pickers are refused, and say so), and `@exact/notifications` has no Android implementation (LLP 1133.004 stage 3); a date or time control's picker is LLP 1115.001.000's. (2) Done 2026-10-14: the painter resolves `AccentColor`, `LinkText` and Exact's tint roles by the nearest authored `accent-color` (`roles::authored_accent`). (3) A `link` left unsaid is drawn in the text colour with no underline, where the web's is `LinkText`, underlined. (4) The painter's own colours that are not roles are still Chrome's and iOS's on Android: a field's fill and border, the bordered button, a switch's off track, a field's selection highlight (D9). (5) The palette's derivation for Android 12 and 13 ran only forced, on Android 16; and a wallpaper change reaching a running app was not tried on a device. (6) The Sharesheet's `dismissed` is inferred half a second after the activity is in front again; a device that tells its choice later than that journals `dismissed` for a share.
- **The Android reader is in the tree; the benchmark still builds its own copy** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.001 D2, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md)). The Canvas host's Kotlin side came in from the benchmark's copy at `c30c683` (`expo-build-1000:~/bench/lists/exact-v11/android/canvas`; its history is not imported), with the benchmark's driver cut to a seam (`ExactActivity.drive`) and the package renamed to `dev.exact.canvas`. `bun scripts/agent-android.mjs build caltrain --apk` builds Caltrain on it (drawn on a Pixel 10 Pro XL that day). Owed: port every commit the benchmark's copy takes after `c30c683` (`git log c30c683.. -- android/canvas` there); move the benchmark to consuming `host/android`'s `:canvas`, its `Driver` attached at the seam, because `canvas_jni!` now defines `Java_dev_exact_canvas_Native_*` and the benchmark's own reader (`dev.exact.bench.exactcanvas.Native`) finds none of its methods in a library built from this commit on. What the import does not do is in the README's last section.
- **`agent ios` wheel on a grouped list stops short of its end** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.003): `Agent.scroll` clamps to the collection view's `contentSize`, which a self-sizing grouped list only estimates until its cells come in, so one `tap <list> wheel 0 20000` landed rows short (row 29 of 30 under the tab bar, row 30 unseen) where drags reach the end. Clamp again until the size settles, as `TabEdgeIOSTests.scrollToEnd` does.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/a2): not reproduced in three drive rounds on an iPhone 17e; `Agent.place` already reclamps after layout (at most four times). A characterization test covers one wheel reaching the measured end (TabEdgeIOSTests); when the short wheel reproduces, reclamp until contentSize and the last index path stop changing.
- **A grouped list's cell kept across a batch that changed the list's height shows empty on iOS** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.003 D2.1): the keyboard batch puts every carried row back (`prepare`), then `mount` carries rows only into `visibleCells`, judged before the new layout; a cell left out but kept (not reused) comes back into the port empty, its row hidden beneath the list, until a batch touches the list. The keyboard's reveal carries rows after it scrolls (`carryRows`, KeyboardAvoidanceIOS.swift); a finger's scroll after the keys come up does not, nor the driver's: after Return moved focus and a submit added a row, `tap` on the next exercise's field answered "… covers its middle" (on f6769a64e too). Likely the same family as round 4's blank Finish row below. Checked 2026-10-22: the stated mechanism does not occur in a bare `UICollectionView` on iOS 27.0. `visibleCells` keeps its set until UIKit lays out (14 cells before and after the frame shrank, 4 after layout), and every cell that leaves gets `didEndDisplaying` and then `willDisplay` when it returns, which carries its row. A scratch list of field rows, with the keys up, a press that changed every row and drags both ways, showed no blank cell. The cause is still open.
- **The first `contract` call on a cold checkout builds the compiler for minutes** (2026-10-11, from closing `issues/closed/20261009-agent-port-cost-vs-swiftui.md`): `scripts/exact.mjs` builds `contract` on first use, so a new app's first `contract types`/`build` waits on a cold cargo build. `host/web-js/module.mjs --prebuild` warms only the web builder; a cached or prebuilt compiler would remove the wait from an agent's first minutes.
- **iOS scrolling's leftover suggestions** (2026-10-11, from closing `issues/closed/20261002-scroll-efficiency.md` §7–§8, whose three changes landed in 7098e9537, edaaa7c90, 1ff7407af, a1cd643d2): a 120 Hz device trace first; then per-frame checks driven by what is actually owed, the macOS PressClock on Core Animation's clock, and a per-frame budget for a fling.
- **Windows app-storage removal: the progress guard and a real batch test** (2026-10-11, review of the storage-results fix): `vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/src/stdlib/windows_fs.rs`'s recursive `remove` drains `names_batch` until it is empty or a call errors, with no `require_progress` as Unix has (`app_fs_unix.rs`), so a file held open with `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` (delete pending, still listed) fails `fs.rm` though the pass made progress. `windows_fs_tests.rs`'s `recursive_remove_drains_more_than_one_directory_batch` uses 300 short names, under the 16 MiB listing cap, so it passes without the batching. Port the guard, make the test exceed the cap, and run both on the fleet's Windows machine.
- **Surface-control overflow-child multitouch and cancellation** (2026-10-11, review of #421): two fingers on the overflow child are not tracked because the child lacks `isMultipleTouchEnabled`; a wrapper disabled mid-press can lose cancellation; a focus callback moving the label before the touch id is stored can strand a held control; a link and a blank spot lifted in one `touchesEnded` can skip the link. The round-3 per-touch child route was descoped; design and test a UIKit-delivered route before restoring it.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/k): parked after three rounds; no change.
- **Shared Elements web zoom can lose its Close button in captures** (2026-10-23, the Shared Elements repaint): drive the grid viewer and close it, open and close a card, finish a task, then open a Zoom photo, settling each flight and switching light/dark preferences between screens. The Close control remains tappable but can disappear from screenshots, and `check` omits its text. Reproduces with the original literal colours as well as roles; investigate the web presentation separately. `check` now reports the label (folded text is measured through its line boxes); the screenshot loss did not reproduce in agent-check.test.mjs's run of the sequence (2026-11-12), so it stays open until it is caught again.

Fit there when a consumer puts a readout in one.

- **Snapback 4 client bugs from the bake-off** (2026-10-10, LLP 1116 §9; the bake-off moved to a plain REST server, so these are their own track): one partition per native device against the answer model (two resources on one partition refreshed in one action → "native call outside an answer", "pending on nothing"); a cold first sync of 176–650 ms over the 100 ms source budget (fresh launches sit on "Connecting…" until the poll); on iOS every successful write hung until relaunch while the 4 s refresh stopped (team-kanban); on the web reads stayed stale after the app's own writes (event-rsvp); the README's "Synced" and "Refused: CODE" lines were copied as UI text in 4 of 5 apps. Reproductions: `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/analysis/batch-c.md` (9 bugs), apps `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/apps/{team-kanban,chat-rooms,event-rsvp,household-list,polls}-exact2`.
- **A prerendered web page shows a persisted state's declared value until its module boots** (2026-10-10, LLP 1116 D5). The JS target reads `localStorage` as a persisted slot is made, so a page the build renders no document for (the dev loop, an `exact new` app) is right on its first paint; one rendered at build (`--render rust|js`) paints the declared value, then the module adopts the page and shows the kept one (a structural difference abandons adoption and builds afresh, journaled), and an `activate="interaction"` page waits for a tap. Either hide the root before first paint when an `exact.state.` key exists (the capture script) and show it at boot, or render no document for an app that persists a state. Neither is done: no app that persists renders one yet.
- **The checks' workspace `cargo test` skips exact-painted** (2026-10-10, the agent-tap review): `host/painted` is a workspace member but not in `default-members`, so `cargo test --lib --bins --tests` in the gate never runs its lib or pinned tests; a stale pinned test (`host/painted/tests/pinned/host.rs`) kept passing the gate while it failed under `cargo test -p exact-painted`. Proposal for Charlie: add exact-painted to the gate as host/web and host/apple were (or to `default-members`). Cost: its build and tests add minutes to every check run (measure before deciding).
- **Storage reads at the sixteen** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 Q2). A TypeScript module's storage step is an opaque effect, so many answers each awaiting storage before a shared load (the Bluesky clone's `hiddenFirst`) still meet the sixteen. Classifying needs the seam to mark an identified read-only operation or batch.
- **`new Date()` works in a data module under bun tests but fails the iOS bake** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 7: muted words' expiry): the bun test environment does not match the data-module runtime. The shared prelude refuses ambient time on purpose (`js/src/prelude.js`), while a `Date` built from an explicit value works, so it is the bun tests that should refuse `new Date()` with no argument, as the bake does. The clone passes the wall time in (`noteWall`).
- **Two message rows drew on top of each other while a sheet was up over the conversation after the keyboard went down** (2026-10-08, the clone's conversation view): cleared when the sheet closed.
- **No way to read the host's root font size** (2026-10-08, the clone's font-size setting): `setRootFontSize("87.5%")`/`("125%")` now scale Dynamic Type instead of replacing it (LLP 1121 D6), so Smaller/Larger need no read; the size itself still can't be read back, if an app needs it.
- **The drift line can misname packages when two sources share a name and version** (2026-10-08, LLP 1086.000.000 §7; Astra's round-4 code review, left open by Charlie's ruling at the fix-loop cap). `lockDrift` (`game/app/shells.mjs`) matches a lock's dependency strings to packages not quite as Cargo does: an unqualified edge to a name and version both a path and a registry package hold follows both (Cargo prefers the path package), and a git-qualified edge may carry no commit fragment where the package's source does (Grok read Cargo the other way). Only the names in "exact2 moved … (added: …; updated: …)" are affected; whether the line appears, what `lock` writes and every build are not. Match Cargo's `encode.rs` lookup and add both regressions.
- **Scrolling a list to a row needs the list's id as a literal** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 3): `type-scroll-into-view` takes no computed id, so N pinned Home pages are named `p0`…`p11` and the logo's scroll-to-top is a chain of literal calls; a 13th page has none.
- **An indicator measured from a row's tabs goes stale when the tabs change and the row's box does not** (2026-10-08, the clone's Home tab row): `resize` follows the row's own box, as `ResizeObserver` does, so new labels of the same total width give no event; an app measuring its children needs to re-measure on the change itself (the clone measures again when its tabs change). A pitfalls line, if anything.
- **One SF symbol drew at two sizes across renders of one sheet** (2026-10-08, the clone's interaction-settings sheet): the same `symbol:sf/circle` with the same `width`/`height` measured differently in two captures.
- **Excord's window crosses the data seam whole on every page** (2026-10-29, measured on an iOS 27 simulator, provisional: host-dev Rust, debug Swift; ring deferred by theme2 12:00). Every page that lands refreshes `win`, so loadWindow's whole window crosses again: the prelude stringifies it and the runner decodes it. Medians of 33 pages (fixture `--history-delay 600`, #general flung to the 300-row cap): the crossing (stringify + decode) is 0.75 ms at ~50 rows held (a 57 KB reply), 1.8 ms at ~150 (153 KB), 3.5 ms at ~250 (283 KB) and 4.7 ms at ~299 (379 KB), about 12 µs a row; the page's whole fulfil is 11-20 ms, and at the cap 10 of 18 pages land in a native-scroll hitch of 19 ms (median; 27 ms max), so the crossing is about a quarter of the late frame. Against the lead's gate (≥3 ms a page or ≥1/3 of the late frame) it passes the first only at 250+ rows. The deferred design is reload1's six-chunk ring (~/x2theme/reload1-excord-window-plan.md: `win` a descriptor, six chunk resources, a page re-asking one chunk), which would take the crossing to ~0.75 ms at any size; the measurement is ~/x2theme/x2p4-excord-crossing-measure.md. Revisit it if, after the cheaper levers (loadWindow's flat 3.5-4.8 ms call, the ~8 ms of re-evaluation at the cap, a page landing outside the scroll callback), the 300-row cap still shows late frames on a device.
- **A list's data update is O(rows) in the size index** (2026-10-03, Expose's heavy list on Android; measured again 2026-10-29, parked by theme2). `SizeIndex::splice_keys` copies every row, rebuilds the `SumTree` and shifts every key's position, and `rekey_shared` walks the whole span between the shared prefix and suffix. On main 2b9447b370 (release, the live-tick heavy list, medians of 40): at 10,000 rows a splice costs 70-100 µs on an M-series Mac and 330-400 µs on an arm64 emulator (provisional; no phone was available), flat in the change's size (a 1-row top insert, a 50-row page and an append alike), about 40% of a structural commit (a top insert plus a reaction: 0.245 ms on the Mac, 1.2 ms on the emulator); at Excord's 300-row window it is 2-6 µs (23-43 µs for a 50-row page); a row's data change under the same key never splices. What else stays O(N) at 10,000 rows: `keys.splice` with the `string_keys` scan, 30-50 µs, and `compare::shared`'s prefix and suffix scan, 16-25 µs. The approved design (~/x2theme/x2p4-sizeindex-plan.md; scratch benches in ~/x2scratch/x2-perf4-si-scratch): a counted B+ tree of rows with per-node count, sum, min epoch and zeros, positions by leaf rank (a key maps to its leaf), a splice per changed run in O((changes + 64) · log rows); anchors keep today's old-neighbour fallback through a splice log kept only while an anchor is outstanding, instead of a snapshot of the whole order; `string_keys` becomes a count. Take it up when an app shows 10,000+ rows on a phone, or a phone tick measures over about 2 ms.
- **Pictures a GPU painter holds need no CPU copy** (2026-10-03, Expose on Android). A cached decoded picture is CPU pixels (`image/bitmap.rs`) and, once a GPU painter shows it, a texture too. Android's hardware bitmaps keep only the texture, so `VmRSS` (the list benchmark's peak RSS) leaves them out: Views' 164 MB of photos are graphics memory, Expose's 113 MB cache is native heap. The Canvas host's pictures already decode into `AHardwareBuffer`s its reader draws (`image::hardware_pictures`); a GPU painter could take the same buffers as textures (an `EGLImage`, a Vulkan import) instead of uploading CPU pixels.
- **Apple: images linked by use** (2026-10-08, LLP 1047.001; measured, not built). Only an `image` node loads a bitmap (its `imageSource`), so the use rule is one line (a plan with an image node uses `images`; the web links nothing for it). But the move is all or nothing: Swift in the core calls the `exact_raster_*` exports (`RasterLoader`), and the core holds image results itself (`NativeRasterLease`/`RasterImage` on every node; `RasterGeometry`'s drawing in the modal, flights and box layer; `AnimatedRasters` in the session, video, agent and box layers). Worth it for image-free apps and embeds only: ~54 KB of `exact_raster` and ~45 KB of Swift by symbol gaps, perhaps 50 KB real (speed). Split `RasterGeometry` and a lease protocol into the core, move the rest to an `ExactImages` module, and emit `raster_exports!` by use.
- **Apple: list swipe actions in every app** (2026-10-08): `SwipeActionsHost` (~34 KB by symbol gaps) is in hello, which has no list. Find its use rule (a list row's swipe actions) and move it out as `ExactDrag` was.

Hello's development executable retains 54,856 bytes (53.57 KiB) across 186 unique `SwipeActionsHost` text-symbol addresses, measured by gaps to the next text symbol. This is not a stripped binary size delta. A separate linked capability requires plan-u
- **Apple: the host's Rust drag and height sync run on every commit** (2026-10-08): `reconcile_height_handles`, `sync_height_owner`, `sync_height_transitions` and the transform drags are called from the commit path whether or not the plan has a drag; behind a const link (Drag) they would leave image-free, drag-free apps.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/aa): Parked. Commit `dbc0c3bc93`. Temporary runner-backed Rust probe: all four empty sync calls, 100,000 iterations, debug build: 0.361 us/commit, or 0.350 us with height targets forced dirty. Whole unseen-state commit: 30.598 us/commit, 1,000 iterations. 1 probe passed in 0.13 s; source restored afterward. The empty-path cost does not justify a const-link split 
- **Web writes lost when a tab closes mid-write, redesign** (2026-10-08; **Charlie, 2026-10-08: leave it documented for now, no redesign; the bench grader stays as is** — the pitfall covers it). A storage.fs write still in its IndexedDB transaction when the tab closes is lost (4/4 on an immediate close; bench t5 on the Android round). The first fix (da6848b8f..29efdaad6, a per-tab localStorage journal replayed at next launch) was reverted after a fresh blind review (Astra: revert; Grok: keep with fixes): a replay can write a closed tab's older value over a newer one another tab committed, a page restored from the back-forward cache was treated as gone for good, a non-permanent replay error dropped every dead tab's journal, and a failed localStorage flush could let a committed write age out of the applied set and replay. A redesign needs: a replay only if the target is still the version the write was admitted against (a durable generation per path), pageshow clearing the gone state, per-entry failure handling, and stale-snapshot removal on a failed flush; its own LLP 1097 amendment and review before landing.

Not reproduced on a local iOS 27.0 Simulator with the software keyboard (2026-10-22): a real touch on a three-option `select` opened its menu and no keyboard. A phone is still to try.

- **Keyboard, the rest of #140** (2026-10-07, after `keyup`, `KeyboardEvent.code` and `.repeat` landed). Two parts wait on the owner's word: a capture-phase handler and its spelling (`keycapture=…`, ancestors outermost first, `stopPropagation()` keeping the key from the target), and a reserved held-modifier fact (`exactKeyboard`, reset on window blur, beside `keyup`; the web's way, keyup plus `exactPage().hasFocus`, is documented). A ⌘ chord during a composition now commits it and reaches the `key` handlers on macOS and iOS; not yet pressed with a real Korean or Japanese input source. Composing keys still differ by host: the web host hands every key Chrome sends while composing to `key` handlers (Contract has no `isComposing`, so a composer's Enter there can send mid-syllable), the native hosts keep them for the input method. Composed text is the same on every host since 2026-10-12 (LLP 1115.000 §5, ruling 6; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/FieldComposition.swift`): `input` at each change with the marked text in the value, where AppKit had told it only once committed. What is left of that: (a) nobody has typed with a real Japanese, Chinese or Korean input method on macOS or iOS since (the tests drive `setMarkedText` and `insertText`); (b) an app's value never ends a composition, by Charlie's ruling of 2026-10-12 (LLP 1045 D5 stands): a different value written while text is marked is held and applied when the composition ends, since an action that rewrites what it hears would otherwise end one at its first keystroke and Contract gives it no way to know. macOS and iOS hold (`pendingValue`, `FieldComposition.swift`, each platform's `writeValue`), and so does the web's wasm target (`writeValue` in `host/web/glue.js`, `heldValues` until `compositionend`). Android and the web's JS target hold too since 2026-10-12: Android through the presenter (`Native.fieldComposing`, `presenter/hosted.rs`; not seen on a phone, which the entry on Android's text fields has), the JS target in `rt.js`'s `writeValue`, with the glue's rule and timing. Chrome does end a composition whose field's value is assigned: the marked text is replaced and the next keystroke begins another (`host/web/tests/compose-hold.test.mjs`, real compositions through CDP's `Input.imeSetComposition`, on both targets); (c) whether Chrome and UIKit fire an `input` at a commit that changes no text was not measured: macOS and iOS tell the app nothing then. Real keys (`orca computer`, events posted to the app's pid, macOS 26.6.2) gave Exact and Chrome 154 the same log: ⌘B is keydown Meta, keydown b, keyup b (with ⌘ still held), keyup Meta. A held key's auto-repeat is not yet pressed for real: the CLI cannot hold a key, and auto-repeat downs posted to a pid reached neither app; a keyboard by hand, or a HID-level post with the app frontmost, would show it. iOS reports no `repeat` (UIKit's presses carry none) and the terminal host sends no `keyup`.

- **iOS: a pressable glass button misses real touches over a transform drag's handle** (2026-10-06, the Bluesky clone's lightbox, b08 and b09). Close and menu buttons with `backgroundMaterial="glass"`, siblings painted over a full-page `transformDragFor` handle (`press=tap`, `dblclick`), took no HID tap on the iOS 27 simulator (`axe touch`): the tap toggled the handle's `press` instead. The agent's taps reached them, and with the material removed the same HID tap pressed the button. Interactive glass returns its effect's `contentView` from `hitTest` (`NodeViewIOS.swift`, "Interactive glass … answers a finger itself") and relies on the responder chain reaching the node; a recognizer on the effect or the sibling handle probably takes the touch first. Not reproduced on 2026-10-23 (iOS 27.0 simulator): a glass `button` over a full-page `transformDragFor` handle with its own `press` took the touch runner's real touch (it landed on the effect's content view, and the button's press ran, not the handle's).
