- **CSS `filter` on a box, what is still owed** (2026-09-29, LLP 1055.000 §0 "CSS `filter` on a box"; the paint walk's part 2026-11-11, LLP 1159.000). Where the paint walk draws (Linux, Windows, Android) a filtered box is pictured and the chain runs over the picture (`host/painted/src/paint/box_filter.rs`; `fx-box-filter` and `fx-image-filter` are held to Chrome on Linux, on an Android emulator, API 36, a greyscale, a blur and a drop shadow drew as the web draws them, and nobody has looked at Windows). Owed there: on a box rotated or skewed in the plane a `drop-shadow()` falls along the screen's axes (the schema's `filter` row says so), which painting the box flat and placing it turned would cure, as a box turned in space is; the picture is a transparent frame the size of the window, made again on every paint that reaches the box, so an animation under a filter pays for it each frame; and a `url()` filter on a box is not taken. On Apple a batch touching the box draws the picture again only when what its layer tree draws from changed (`RenderInputs`), and a subtree holding contents that change in place (a field's or text view's backing store; on macOS an IOSurface, so every paragraph) is drawn at every such batch; a filtered box's motion is sampled rather than handed to Core Animation, and a style op changing only `filter` still runs the whole of `NodeView.applyStyle` (#45); a `url()` filter on a box is not taken; the picture is drawn on the main thread at the box's full region (the live SVG path's off-main drawing would suit an animated one). On macOS the picture is `CALayer.render(in:)` of the box, which ignores `maskedCorners`: a filtered box with some corners rounded and others square (a layer-property box, `BoxLayerMac.swift`) is pictured with all its corners rounded; one radius on every corner is exact.

- **What F1 and F3 still cost against SwiftUI** (2026-09-29, M1 iPad Pro, LLP 1055.000 §0 "F1 and F3, the second round"). F3: the app's CPU 413 ms/s (120) and peak memory 145 MB (24), at 120 fps and main thread 73 (84). SwiftUI's filters run in the render server; ours render the sub-scene with `CARenderer` in process (its CPU scan converter fills the shapes, ~50 ms/s), submit two command buffers a frame (~95 ms/s in the driver) and keep the target, two working textures and four surfaces (~80 MB of GPU memory). A picture drawn only when something in it moves (the orbit's rate, not the display's) or a render-server path (a `CAFilter`-free chain in a `CALayer` the server composites) would close it. F1: launch lifetime peak 121 MB (99–116) and steady 88 (49), measured while the vignette was a mask island (23 MB); it is a gradient layer now (first ink below), memory not re-measured.
- **F3's first ink after an install** (2026-09-30, iPhone 13 Pro Max, LLP 1055.000 §0 "First ink"). With the kernels compiled at build, the first launch after an install draws F3 at 280–348 ms against 124–133 warm (SwiftUI 61–68): the GPU compiler's work on each pipeline and Metal Performance Shaders' blur, inside the feature's commit. A `MTLBinaryArchive` harvested per GPU family (or pipelines made on the boot prewarm and the first picture drawn a frame later, against D1's never-without-the-effect rule) would take it out. Warm, F3's commit is ~20 ms on the phone's main thread: the GPU wait (6–7 ms; the first picture is drawn in its commit), the sub-scene's CPU gradient (3–4) and `CARenderer`'s make (2.5–3).

- **What is left of the crypto SVG list's memory against SwiftUI on iOS** (2026-09-27, perf/crypto-mem3, M1 iPad Pro, three interleaved rounds against origin/main ef137e76 and SwiftUI, six for live fling and nine for rest, `results/cm3-r1`). perf/crypto-mem3 holds cold shaped text to two screens and keeps answers (metrics, line breaks, baselines) for what it lets go, drops a settled motion slot's presentation, moves a `NodeView`'s rarely set fields to a lazily made object, and gives the text residency's tables back at rest: fling peak 44.3 -> 40.3 MB (SwiftUI 33.0), live fling peak 44.4 -> 42.4 (33.1), rest 30.4 -> 30.9 (22.7; that is free-page noise: malloc in use at rest is 19.9 -> 19.5 MB in every run, `results/cm3-restvm`, and a four-run footprint recheck read 30.4 -> 30.35); fps, late frames, CPU at every ladder speed, jumps, blanks, cold start and pixels unchanged. What is left: (1) the port's data, about 9.3 MB at 5,000 coins (the entry below). (2) Text answers keep their `TextIdentity` alive (a `Spec` with a strut `Run`, the run array, a token: about 0.6 KB of the 1.2 KB an answer costs, Mac heap): a lean identity (the metric fields and UTF-8 only) would halve what a fling leaves; `fitShaped` is only wired on iOS (macOS has no rest trim). (3) Taffy's node store keeps its peak capacity: its slot map's keys are the kernel's node ids, so it cannot be compacted without renumbering every node, and rebuilding it drops every cached measurement; not attempted. (4) `NodeView` on macOS keeps its rarely set fields inline (the iOS split in `NodeExtrasIOS.swift` would carry over). (5) The collection index about 1.1 MB at 5,000 items: sharing the item's `Rc<str>` needs a key type of a tag plus the string through `HeightIndex`, the anchor, `mounted` and the snapshot JSON the hosts read, for about 30 bytes an item (0.15 MB here, 0.3 MB on the heavy list); not built. (6) The 16-byte `Value`: landed (perf/value16, below). Malloc's dirty-but-free pages swing 3-6 MB between runs, so rest and live-fling footprints need three or more rounds.
- **The expression VM costs about 6 ns an instruction** (2026-09-27, feat/contract-listmap). A crypto row's `points` is about 60 instructions and four string allocations a point: 23 us for 48 points, five times Rust's `format!`. Each instruction is decoded from bytes through the operand table (`vm::decode`), and a template's `+` allocates per part. A pre-decoded instruction array per code body, or an n-ary concatenation for templates, are the levers; measure on the crypto fling (row mounts) before building either.
- **Watch: `sortBy`'s two spellings** (2026-10-17, LLP 1116.016 §5). Its direction is a literal, so a direction view state picks is a ternary of two calls (the euxy port's Patterns sort); and it is a function, as `sum` is, so `xs.filter(…).sortBy(…)` is refused for `sortBy(xs.filter(…), …)` (this lane's own corpus case was written the method way first). Record whether builders hit either, and whether a `"asc" | "desc"` value for the direction is worth its rule.
- **Watch: agents writing JS list idioms in Contract** (2026-09-27, LLP 1017.003 §Diagnostics). `xs.map(f)`, `xs.length`, `reduce`/`find`/`sort`/`slice`, `Math.min(...xs)`, `toFixed`, `map` as a view line or a callback returning a node, and truthy `filter` callbacks are each refused with the spelling that works (`contract_syntax::idioms`). Record, as with the canvas bitmap-width watch, whether agents building apps trip on these, which message got them out, and any idiom the table misses.

What would pay without a mask: white text (the crypto list's letter and change badge, about 0.4 MB at rest) as plain A8 contents, which matched the BGRA pixels exactly; it leans on undocumented Core Animation behaviour.

- **GPU canvases: what is left of an animated shader row's CPU** (LLP 1009 D7 and §3; re-traced 2026-09-30 with one Metal command buffer a canvas frame). At rest on an iPad Pro M1 the 19-kind feed's one animated shader row costs the main thread about 55 ms/s under the profiler (the starved pass: wgpu-core's `finish` 21, its submit 11, the render call 6; the tick 8) where UIKit's `MTKView` draw costs 35, and process CPU 245 ms/s against UIKit's 172 without the profiler (main 102 against 60). What is left is wgpu-core's bookkeeping for a pass and a submit, on the main thread, and a starved canvas's two passes a frame — D8 (the module on a thread of its own) is the design for the main thread; process CPU stays about 1.4× UIKit's for one canvas at 120 Hz with wgpu-core encoding the pass. In a fling the acquiring threads' `nextDrawable` is mostly new IOSurfaces for rows that scroll in (next line). Weird Castle's surface migrates by the patch kept with the lane (`~/bench/xheavy/gpusubmit/weird-castle-d7.patch`).
- **Upstream Naga's unnecessary f16 uniform-storage capability** (2026-10-17, `vendor/wgpu-hal/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, patch 11). Naga 30 emits both 16-bit buffer capabilities for function-local half arithmetic. Declare only capabilities the SPIR-V uses, then separate native arithmetic/storage from uniform-buffer support without weakening WebGPU's `shader-f16` guarantee. Exact's Android-only instance opt-in and uniform rejection keep Adreno usable in the meantime.
- **Upstream ibex2's queued-message count** (`vendor/ibex/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, "Socket queued-message count", 2026-10-15): `MessageSender::buffered_messages`, which LLP 1016.000 D7's publication uses to refuse a batch whole before the transport's 256-message limit; propose it to expo/ibex and drop the local patch when it lands.
- **Upstream wgpu-hal's lazy Metal command buffer** (`vendor/wgpu-hal/EXACT-PATCHES.md` patch 1, 2026-09-30): an encoding that encoded nothing makes and commits no `MTLCommandBuffer`. It changes nothing a caller can see and removes two or three committed buffers per render pass for every wgpu user on Metal; propose it to gfx-rs/wgpu against `wgpu-hal/src/metal/{command,mod}.rs`, and drop the local copy of that patch when a release carries it. Patch 2 (a frame's presentations riding its submit) would need an option on the surface configuration upstream and is not proposed yet.

- **`adopt` covers the compiled value only** (2026-09-26, perf/adopt, LLP 1027 D11). A value carried across a reload, a kept store-reader answer or a document's seed is not adopted, and the TypeScript executor and a worker-placed source ignore it (their values cross as copies). A source that edits what it answers still rebuilds its data after a dev reload (the heavy-list port parses its JSON on first edit then). Extend it when a consumer shows the cost. The heavy-list port also still bundles its 7 MB messages.json into the device binary for the bake's sake; untouched, those pages are clean and not charged to the footprint.

- **What is left of a slow heavy-list scroll on iOS** (2026-09-26, perf/slow-frame, Time Profiler on the M1 iPad Pro trace and the iPad Pro 13" simulator). Three pieces are separable. An ellipsized paragraph (`text-overflow`) never rasters on a worker, so each new row's author name is shaped and drawn on main in `NodeView.draw` (`paragraphLayout`, ~2% of main on the iPad); a worker raster that truncates would take it off. Every commit re-serializes every mounted row's snapshot (`collections_json`) and Swift re-parses it (`applySnapshots`, `CollectionSnapshot.init`), ~3%, including the measurement-only report that follows each built row. And each report still carries every mounted row's height (`CollectionHost.height` walks each row's ancestors for `hidden`), which the runner now ignores when unchanged but the host still gathers and encodes.

- **A hardware keyboard on a headless simulator** (2026-09-26). This Xcode has no Simulator.app, so a simulator has no hardware keyboard and UIKit's focus system never starts. One HID keyboard event from SimulatorKit's `SimDeviceLegacyHIDClient` (`IndigoHIDMessageForKeyboardArbitrary`) attaches one for the rest of the boot, which is how perf/slow-frame reproduced the iPad's focus-search cost; the keys themselves did not reach the app, so a real Tab walk still needs a device or Simulator.app.

- **iOS boxes on the pixel grid, what is owed** (2026-10-17, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PixelGridIOS.swift`). Every node view and flat leaf is drawn with its absolute edges rounded half up to device pixels, as Chromium's `PixelSnappedIntRect` rounds them; text wraps and native editors and collections measure at layout's size (`layoutBounds`). Typetour's first screen at 3x has 20% fewer horizontal edges blended across two pixel rows (21,118 to 16,943); Shelf's first screen was on the grid already. Owed: (1) offsets UIKit's own views take, a grouped list's `UICollectionView` and a dialog sheet's scroller, and Exact's other programmatic writers (scroll anchoring, reading-position restore, keyboard avoidance, collection anchoring and its animated offsets), which can leave content a fraction of a pixel off; `writeScrollPosition` and content sizes are on it; (2) border widths: Chromium snaps a computed border width (under one device pixel to one, else down to whole pixels), which belongs in the kernel for every host; (3) what blurs inside a box: a paragraph's lines sit at fractional baselines; (4) a carried grouped-list row keeps its children's places from its authored parent, which can differ from Chromium's by a pixel. The Mac draws on the grid since 2026-11-04 (`Mac/PixelGridMac.swift`, its own copy of the arithmetic, at a scale that is not whole under a page zoom). It owes a dialog or popover the host centres on a fractional point, and a region surface's published selection rects (`RegionWorkerLayout.swift`), whose baselines still round in their row where its pixels round on the surface.

- **A list benchmark row is now mostly the runner and the batch** (2026-09-25, perf/rowcost, simulator Time Profiler over the fill slices; it was mostly UIKit). The iOS presenter's share of a built listbench row fell from about 1.5 to 0.75 ms (no window layout engine, no scroll view per closed swipe row, parked row views reused by shape: `NodePoolIOS.swift`). What is left per row: the runner's commit and a Taffy layout from the root on every report (about 0.7 ms, the perf/layout lane), `Batch.decode`'s JSON (about 0.25 ms), `CollectionSnapshot` parsing the whole snapshot each batch (about 0.12 ms, `Collection.swift`), and `UIImageView` resolving a symbol it is handed anew (about 0.08 ms). Layout from the collection, a binary batch, or a snapshot that sends only what changed would each go further. View reuse is iOS-only; macOS and web could follow the same contract.

- **`timeline-scope: all` has no browser oracle** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 3). Chrome 154 drops the declaration (`CSS.supports('timeline-scope', 'all')` is false), so the kernel and the glue follow the specification's text, and the web's own CSS carries no `all`: phase 4's scroll timelines, which the browser resolves, would not see one. Rechecked with phase 4 (2026-11-12): Chrome 156 still parses no `all`, so on the web a scroll or view timeline's name scoped only by `all` finds no timeline while the native hosts find it. Refuse `all` until a browser parses it, or lower it there to the names declared below it (the wasm host's kernel knows them; the JS target would need the plan's). The lookup's other 23 cases are Chrome's answers recorded in `kernel/tests/it/timeline_scope.rs` from a scratch page; `parity.html` rendering those trees with scroll timelines would make them a fixture.

- **Surrogate keys that name only what shaped a page** (2026-09-23, render lane): the server keys a page by every source its render asked (LLP 1048.000 D11), so a source whose anonymous answer never changes (Interview's `loadDraft`, `accounts`) puts every page in its purge. Naming only the answers the document read needs the projection to report the resources it read; the runner doesn't track that.

- **Contract compiler follow-ups** (2026-09-22, branch `compiler/expansion`): Lowering canonicalizes and opens a file on every compile (6–14% of lowering, `lower_with_sites`); the resident compiler could keep it. Unoptimized `infer` takes ~10 KB of stack per level, which is what holds an expression to 100 deep (release has room for ten times as much); splitting its arms would raise the bound. `change=add` for a parameterless `add` reports only the unrelated `setDraft`'s uninferable parameter, since analysis's handler arity needs types first. - **Native Markdown undo boundaries** (2026-09-22; LLP 1045 D5): UIKit must retain its specialized per-editor undo manager; source undo/redo across natural run-loop turns and independent histories are verified with it. Still owed on native: a source replacement and formatting command in one automatic event group can undo together, skipping the intermediate source. Three bounded automatic-group repairs did not establish a safe boundary; the final attempt was reverted after scheduled run-loop closure exceptions on both platforms. Preserve native grouping semantics; the one-command-one-undo requirement remains incomplete. Physical phone input and intrinsic editor sizing remain unproven.

The stress app has a formatting toolbar and link field. Interview's separate consumer branch adds composition, source-preserving publication and durable account/prompt drafts. Native syntax stays visible at full advance but for link destinations, which Apple hides by `exact-markdown-editor`'s rules (LLP 1045 D5 r6, 2026-10-20); the web hides all of it (WYSIWYG). The DEFERRED take is approved. They agree: Fieldnotes first (a legal 1,000-note notebook cannot be opened), then Messages changed-record writes, then native filling; no heap, deltas or new apparatus. r2 §9 recommended 1027.003.001 as the spine with four amendments; r3 is the synthesis, reviewed by Astra max and Grok 4.6 xhigh (both MATERIAL FINDINGS, six defects raised by both families, dispositions in `llp/reviews/`) — this document's summary-only Fieldnotes answer with no pager, its sibling's chunked exact search with no stored folded column, the three-second idle sync tick as its own first Messages change, and hoisting `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` out of the per-operation path. 1027.003.001 §8 found what both repairs cannot fix: browser SQLite exports the whole database after every non-query op (`host/web/storage-worker.js:113-123,189`) and every `storage-fs` operation `getAll()`s every file with contents (`host/web/storage-fs.js:166`). A contract compiler fix landed (a bool `derive` could not be a ternary condition); Fieldnotes now fetches short prefixes for unfiltered previews, with bounded 32-row full-body fallback, and loads only the selected editor body. Exact search still scans every body. TypeScript reads and Rust backups now check the stored schema instead of issuing no-op CREATE writes; browser whole-database export on actual writes remains open. Filesystem operations now fetch their required records or subtree keys; removal reads no deleted-file contents, and rename loads only the moved subtree and records it validates. Messages idle sync now skips full-history reads/comparisons unless reconciliation is owed; reset/adoption, apply and settlement failures retain the obligation until successful read/publication. Browser idle metadata commits now skip persistence when a bounded SQLite comparison finds every final value unchanged. Unchanged reply timer ticks now skip full-model snapshot/diff work too. Durable source footprints now avoid full-history snapshot/diff and held-map copies on local edits. Receipt transitions now visit only outgoing messages still awaiting Read. Sends and contact edits now preserve existing person positions and select only affected contacts. Recovery footprints now include only selected messages and expiry candidates; due expiry metadata scans and whole-database export for actual browser writes remain.

What would make sense to do next, in rough order. This is not a spec and decides
nothing: `rules/RULES.md` §Scope says a spec without an implementer and a date isn't
written, so a line here is one to three lines — the thing, why, what it needs. When
someone picks one up it becomes an LLP with a name and a date (archive a link when
the working set is full); when it lands, delete the line. Anyone, agents
included, may add or remove a line. `rules/DEFERRED.md` still binds: an entry that
sits on that list carries the trade it would take.

## Next, in order (2026-08-29)

- **Game texture families in bundles and on the wire** (2026-09-23, textures lane; LLP 1046.003, "Compressed textures"): every baked texture ships as `x.tex` (RGBA8), `x.bc.tex` and `x.astc.tex`, and a device fetches one — on the web. Native bundles copy all three (the Fox's 5.6 MB becomes 8.4 MB); a macOS bundle needs BC plus the fallback, an iOS device ASTC plus the fallback, but the bake's compatibility inventory lists every asset, so thinning is a bake change, not a copy filter. Separately, `host/web/serve.mjs` sends `.tex`/`.model` as uncompressed `application/octet-stream`; RGBA8 payloads gzip 70× (the Fox's 5.6 MB → 80 KB) and blocks 1.2–17× — a compressible type for game assets would cut the fallback's download.

## Cheap, any time

- **Editor support in the async lane** (2026-11-13, LLP 1035.005.003): once TypeScript 7.1 is
  stable, run `editors/content-mapper`'s tests with a pinned `tsc` (`EXACT_TSC71`).
- **Weatherlight's Android APK does not build on main: its Gradle locks do not match** (2026-11-14, at 32036894e on the fleet's tier-2 toolchain): `bun scripts/agent-android.mjs build weatherlight --apk` stops with "Gradle locks or verification metadata do not match its dependencies". Find the dependency change that moved the graph, then run the command's `--write-locks` and review `gradle.lockfile` and `gradle-verification.xml` before committing them. Tier 2 of the async lane builds this APK.
- **On iOS a scroller that hides one axis cannot reveal a target past it on that axis** (2026-11-13, Astra's review of the fragment scroll): an ordinary `overflow-x: hidden; overflow-y: auto` scroller (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/NodeViewIOS.swift` ~1343) does not move to a horizontally offscreen target on `scrollIntoView`. A projected route under a scrolling navigation root now keeps that axis's extent and locks the person's drag on it (`NavigationIOS`); the ordinary scroller needs the same.
- **On iOS a row nested beside the tab panels paints under the native tab container** (2026-11-13, found fixing the tab routes' bottom inset). A bar in a column it shares with the panels now clears the routes' bottom inset, but `placeOwner` orders the container only among the navigation root's children, so the bar is drawn under it and a tap at its centre reaches the route's scroller. Raising the column instead puts its empty panels over the routes; the container would have to mount at the panels' depth. `TabEdgeIOSTests.testAReplacementBarInAColumnWithThePanelsClearsTheGrid` checks the inset only.
- **A header title's avatar keeps the accent behind an alert on iOS** (2026-11-13, left by the tint-edges fix): `HeaderTitleView.avatar` is drawn baked, as a bar badge was, and is not redrawn when UIKit dims the tint. A bar badge now records its tint parts and redraws them dimmed (`BadgeFace`, `UIColor.dimmedTint(share:)`); the avatar needs the same.
- **Two iOS dimmed-pixel tests fail on main** (2026-11-13, at 0104e4023, identical pixels with and without the tint-edges change): `DimmedPixelsIOSTests.testBehindAnAlertAColourOfItsOwnKeepsIt` and `testBehindAnAlertTheTintGreysAsUIKitGreysIt` (`bun host/apple/build.mjs --test --ios --only DimmedPixelsIOSTests`).
- **A one-axis overflow clip is not walked for document overflow on Apple** (2026-11-13, Grok's review of the one-axis clip box): `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/SpaceTransform.swift` ~259 does not walk a node that clips one axis, so `documentOverflow` misses a descendant placed past the node on its visible axis.
- **Video Player's dark-chrome test fails on main** (2026-11-13, at 094c929de and before it): `apps/video-player`'s "the player and its controls stay dark under light preference" reports the scheme `dark` where it expects `light` (`bun host/web/build.mjs video-player-web`, then `bun test --isolate` on the app's test).
- **Drive iOS scroll drags with a real touch** (2026-11-10): `SwipeScrollDragTests` passes on a simulator, but no `--touch platform` drive has flicked a scroller with `scrolldragstart` and `scrolldragend`. Check that a flick fires one start and one end with UIKit's velocity and target.

- **Evaluate a component's computed prop once across an instance's bindings** (2026-10-18; worth ~2 points of euxy's playing CPU): repeated reads within one binding, a forwarded argument or an inject now share one evaluation (`contract/syntax/src/inline/use_node.rs`, mirrored in `semantics/Contract/Expand.lean`). A prop read by two bindings of one element, such as its text and `aria-label`, is still evaluated by each. Each binding is its own program and a refusal unsets only that binding, so sharing across them needs a plan value cached per element or instance that holds a result or a refusal and unsets each binding that reads it: the plan format, lowering, the runner, the web JS runtime and the Lean render semantics and proofs.
- **A large reply's owed Hermes collection can wait forever** (2026-10-17; `js/src/prelude.js:1051,1187`, `js/src/lib.rs:863,1084,1128`): the full collection a promoted large string owes runs only when no other answer is pending, so a fetch that never settles keeps a dropped 376 KB QR buffer for the life of the process; and a reply whose serialization throws (capture limit, cycle) leaves its call in `calls` as `done`, blocking every later owed collection. Run the owed collection on the reply and re-stamp pending calls' epochs; `__exact_forget` a call whose settle fails.
- **Bold Text on Material popup typography** (2026-10-24; LLP 1115.000 D12.3): `host/painted/src/presenter/top/look.rs` builds dialog and menu paragraphs at fixed weights (400 for headline/body, 500 for labels), and its popup symbols stroke at a fixed regular weight (`(1.1 + 300.0 / 400.0)`, ~line 298). Confirmed in `paragraph`: it takes neither a node nor the preference; `Inputs` carries only scheme, font scale and window size. Carry the applicable Bold Text setting into surface layout and its cache inputs, and step these host-assigned weights once for measurement and paint.
- **scrollend gaps on the hosts that have it** (2026-10-13; LLP 1005):
  - Apple unbracketed moves use a 100 ms `DispatchWorkItem`; known instant commands and discrete wheel notches should complete without that quiet delay.
  - Apple visible layout clamps can arm an end through `scrollEndMoved`; classify clamps separately from scroll operations.
  - Apple hidden-scroller completion differs from Chrome: `take()` precedes `hasScrollLayoutBox`, so a hidden callback consumes an owed end, while hide/show before the callback can retain unfinished work; distinguish unfinished scrolling from an already queued end.
  - Apple settlement must include unfinished scrolling and queued completions: `Agent.nativeInFlight()` does not count `ScrollEnd.owed` or its quiet work item.
  - Web `clock settle` can return `settled: true` during smooth scrolling and before a `scrollend` handler's jump chain finishes, as measured by LLP 1005's runtime probes.
  - Verify web lifetime parity: `rt.js` suppresses `scrollend` for a disconnected element or a gone owner but still delivers `scroll`; the `glue.js` end listener has no equivalent guard.
  - Physical macOS momentum completion remains unqualified; verify one end after the coast with a real trackpad, as in the existing momentum entry below.
- **`scrollend` on the Linux and Android host: full timing and device qualification**
  (2026-10-15; LLP 1005, Scroll completion):
  the Presenter now completes moving notches and instant jumps at its next delivery
  point (deferred notches share one completion after their frame's `scroll`),
  reader samples after 100 ms quiet (a drag the Android reader reports ends at the
  lift or its fling's stop), and owned edge scrolling when it stops.
  Lifetime cancellation and agent settle are covered; apps without handlers retain
  main's behavior. Declared gap: viewport resize can classify end-follow layout
  movement as a new scroll. A 100 px scroller with `scrollFollowEnd`, a child of
  height `100vh`, and a viewport growing from 500 to 600 px emits a completion.
  Remaining: the full deadline-before-later-timers rule and its
  snapshot/reentry budgets, plus Android device qualification. The first step keeps
  Host's catch-up and shell call order. The earlier full implementation remains
  parked on `q1013/scrollend-impl`; use the no-handler differential harness at
  `~/lanes/q1013/wt/scrolldiff` to guard any later timing work.
- **One presenter pass for the batches a frame lands together (iOS)** (2026-10-13, from the
  Bluesky clone's quotes fling): most fling frames apply four batches: the app's `scroll=` batch
  (the clone writes state every frame), one or two list fill slices that `landFill` lands inside
  it to keep the owner's order (T4), and a clock batch. Each that reaches `Presenter.apply`'s full
  pass (not a collections-only slice `applySnapshots` takes, nor a scroll batch `applyUnlessEmpty`
  skips) pays its fixed work whatever it holds: NavigationHost `batchApplied`/`prepare`, `onlyListRows`,
  `syncAccessibility`, `ControlHost.sync`, CollectionHost begin/end, about a quarter of the main
  thread's time in that fling (Time Profiler, 1136 samples). Merging the slices landed inside one
  apply into a single pass would pay it once. It touches the T4 ordering code, so parked until a
  device shows the cost.
- **Physical iPhone HDR qualification** (2026-10-13):
  On the iOS 27 simulator (iPhone 17 Pro), independent Apple API probes
  report `RGBA8Uint` for `.RGBA16Float` and `.gray8Uint` layer format hints,
  headroom 1 instead of 4 for gain-map HDR decodes, retained PQ/HLG for SDR
  decodes, and no ISO gain map when encoding the gain-map fixtures. The
  same requests work on macOS 27.0.1. Each declined assertion group records
  its fixture and observed result; storage, headroom and neutral-pixel
  comparisons still run. See [Apple's format hint](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/quartzcore/calayer/contentsformat)
  and [headroom semantics](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreimage/ciimage/contentheadroom).
  Physical iOS remains unqualified: run `HDRColorTests`, `HDRImageTests`,
  `PickerColorTests` and `RasterColorTests` on a connected iPhone and require
  no declined assertion groups, skips or expected failures. Their colour
  fixtures live in `host/apple/tests/ExactKitTests/ColorFixtures` as regular
  files, copied into SwiftPM resources and loaded through `Bundle.module`.
  From the repository root, build the device archive and unsigned test bundle
  (the generic iOS build passed without a phone):

  ```sh
  export PATH=$HOME/.bun/bin:$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH
  export DEVELOPER_DIR=/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Developer
  SDKROOT="$(xcrun --sdk iphoneos --show-sdk-path)" \
  HOST_CXXFLAGS="-isysroot $(xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-path)" \
  MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0 IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 \
  EXACT_UPDATE_TRUST=development HERMES_LEAN_SYS_OFFLINE=1 \
    cargo rustc --crate-type staticlib --profile host-dev -p caltrain-apple --lib --target aarch64-apple-ios
  export EXACT_TESTS=1 EXACT_LIB=caltrain_apple
  export EXACT_LIB_DIR="$PWD/target/aarch64-apple-ios/host-dev"
  cd host/apple
  xcodebuild build-for-testing -scheme Exact -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' \
    -derivedDataPath ../../target/ios-hdr2/device-tests \
    CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO LIBRARY_SEARCH_PATHS="$EXACT_LIB_DIR"
  ```

  `xcodebuild test` cannot run these on a phone: a package's tests are
  tool-hosted, and Xcode refuses that on a device ("Tool-hosted testing is
  unavailable on device destinations", tried 2026-10-13 on an iPhone 17 Pro
  Max). Qualifying them needs a host app for the test bundle (as
  `host/apple/touches.mjs` makes for the UI tests), or a minimal app that runs
  the same ImageIO, Core Image and `CALayer.contentsFormat` probes and is
  launched with `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console` on an
  unlocked phone; a person at the phone is needed either way.

## Declared gaps, by system (each spec's "Not in v1")

- **Kernel / layout** (LLP 1001, 1010): `position: fixed`; `text_align: start`;
  presentation transforms in scrollable overflow; `overscroll-behavior`;
  snap re-snapping on Android after a layout change; Linux scroll events.
- **Durable state** (LLP 1018 §5, built 2026-08-30 for Weird Castle's token): the
  `plain` tier — persisted slots, with Caltrain's "last station"; `me`; the iOS
  first-launch keychain wipe; the data-protection keychain on macOS (a bundle and a
  profile); the Linux file store when that host links ibex2.
- **Executable instance rows: lowering proof** (LLP 1017.000 P4c):
  `VmRows` models calls and `LowerCheck` validates root-owned bodies against
  checked substitution. Prove the sharing correspondence, including the
  matcher's binder and owner conditions and transitive calls; validate arm-owned
  bodies and view readers in instance environments, supplying their instance
  stores; add roster/extent accounting to the model and prove its runner refinement.

- **Plan / runner** (LLP 1005 §8): cached per-instance derives or resources inside `each`. At-read instance
  expressions share executable rows without caching their values.
- **Contract** (LLP 1006 §8): inlining budget; `contract` blocks as assertions;
  `cursor`; per-instance state; a Contract language server (opt-in editor support
  through TypeScript's is LLP 1035.005.003).
- **Web host** (LLP 1007 §9): a text-measurement bridge (the kernel's layout does not
  run on the web — and only grid has a kernel-against-browser layout check,
  `host/web-js/conformance/grid.contract` with `--linux`; a general layout parity
  corpus would be the analog of `parity.mjs`); scroll position across a reload; a
  spring interrupted by an easing.
- **Apple host** (LLP 1008 §7): images; toggles; accessibility beyond `testId`,
  `accessibilityLabel` and macOS's button/link/image/heading roles (iOS has no
  image or header trait); justified text; rubber-banding on inner
  scroll nodes; a generated header. **iOS** (LLP 1008 §9): the agent API on a
  phone (`build.mjs --device --run` installs over USB or Wi-Fi, but nothing drives
  the app there); a
  synthesized touch for the agent's `tap` (UIKit's hit-test and the responder-chain
  rule today); a pan chaining out of a nested scroll view at its edge (UIKit's own;
  the agent's wheel chains).
- **The keyboard's blink on a field hand-off** (iOS): UIKit rebuilds the accessory bar
  when first responder moves between two `UITextField`s — one bar-less frame (308 →
  335), reproduced in a from-scratch two-field app with any traits and by re-traiting
  one field in place; a phone shows it faintly. Safari does not, so WebKit hides it
  somewhere below the responder (its `UITextInput`-conforming content view and its own
  input-assistant handling). Worth finding out how, if a form ever needs it gone
  (LLP 1008 §9).
- **Linux host** (LLP 1015 §7): evdev has carried no real event yet; `canvas`; libinput and
  xkbcommon (acceleration, touchpad gestures, hotplug, non-US keymaps); Wayland/X11
  windows; selection, IME, a caret blink; `text_decoration`, `font_family`, RTL;
  shadows, gradients, grid; JPEG, image URLs; accessibility; a font cache; text pixel
  fixtures against Chrome (the Linux tests pin a font, `host/painted/tests/pinned/`; Chrome draws the system's).
- **GPU** (LLP 1009 §4): readback bands for blending and MSAA; a generated module ABI
  once there is a second consumer; the `Custom(u16)` animatable-property extension
  when a surface needs it.

## Later

- **Shared elements, stage 2** (LLP 1013.000 §3; moved here 2026-10-19 as the take
  for LLP 1020 §11's iframe host updates, `rules/DEFERRED.md`; stage 1 built
  2026-10-04: the `sharedElement` attribute, live native flights on iOS/macOS, CSS
  View Transitions on the web's JS target, `apps/shared-elements`). Waits for a named
  consumer: velocity carried into a flight (a release's, an interrupted flight's: Q5),
  drag-to-dismiss handing a hold to the flight back, a name as the zoom's source
  (`navigationSource`), flights on Linux and on the wasm web target, and a prop
  changed on a live node pairing.

- **Web view rows: memory against SwiftUI** (2026-09-28, LLP 1020 §10, LLP 1068 §5.2; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1020.002, the browser shell, `rules/DEFERRED.md`). The feed's web view rows now tie SwiftUI on the iPhone (97.6 against 97.4 fps) and beat it on the iPad (100.5 against 95.8). Footprint is still higher: 70–76 MB against SwiftUI's 38–44 on the iPhone, 61–82 against 43–50 on the iPad. The two oddities are noise. Per-run peaks on the iPad are bimodal (about 62 or 81 MB) with and without near-only leaves, so the medians of three moved with the draw. System Trace's WebContent launch counts vary by ±100 between runs of one build (SwiftUI 290 and 479). A leaf, once made, stays until its row retires two viewports away, so it cannot churn at the quarter-viewport margin. Where the extra ~30 MB lives (the app's WebKit proxies, the text rasters, the pool) is unmeasured.

- **A followed end resolved by the host (deferred, 2026-10-05).** The
  proposal on `ide/doc/followed-end-one-target`
  (`issues/20261005-one-target-for-a-followed-end.md`): the correction names
  the end, and the host fits the extent with its own laid-out rows. LLP 1010
  §6.8's 2026-10-05 note does the cheap half instead: the measured row
  arrives 0.5 to 4 ms after the estimate, so a target before the motion's
  first frame is its one target. Take this up only if a measured report
  round trip is ever longer than a frame. Against it: a wire change on every
  host, and the extent with two sources of truth, so a later runner extent
  that disagrees (f32 rounding, a row whose measurement a cross-size check
  skipped) moves `contentSize` again at the end.

- **Chrome conformance steps for Paste and Match Style and for a clipboard event with nothing focused** (2026-10-21, PR #901; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1133.006's clipboard `write`, `rules/DEFERRED.md`). The driver's `type <id> paste` dispatches a synthetic `ClipboardEvent` at the focus or the target, so it never reaches `<body>`, and it has no Paste and Match Style; both were checked by hand in Chrome over CDP (`Input.dispatchKeyEvent` with `commands: ["paste"]` and `["pasteAndMatchStyle"]`). A driver operation for an edit command, sent as CDP's `commands`, would let `host/web-js/conformance` hold them.

- **A parallel mutation's held fetch is neither named nor counted as a reply on the web** (2026-11-13, held requests across the Rust module seam). `current` in `host/web-js/held.js` checks only `owner.ticket`, so a held fetch from a parallel mutation (`owner.tickets`, LLP 1161.000 D6) is missing from `heldAsks`, its reply from `heldReplies`, and its work from a render's `answersAtWork`; TypeScript sources and Rust ones alike. Taking `owner.tickets` into account in `current` fixes it, but puts the production starter 6 brotli bytes over its 45 KiB budget, so it needs a place outside the starter or room in it. Test: `host/web-js/held-count.test.mjs` with the owner a parallel mutation (`owner.tickets = [t]`), on the page and in a render.
- **The Android reader's display turn can fire a timer already due at the frozen clock** (2026-10-22, held requests round 2, Grok). `CanvasHost::agent` runs `frame_held` after every request (`host/painted/src/canvas/agent.rs`). With the driver owning the clock, `frame_held` calls `Presenter::advance(now)` when a runner timer, a scroll end or a hatch `after` is due at that now (`host/painted/src/canvas.rs`). So a `state` read there, and `clock held`'s polls, can fire such a timer, which Linux stdio, Apple and the web hosts never do. It's rare: a timer due at the frozen now has usually fired at the clock step that reached it. Fix: no advance on a read-only request.
- **The web's wasm host has no extension modules** (2026-10-24, motion stage 0, LLP 1133.013). Games run on it, and it binds no package implementation and answers no agent `prefer extension` or `prefer permission`; `state.pending` holds no module ticket (`host/web/glue.js`'s `prefer` case). Beacons' tilt therefore works on the web neither for a person nor under the agent, and `game/games/beacons/proof.mjs` skips its tilt leg there, so the leg is unpinned (§10 drive 6). The bridge would mirror `host/apple/src/midi.rs`'s `extension`, with a JS loader for `packages/*/web/index.js` as `host/web-js/modules.js` has; `glue.js` is at its cap, so it goes in a module of its own.
- **Qualify the motion screen-frame browser oracle on hardware** (2026-10-26). Headless Chromium 156.0.8078.4 maps a virtual device gravity `[1, 2, 9]` to `[2, -1, 9]` at an emulated screen angle of 90°. Applying that inverse rotation to Safari on iPhone 16 Pro reverses the physical screen axes. The existing vector matrix remains; compare a real Generic Sensor device and the emulator before using the virtual screen-frame sensor as its oracle.
- **Motion, what the canvas seam leaves** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.013 §13, "The canvas seam"). The Apple and Android contexts feed a canvas, and Beacons walks by tilt with the real `Motion.kt` bound on an Android emulator. The iPhone drive confirmed reversed roll; D6 now negates screen-frame x, with regression coverage for both roll directions and unchanged pitch. Owed: a physical tilt check of the corrected build (`packages/motion/test/phone-checklist.md`, item 9); ending an input when its canvas unmounts (its samples now go to no canvas, logged once); the web JS target's canvas inputs (no JS-target app has a canvas world); `headphoneMotion` with its usage key; Android's `HIGH_SAMPLING_RATE_SENSORS` derived from a grant line (without it a faster frequency is asked at 200 Hz); and D9's Android options and its status's `android` (null).
- **A heartbeat in transit can release a signalled wait in the design tool tests** (2026-10-24, dt/dtt round 2, Astra; theme2: left open). After `clock +N real until <signal>`, `clock held changes` returns on the next reply the host lands; a 204 heartbeat the server sent before the event (a write, a frame's registration) but the host landed after the signal releases it, and the event comes on the next ask (at least a second later), so the drive reads state without it. Repro (Astra): the real `readLongPoll` and `heldClock` with the heartbeat's delivery held past the signal: `settled: true`, the event unapplied. Sites: design-tool-poll, -canvas, -scrub and -kinds; each failing expect names it (`HEARTBEAT_RACE`, design-tool-harness.mjs). It can only fail a test, never pass one. Fix: one more `clock held changes` after the signal at each site (one heartbeat each, 3 to 5 s); if it fires in any gate, close it then as a main-red fix.

- **Ink readback RSS peaks** (LLP 1149 S0 C5). Desktop peaks rise during the CPU pixel copy, after device and glyph setup, with or without `ps` sampling. Zeroed output allocation did not remove them. Reuse the presenter's CPU pixmap and the device's staging buffer, then repeat the C4 memory protocol; see `host/ink/MEASUREMENTS.md`.

- **Ink D3D11 readback cost** (LLP 1149 S0 C6). On the NucBox, 1920×1080 C4 scrolling takes 3.920 ms with D3D11 versus 2.718 ms with wgpu DX12; readback, including completion, takes 3.238 ms versus 1.632 ms. Profile staging, completion and copying before optimizing the headless path. DirectComposition avoids that readback in the window. See `host/ink/MEASUREMENTS.md`, “NucBox”.

- **Apple frame-health qualification (LLP 1079.000, 2026-10-28).** An iPhone 15 Pro on iOS 27.0.1 is connected; the minimal development app is installed and launched, with its running process verified. The minimal frame-health fixture and signed development/core/off builds are prepared locally. Use the phone when available and identify an above-120 Hz display. Run the independent presentation and observer off/on experiments, hidden/stationary/dragging overlay pairs, and energy/thermal checks. Callback/apply proxies and the debug scroll probe currently provide no presentation or battery certification. The card's own work in the app's frames has an opt-in benchmark (`FrameHealthCardCostIOSTests`, `EXACT_FRAME_HEALTH_BENCHMARK=1`) measured only on the simulator; run it and the overlay pairs on a phone over a full-window GPU canvas, a 60 Hz iPhone included, where the card's own work was 13 to 17 ms an update before it drew in bands and tiles (expo/exact#296).
- **The iOS frame-health card's header rests under the status bar** (2026-11-13, found driving the minimized card; a decision). A new full card rests 8 points from the window's top edge, past the safe area (LLP 1079.000 §7.2, #303), so on an iPhone simulator its Close, Pause, Details and Share sit under the status bar and take no taps until the card is dragged down. The minimized card already rests below the status bar. Decide whether the full card rests below it too.

- **macOS live trackpad replay (LLP 1079.000, 2026-10-28).** Add a reliable completion acknowledgement for synthetic phased wheels before admitting them under platform timing. Immediate layout reads exposed zero or partial motion; posting the later phases first did not resolve it. The carrier currently refuses with `platform-trackpad-replay-unqualified`; ordinary wheels and frozen-clock functional replay remain available.

- **NucBox: qualify an AMD overlay diagnostic if the paint crash recurs**
  (LLP 1149.001 Stage 1w). The retained wgpu paint crash is an execution
  access violation in `amdihk64.dll_unloaded` 2.0.0.1788 at `0x648d`;
  a post-reboot module inventory finds the hook in many desktop processes.
  G7's single isolated rerun passes 56 tests with the hook loaded in the arm
  and pinned process, and captures no new fault. If it recurs, test disabling
  the overlay through [AMD Software's Preferences](https://www.amd.com/en/resources/support-articles/faqs/DH3-001.html), verify whether
  the hook leaves newly launched arms, then requalify paint. This is a
  proposed machine remedy, not a demonstrated fix. Keep the later
  sleep/wake hang separate: chunk A reached `shelf_sleep_wake` at 18:39:46,
  after the 18:32 paint crash, and the next boot's Kernel-Power 41 reports
  `SleepInProgress=5`, `BugcheckCode=0`. Retain both investigations.
- **G7c Windows displayed cadence needs a working PresentMon capture**
  (2026-11-01, LLP 1149.001). A visible PowerShell task resolves foreground
  acquisition with the unchanged driver. The post-readiness captures still
  lose ETW events and produce no CSV, on D3D11 and egui as well as Ink/wgpu.
  Establish zero-loss captures before reporting 120 Hz fling cadence,
  late intervals, p99, rest displays or bar/caret display rates. Retained
  CPU, memory, foreground and snapshot observations are in G7c's RESULTS.
  Bar also needs a quiet cohort: all three attempts fail one of 15 load
  observations, with maxima 10.9%, 10.9% and 11.0%; collection stops at three.
- **G7c observer-off cold control needs a quiet cohort** (2026-11-01,
  LLP 1149.001). All three ten-launch-per-arm cohorts pass the initial six
  idle observations but fail the complete-cohort gate: 7/34, 16/34 and
  1/34 observations reach at least 10% total CPU. Their excluded DWM
  medians remain above the prediction without PresentMon. A qualified
  control is still needed to distinguish observer cost from machine state;
  the session process files do not establish a cause.
- **G7b Windows startup PresentMon capture remains unqualified**
  (2026-11-01, LLP 1149.001). Three configurations produce no CSV and lose
  ETW events: start before launch and close at readiness, retain the process
  through capture, and PID-filter immediately after process creation.
  Repair stops at three configurations; exact archives and raw failures
  remain in `target/g7b/`. Establish a zero-loss trace with app-owned startup
  presents before claiming PresentMon corroboration. The quiet native
  DWM/DXGI endpoint comparison is recorded as such, not as a successful
  PresentMon startup capture.
- **Ink's Windows window must preserve a lone full-window game's module presentation**
  (2026-11-01, Windows default review; LLP 1149 D10/D12). Until that handoff
  exists, games must keep the CPU painter so the module owns presentation.
  Qualify the handoff against LLP 1015.001's frame p50 of 7.22 ms at
  1280×720 and 10.83 ms at 1920×1080 before changing the game path.
- **Windows modal deadline may flake** (2026-11-01, Windows default review).
  `modal::tests::native_modal_and_carrier_deadlines_cancel_rearm_and_release`
  failed in `about_to_wait_honors_retry_pending_redraw_and_earlier_timer`:
  it chose the two-second deadline instead of the half-second timer. The
  rerun passed with a different profile override; cause not established.
  Reproduce before changing the clock or assertions. Raw failure:
  `target/win-default/nucbox/checks-initial/windows.log`. At `cc9e9d0377`,
  it failed again, then passed with the same explicit package-profile

- **NucBox sleep/wake is excluded from unattended runs** (2026-11-01,
  LLP 1149.001 G7 and Windows default qualification). The October 30 and
  November 1 journeys did not wake unattended. November 1 retained enabled
  wake timers and a confirmed scheduled wake, then lost SSH; neither
  device's result was collected (`host/ink/MEASUREMENTS.md`, “Windows
  default”). Any future sleep/wake qualification needs a person at the
  machine. Keep this separate from the AMD-hook crash.
- **Excord's optional RichEdit composer does not accept typing** (2026-11-01,
  Windows default qualification). With `EXACT_INK_RICHEDIT=1`, clicking the
  composer and sending Ctrl+A and text at 100% leaves its value unchanged;
  scaling to 150% preserves that value. The log reports `RichEdit UIA: device: Catastrophic
  failure (0x8000FFFF)`. The same drive edits the ordinary Ink and CPU
  composers. Reproduce with Excord's local transcript fixture; captures and
  logs are retained in `target/win-default/nucbox/richedit-*`. Investigate
  alongside chunk B's pointer and focus work below; this observation does
  not establish the cause. RichEdit remains opt-in.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: native-field pointer delivery** (Claude review 3,
  `target/review-claude-s1w.md`, `98cff0bdfa`). RichEdit's handled mouse messages
  make winit skip dispatch, so field and ancestor clicks never reach the page.
  Keep the native edit and let the pointer sequence reach the presenter.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: app keys from native fields** (Claude review 10).
  Forward a known editing set to RichEdit; let Escape, function keys and
  app chords outside that set reach winit regardless of RichEdit's answer.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: composition ownership** (Claude review 4). `SES_USECTF`
  enables TSF while the host tracks IMM messages. Drop it and qualify IMM,
  or track composition from the editor before claiming app updates cannot
  overwrite a composition.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: UIA focus and accessible name** (Sol review 7,
  `target/review-sol-s1w.md`, `98cff0bdfa`). `TxSetFocus` focuses only the
  HWND; bridge it to logical field focus. Pass the computed accessible name
  to `EM_SETUIANAME`, separately from the placeholder.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: input event causes** (Sol review 4; Claude review 15).
  Carry `deleteByCut`, `insertFromPaste`, `historyUndo` and
  `deleteContentBackward`, including composition commits, from the message
  that caused the edit. Every native edit currently becomes `insertText`.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: selection direction** (Sol review 5). `EM_EXGETSEL`
  normalizes endpoints; use TOM to retain the active end through programmatic
  backward selection and Shift+Arrow continuation.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: field message and paint churn** (Claude review 15).
  Avoid per-frame `EM_SETUIANAME` and setting `changed` on every routed
  message, including key-up and mouse movement with no edit.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: product qualification** (Sol review 7 and checked
  IME path; Claude reviews 3, 4, 10, 11). Complete Shelf's native UIA tree,
  Japanese IME placement/reconversion and Narrator journeys, then Excord's
  composer, focus traversal and growing/wrapping text. The flag stays off
  until those drives pass; Unicode insertion is not an IME drive.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: startup observer and corrected A/B** (Claude reviews
  1, 7, 8; Sol review 8). The old log and observer bracket different writes:
  2/100 logged entry-arm intervals exceed 50 ms (138.547 and 77.718 ms),
  versus 0/100 after layout, with two-sided Fisher p = 0.4975. Neither tail
  has a stack; one 2.646 ms interval has a late capture. These are not yet
  established as runner-to-layout work. A queued completion suppresses capture.
  Pre-spawn a helper blocked on an event, or capture threads and contexts
  in-process without a VA clone and symbolize offline; the measured VA clone
  pauses threads for about 6.026 ms and process spawning adds latency. Probe
  `LdrLockLoaderLock` with `TRY_ONLY` on the watchdog thread at timeout to
  test loader-lock ownership without snapshotting. Rerun the A/B on corrected
  marks only after that redesign, retaining first-pixel differences between
  arms and marking captured launches as perturbed. No startup fix is earned.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: M9 axis mapping** (Claude review 13; Sol's checked
  DirectWrite path). The candidate maps no axes. Use
  `IDWriteFontResource::CreateFontFace` with design-space values, obtained by
  un-normalizing Parley's coordinates (including `avar` and `fvar`) or carrying
  the requested values. Repeat the audit before admitting variable faces.

- Game audio: after a large upward pitch change on a one-shot, the web output can be stopped about 168.5 ms of source before its end (1→16, 10.53 ms of playback), and a downward change leaves silence until the simulation ends the voice.

- Changing an in-flight upload's finite `with` context makes a worker source's reply miss its original argument key (`data/src/placed.rs`, `parse_with`); the main-thread source takes the new context.

- The web JS auth hold outlives a superseded mutation when its replacement does not call `openAuthSession` (`host/web-js/auth.js`). Replacing a sign-in mutation with a synchronous cancellation leaves its auth ticket in `state.pending`; a late callback can resume the old source and write its session. A browser regression test reproduced both effects. The supersession cleanup runs only when another auth session opens. Match the runner's let-go behavior.
- Update the JS web target's socket-protocol test fixtures: `held-count.test.mjs` omits `socketProtocols` from its admission stub, and `build.mjs`'s empty-set grant exports omit it. Both suites fail on origin/main's sources before their assertions (two tests each).
- JavaScript runtime Clippy: remove the needless borrow passed to `with_canvas_surfaces` in `js/src/bootstrap_tests.rs`; `cargo clippy -p exact-js --all-targets -- -D warnings` rejects it.
- The Caltrain JavaScript identity fixture omits `exactTime` from its expected sources (`js/tests/it/caltrain.rs:101`); the full runtime suite and the isolated test fail with that extra built-in source. Update the expected built-ins.
- The shared executor read-deadline test can abort the Linux unit-test process: `executor_read_tests.rs:105` finds no held socket, then cleanup takes the poisoned lock. It failed in a full run and passed alone on this Mac at a load average above 120; make the fixture coordinate the held socket without poisoning cleanup.
- `SheetSurfaceMacTests.testAuthoredGroundMatchesAnAppKitSheetInDarkMode` should report an unavailable window-server reference capture before reading its pixel colour. On a locked or asleep display it fails at `SheetSurfaceMacTests.swift:20` with no NSColor.
